A list that gains a line is edited in place (`Fluid.patchList`), a streamed reply is appended to (`Fluid.paintStream`), repaints caused by events are batched per animation frame (`Fluid.frame`), and data that did not change is not drawn again (`Fluid.signature`). J2's idle drift and the spokes' march are switched off from `sutra-panel.css`; `j2.css` and the J2 page are untouched.
- Why: the choppiness was in what the page did around its motion (the whole transcript rebuilt on every message, a streamed reply rendered again per chunk, a graph redrawn for data that had not changed, paint-heavy animations), not in the animations themselves; a rule a test enforces keeps the next stylesheet from bringing it back.
- Rejected: only shortening the durations (the cost was in the repaints); an animation library (nothing here needs one); editing `j2.css` (J2 stays byte for byte, D12); a frame-budget check in CI (machines differ: the probe is kept for a person to run).
- Diagram: key label "motion: transform and opacity, edit in place", pill on the Experience band.

## D123 — A team's chat is its own: the org chat only points at it, and Home switches between them
- Sources: owner: "Whenever it is actually initiating new departments, the chat sessions need to be separate. I think right now it is in a single chat session."; D63 (a node and its session are made together); `docs/notes/GAP3-dept-chats.md`; ORCH-D1 (two kinds of chat, one voice each).
- Decided: a department or division the org chat creates gets its own chat session, made with its node in one transaction (D63), titled by its name. The owner's request is copied once as the first user line of that chat and marked as passed on from the org chat (`ChatMessageOut.passed_on`, read from the copy's `src_ref`); the org chat gets one `team_chat` card per new team ("Started <name>: open its chat"). A team's replies, its questions and their answers stay in its own chat; when its Identity asks, the org chat gets a `team_chat` pointer (about `question`), never the question's words. This replaces the `needs_you` mirror card, which is still read and drawn when stored (no migration of old rows). A provider session is per chat session id: a team's first turn starts a new one primed only from that chat's own transcript, and no engine or team line is ever written to `cs_org`. Home's chat column shows one chat at a time with a strip of tabs; a team's chat is the node panel's Chat tab shown inline.
- Why: the sessions were already separate in the database; what the owner met was one conversation, because the org chat carried a team's question and let the owner answer it there. One place per conversation also keeps the context each provider session is primed with its own.
- Rejected: keeping the mirror card beside the pointer (two places to answer one question); merging the teams' lines into the org chat (the owner's complaint); one provider session for all chats (a team would be primed with another's talk); a migration that rewrites stored `needs_you` cards.
- Diagram: key label "a team's chat is its own", pill on the Chat sessions box.

## D124 — The desktop app updates itself: a verified download, installed on the owner's word
- Sources: owner: a request that the desktop app update itself; D34 (Sutra does not install the provider CLIs `claude` and `codex`: it does not forbid updating Sutra Pro itself); D100 (releases are `sutra-pro-v*`, never "Latest", never the older product's); D102 (the Windows installer is unsigned); D116, D118 (the window, the server and the quit path); `docs/notes/GAP3-autoupdate.md`.
- Decided: the desktop app (never the command line or a browser-only server) asks GitHub's release list for the `sutra-pro-v*` releases of this repository about a minute after launch and every 6 hours, unless the owner switched "Check for updates automatically" off in Settings, About, or the app runs offline, with the stub connector, or in the self-test. It downloads the asset of its platform into `<data>/updates`, verifies the SHA-256 and then the signature (the same Team Identifier, bundle id, a newer version and Gatekeeper's verdict on macOS; the same Authenticode publisher on Windows) and installs only when the owner chooses Restart Now or Install on Quit, after the app's own quit path has stopped its server. A copy that cannot verify a signature (ad hoc, unsigned, a checkout) only tells. Anything that fails to verify is deleted; a failed swap puts the old app back; the data folder is never touched.
- Why: an app that replaces itself is the most trusted thing it can do, so it does so only for what it can prove came from the same publisher, and only when the owner says; the data folder and the server's one-writer rule (D94) are untouched by it.
- Rejected: installing without asking; installing what cannot be verified (so the unsigned Windows installer, D102, only tells until a certificate exists); using the release list's "Latest" (D100); updating the command line or a browser-only server (their owners update them with their own tools); a download in the page (it has no IPC: the dialog is Electron's). Not proved here and said so in the note: a real download, a real signed macOS install and a Windows install.
- Diagram: key label "update: verified, then on your word", pill on the Desktop app box.

## D125 — The owner chooses what the working folder allows, and the project's own instructions go with it; nothing is routed
- Sources: owner feedback on beta 3: a team told to look at the app and update the J2 prototype answered with the website intake of the document engine and never looked at the folder, while Claude's own desktop app reads the project, follows its instructions and does the work; D121 (one working folder), D123 (a team's chat is its own).
- Decided: (1) The owner chooses what the working folder allows: read files only, or read and change files (the default for a folder chosen from now on; a folder chosen before this stays read only). With "change" the claude tools are Read, Glob, Grep, LS, Edit, MultiEdit, Write and Bash restricted to `git status`, `git diff`, `git log` and `git show` (writes into `.git` and the git options that write a file or read outside the repository are denied); codex gets the `workspace-write` sandbox in the folder; nothing else runs, no network, no commit (the owner commits). Edits outside the folder are refused by the CLI. (2) The project's own instructions (`CLAUDE.md`, else `.claude/CLAUDE.md`, else `AGENTS.md`, at the top of the folder, up to 12,000 characters, never through a link) go with the folder: they are put in the system text of the chats that have the folder and followed, and the CLI's own settings are still not loaded, so a project's hooks never run. (3) Settings has a Choose folder button in the desktop app (the system folder dialog) and the access choice. (4) A team's chat shows which folder is set, or a link to choose one (the folder chip).
- No routing exists. Where a message goes is not decided by its words: a team's chat, a division's chat, a task from the org chat and a recurring task start the department run of the one engine, exactly as before D125. The engine inherits the folder as a capability, for every agent and every output, in a later decision (D126 and on, docs/design/agent-sessions-and-engine-capabilities.md). A first version of D125 sorted the owner's words into the engine or a team turn in the folder; the owner rejected it and it was removed.
- Why: the folder was only ever given to the direct chat and the org agent, and the owner had no way to let it change files, no way to give it the project's instructions and no clear place to choose it; these are the things a session in a folder has that the app's chat lacked.
- Rejected: routing by the owner's words (the owner's decision: the one engine gains capabilities, nothing is put in front of it); a per-team mode setting; loading the CLI's own settings and hooks from the folder (a folder's hooks would run arbitrary commands); letting the team run any command (the owner has a terminal; D34's reasoning about what the app installs and runs); making edits default on for a folder chosen before (that is the owner's decision to make, once).
- Diagram: key label "your folder, your access", pill on the Chat sessions box.

## D126 — One engine, extended by capabilities that every agent and every output inherit; nothing is routed in front of it
- Sources: owner requirement R1 after the first fix; D125 part (1) (superseded), D121 ("the engine's runs are unchanged", superseded), D13 (the vendored engine is changed only by numbered patches), D14 (one process per run, files only).
- Decided: Words to a team always start the department run of the one engine. New abilities are added to the engine through a capability registry (`engine/capabilities/`): a capability can add outputs, call-tag fields, prompt addenda, progress sinks and one setting; it cannot replace another's; every agent call of every output runs every hook. The API-level route to "the team's own turn" (`work_route.py` and its parts) is removed.
- Why: a front door that guesses from words splits the product in two (a document engine and a second path), and every later ability would need a third; one engine with inherited abilities is what the owner asked for and keeps the five-function flow, audit and receipts for project work.
- Rejected: keeping the route and tuning its words (two flows, wrong whenever the words mislead); asking the owner which mode each time; a plugin system with a manifest and loading from disk (a fixed tuple in code is enough, and nothing is loaded from the owner's folder).
- Diagram: key label "one engine, capabilities add", pill on the Department runtime box.

## D127 — The workspace capability: every engine agent call may read the owner's folder, only project builders may change it, the owner's access setting caps both
- Sources: R1, D121, D125 parts (2) and (3), `router.AGENT_FUNCTIONS`.
- Decided: `CallTag.workspace` ("", read, edit) is set by the capability (five functions and all builders: read; builders of an output whose spec says edit: edit; helper calls: none); `Router.folder_for(tag)` turns it into the folder, capped by the `access` the owner chose, and adds the project's CLAUDE.md and the folder text after the egress check; `AGENT_FUNCTIONS` is deleted (the org agent tags its own calls). `EngineSettings.workspace: auto|off`.
- Why: the folder and its rules belong to the connector layer, the agent and output belong to the engine; a tag carries one to the other without either learning the other's names, and J2's prompts stay byte for byte.
- Rejected: a list of function names in the router (what failed); giving the folder from the host to the whole process (no per-call least privilege); appending the folder text through `system_addendum` (it would change every prompt golden and be logged as J2's prompt).
- Diagram: key label "agents read your folder", pill on the Connector router box.

## D128 — "Project work" is an engine output built on the document flow; the one vendored change is patch P14
- Sources: R1, engine.md 3.4, `OUTPUTS` in `j2.py`.
- Decided: `OUTPUTS["project"]` is registered by the capability: sections Findings, Changes, Verification, Open items, Outcome; its own intake, builder, plan, atom rules, rubric, conditions (an absolute path outside the vendored folder), mocks and addenda; builders work with their tools and report, Audit verifies in the folder. `single: True` plus patch P14 (`_plan_stages` returns early) stop named documents from swapping the output. Rates reuse `rates_prd`.
- Why: the document flow already has intake, planning, building, auditing, three attempts, receipts and nodes; a work report is a document whose sections are done with tools, so almost everything is inherited; one recorded line replaces a runtime patch nobody could verify.
- Rejected: a second flow beside the document flow; a runtime monkeypatch of `_plan_stages`; editing `conditions/`, `rates.json` or the role files (they are byte-checked); a test-running Bash allow-list (D125 keeps tests for the owner).
- Diagram: key label "Project work output", pill on the J2 chips box.

## D129 — The engine picks the output for a brief itself; `auto` is the default and the owner overrides it anywhere
- Sources: R1, PROJECT-WORK root causes 1 and 4, the settings cascade of engine.md 4.4.
- Decided: `EngineSettings.output` defaults to `auto`; the host decides once, before J2 starts, by a pure rule table (owner's choice, strong project words, a follow-up's own output, a make-a-deliverable phrase, a set folder, else website); the result is written to `settings.json` and `output.json` and, for project work only, said in the run's chat. The start page select gets "Let the team decide" and "Project work".
- Why: the decision must be inside the engine and before the first prompt (the intake prompt depends on the output); a rule table costs no model call, is testable, and leaves every existing run unchanged.
- Rejected: Identity choosing in its intake reply (the reply format and prompts are golden); a model call to classify (cost, latency, nondeterminism); no default (the failure the owner hit).
- Diagram: key label "engine picks the output", pill on the Department runtime box.

## D130 — Every agent works in a chat session of its own, new for each run
- Sources: R2, I1 (orchestrator.md), D63, D123.
- Decided: builders already get a step session with their node; function agents' sessions roll at the agent's first event of a run (archive the used one, start the replacement in the same transaction, carry the choice); sessions are stamped with the run, titled by the agent and the task or workflow, and listed per team and run (`GET /api/teams/{dept}/agents`). Past runs' agent chats stay in the archive, read only.
- Why: the owner wants each working agent to have its own thread like a Claude desktop subagent; the existing one-session-per-node invariant and archive-and-replace machinery do it without a new table.
- Rejected: one endless session per function (a thread that mixes every run); a new graph node per function per run (breaks the team's fixed five); a separate sessions table.
- Diagram: key label "a chat per agent per run", pill on the Chat sessions box.

## D131 — Each agent session has its own provider, model and effort, honoured by the engine through a file the server writes
- Sources: R2, ABS-6/8 (direct chat pill), Claude desktop's per-session picker, ORCH-D5 (SQLite is the server's).
- Decided: schema v4 stores `pinned_model` and `pinned_effort` beside `pinned`; the cascade is own > run > team > policy default, a whole pick at a time; the server writes `<run>/choices.json` and the engine's router reads it per call (`connectors/agent_picks.py`), applying it from the agent's next call and falling back with a visible note; the pill is the existing one plus "Default".
- Why: the engine is a separate process that must not open the database; a small file rebuilt from the database survives restarts and is the same pattern as the inbox.
- Rejected: the engine reading SQLite read-only (two writers' view of one schema, locking, a path the child environment lacks); keeping model and effort only in `session.json` (a temp folder that can vanish and that the engine cannot see); a per-field cascade (hard to explain in one chip).
- Diagram: key label "model and effort per agent", pill on the Connector router box.

## D132 — The owner steers an agent with a note in its chat, delivered through the engine's node-context file
- Sources: R2, P9 (`system_addendum`), the `ChatSession` rule that function and step chats are not writable.
- Decided: `POST /api/chats/{sid}/steer` stores the owner's line and writes the agent's entry of `<run>/node_context.json`; the engine appends it to that agent's system prompt from its next call; the generic message route stays read only for these chats.
- Why: it uses a seam that exists, needs no new inbox kind and no change to the engine protocol, and says honestly when it applies.
- Rejected: letting the owner post as a normal message into an agent chat (breaks the record rule and implies the agent answers at once); a new inbox kind that interrupts a running call (cannot be honoured mid-call); sending the note to Identity only.
- Diagram: key label "note to an agent", pill on the Chat sessions box.

## D133 — An agent's live work reaches its chat through a published file and `chat.partial`, not a ninth run event
- Sources: R2, engine.md 4.5 (eight event types), D111 (one streaming mechanism).
- Decided: the engine rewrites `<run>/agent_live.json` (at most four times a second) and the watcher turns it into the existing ephemeral `chat.partial` events for the agent's session; the stored line still comes from the `say` event.
- Why: partials are many and not durable; the protocol and its contract tests stay as they are; the page already knows `chat.partial`.
- Rejected: a `partial` event in `events.jsonl` (log growth, contract change); polling the run folder from the page (a second reader of engine files).
- Diagram: key label "live agent chats", pill on the Chat sessions box.

## D134 — Claude desktop is the reference for the picker: per session, applied next turn, "Default" follows the team, subagent equals thread
- Sources: R2, `model_catalog.py` (model groups and efforts per model), D111.
- Decided: the mapping table of 3.3 is the contract; the labels and efforts (low, medium, high, xhigh, max) are the CLI's own; "Default" shows what it resolves to and from where.
- Why: the owner linked the behaviour to Claude desktop; one table makes it testable.
- Rejected: a separate "advanced" panel; a global model switch for all agents (that is the team pill).
- Diagram: key label "same picker as Claude", pill on the Chat sessions box.

## D135 — Project work tells every agent what the team may do, puts its report in the team's chat, and gives a follow-up the earlier report (amends D127 and D128)
- Sources: an owner's real run of project work (installed app, access "read and change", the brief asked the team to check the app and update the J2 prototype in the app and its repo): (1) the charter held the constraint "Folder is read-only: output is a markdown document, no code or files in the folder are changed" and the rule "Do not change any file in the working folder", the builders obeyed it and only wrote a report, and the closing message said the folder was read only; (2) the finished report (`project.md`) was shown nowhere the owner looks, so the owner asked the team "Where are the changes and how can I read it?"; (3) that follow-up made Identity answer that it could not find the files of the earlier request and did not know which project was meant. D127 (the workspace table: the five functions read, builders edit), D128 (the project output), D121 and D125 (the folder text), D130 to D134 (a chat per agent: kept as it is), R1 (one engine, capabilities add).
- Decided: **Honest capability text.** `CallTag.team_workspace` (none, read, edit) is set by the workspace capability on every agent call of an output whose spec says its builders edit (project only); `Router.folder_for` caps it by the owner's access into `Folder.team` (`edit`: the builders may change files and the owner allowed it; `read`: they would but the owner chose read only), and `grounding_text` says what the TEAM may do. With `edit`, an agent whose own tools only read (Identity, Adaptation, Priority, Coordination, Audit) is told that its tools do not change files because the builders do, that this is not a limit of the run, what the builders can do (create and change files, git status, diff, log, show; nobody commits) and to plan and judge for it; with `read`, every agent is told the owner chose read only in Settings, under Working folder, that nobody can change a file, and what the report then holds (the exact edits, file by file, and how to allow changes). A call with no team gets the words of D121 and D125 byte for byte. Identity's project intake (the output's own text after J2's prompt) says: a brief that asks to update, implement, fix, change or add something makes the change part of the goal, with the files named in the Changes item; a brief that only asks to look, list, check or report stays a report; "Folder is read-only" is written only when the owner chose read only (then the goal is the report with the exact edits and how to allow changes); Identity's own read-only tools are never a limit of the run. The builder text never describes a change instead of making it (and never fences its whole reply: a nested fence ended a section early and lost the exact edit); Audit checks every claimed change against the folder (`git diff`, `git status`), and because file tools cannot run git its call has a fourth workspace level, `inspect`: the file tools and Bash restricted to `git status`, `git diff`, `git log` and `git show` (the edit deny list: no `--output`, `--no-index`, `--ext-diff`, `--textconv`, `-c`), no tool that writes; the workspace table gives it to Audit of an output whose builders edit, and the owner's access caps it (with read only an inspect call has no command at all). **The report in the chat.** An output may say `deliver` (project does). At the end of such a run the engine host writes `<run>/deliverable.json` before `run.ended`, whatever the end was: the file the run delivered and, when it could read git at both ends, the files that changed in the owner's working folder (`git status --porcelain` before and after, a content fingerprint to tell a file changed again from one untouched; computed by the host, never by an agent; paths relative to the folder; at most 100 named). Nothing is said when the folder is no git work tree, git is missing, slow or refusing, access was read at both ends or the status is huge; "nothing changed" is said when git looked. The folder is only read (no lock, no index refresh, no fsmonitor, no `GIT_*` from the process) and nothing is written into it. When the Projector applies `run.ended` it posts ONE `deliverable` card to the team's chat (the report as the team wrote it, markdown, cut at 30,000 characters with a note; the files; "Open the full report") and puts `report {dept_id, run_id, label}` on the done card in the org chat, which draws "Open the report"; a run that stopped or crashed before the report existed still lists the files it had changed, a run with neither posts nothing. The page draws the report with the existing markdown renderer and folds it when it is longer than 1,400 characters (`ChatFold.longText`: "Read the report (Findings, Changes, ...)"). **Where the link points:** the team page's Preview tab for that run, `/d/<team>/?view=detail&tab=preview&run=<run id>` (J2's page draws a document run's markdown there as a page, and `?run=` is how it opens a finished run). **Follow-up continuity.** For a project run that continues an earlier run (`seed_from`), the capability's addendum gives Identity, every builder and Audit the earlier `project.md` (the first 12,000 characters cut at a line end, with a note where the whole is; the earlier run's own folder first, else the host's `upstream/<run>/` copy; between delimiters it cannot close, introduced as data and never as instructions) and the files the host recorded for that run. The intake says a question about the earlier result ("where are the changes", "what did you find") is answered from the report in Identity's one reply, naming its files, with no change in the folder, and that Identity does not ask which project the owner means when the report names it; builders restate the earlier report for such a question so a later follow-up can still rely on it.
- Why: the folder text said "they are read-only" to every agent that does not write, so a planner took its own tools' limit for the run's and the team obeyed it; the owner should read the team's own words and the folder's own evidence where the owner talks to the team, and the files git saw change cannot be wrong the way a report can; a follow-up starts in a new run folder that an agent's file tools (working in the owner's folder) cannot reach, so the earlier report has to be handed to it through the seam the engine already has for text an agent reads. Every part is inside the one engine (a tag field, the capability's text, the host's run end, the projector's run end): nothing is routed.
- Rejected: telling Identity only in its intake and leaving the folder text as it was (the sentence that leaked stays in the same prompt); making every agent's own tools editable, or giving the git reads to all five functions (Audit must stay an independent reader and only Audit needs them); asking a builder or Audit for the list of changed files (an agent's list is a claim, the point is evidence); posting a plain chat line from the engine (no card, no link, no markdown, and the event protocol stays eight types); a link to `/site/latest` alone or to the file download (a bare page or raw markdown, no team around it) or to the Documents tab (PRD, HLD and LLD only); a copy of the report or of the list in the owner's folder (the folder is the owner's); running git in a folder with no `.git` above it (a Mac without the developer tools opens an installer dialog); the whole of the earlier report whatever its size (prompt cost); routing a question about the earlier result around the engine (one engine: it is a short run of the same flow).
- Diagram: key label "your report in the team's chat", pill on the Chat sessions box.

## D136 — Sync 4 vendors from the J2 copy `prototypes/j2` at a pinned commit and tree
- Sources: owner brief of 2026-11-10 (names the folder as the source) and its later move of the pin to the Studio tip (D153), `docs/notes/J2S4-a.md`, `docs/notes/J2-DIFF-OF-RECORD.md`, D114, D13.
- Decided: the source is the folder `prototypes/j2` of this repository at commit `025e265c594564f47a2365874cd5a5cc5d0d20a6` (tree `21c67045fe3230c1ab9be381c2f21465ea91849c`), extracted with `git archive <commit> prototypes/j2 | tar -x --strip-components=2 -C <empty folder>` and given to both scripts with `--commit`. `scripts/vendor_j2.py`: `BASELINE` is that commit and `BASELINE_TREE` its tree id; the static header of `VENDORED.md` is written from them (the sutra commit, the tree id and the extraction command; the commit ids of the earlier syncs stay there as history only). `CODE` gains `j2_agent_models.py`, `j2_agent_views.py` (D143) and `j2_studio.py` (D153): 17 code modules, 208 files (the manifest has 207 rows: the files minus itself). P1 now expects 3 `import j2_map` in `j2_artifacts.py` (it was 2), rewrites the flat imports and the `sys.path` insert of `j2_agent_views.py` and takes the new `j2_studio` import of `j2.py`; P13 is retired into Sutra's `engine/runlist.py` (D140); P15 is new and is a data patch: the seed's `library/index.json` holds six absolute home-folder paths of a J2 developer's machine (`runs[].library`) and each becomes `"not recorded"` (R5 forbids an absolute home path in any file of this product; no code and no page reads the field; the two `/tmp/` values stay; the script holds the pattern, never a path, stops unless it finds exactly six, and the patch table names the six lines); P16 is new (D152): Decide for me's two writes of `settings.json` merge into what the file holds. The patches are P1 to P5, P7 to P12, P14, P15 and P16 (no P6, no P13): 55 text edits and the one data patch. `REF_DOCS` gains `agent-views-j2-proposal.md`. `scripts/port_j2_ui.py` gets the same `--commit` flag, because a `git archive` folder is not a repository. Not carried (67 files, each with its reason in the exclusion table of `scripts/j2_diff_of_record.py` and in `docs/notes/J2-DIFF-OF-RECORD.md`; D91 is amended for it): the HTML mocks, the explainer pages, the flow picture, `build-on-run-design.md` (a design with no code), the older studio and the budget demo.
- Why: it is the only source of J2 (D154); a commit plus a tree id is reproducible while `main` moves; numbered patches on exact anchors make the next sync a re-run and a reviewable diff.
- Rejected: the working tree of `prototypes/j2` (not reproducible: an edited file or a `.pyc` would be vendored); the folder's HEAD without a pin (it moves under the build); a second source beside the copy (two truths).
- Diagram: label "J2 sync 4: prototypes/j2 @025e265", pill on J2 chips.

## D137 — The department page is J2's new page, re-ported by anchor; Sutra's strip, tabs and panels are added around it
- Sources: R5, D76, D114, `docs/design/ui.md` 5, `docs/notes/J2S4-e1.md`.
- Decided: `scripts/port_j2_ui.py` ports J2's `j2_ui.html` at the pin (the Studio redesign of the Simple view, the icon rail with flyouts, the chat panel, the Adaptation Map, the Artifacts groups and tabs, native Run settings, the shared template library page) with 33 page edits, each an `Edit(id, old, new, count)` checked on J2's own text first (a missed count stops the port and names every edit): E1 (three style links, the boot marker, `sutra_ctx.js`); E2a, E2b and E2c (the sandbox, with no `allow-same-origin`, on `frame`, `sframe` and the Studio's second frame `sframe2`); E3 and E4 (d3 under `/ui/vendor/`, `runQ()` carries the team); E5 (a strip above the Simple view, `div.steamstrip`: not `.shome`, which J2's New project button uses); E6a and E6b (graph and org chart use the whole work area); E7 (a strip at the top of the icon rail: back to All teams, the team chip, the two tab buttons); E8 to E12 and E9b (two panes, the node panel, Esc closes only the node panel when it is open, pins per team, `NodePanel.wants`, the modules `function_chat` and `output_pick` before `dept_ext`); E13a to E13d (wording); E19 (the place of the output choice, D139); E20a to E20i (the template library, the Start page's library link, Recent work and New project take their addresses from `SutraCtx`: D141); E21 (`loadFeed` fills J2's question list from the function's session: D148); E22 (J2's own `TABSUB` line still says Run settings has "what to build"); E23 (the Studio's Google Fonts injection into a readable frame becomes a comment: no CDN and no web font, UI-D3). Retired: the old E14 to E18 and `run-settings.css` (J2's own Run settings is the design they copied) and E19a to E19c (D139). The page is regenerated, never hand edited; `scripts/port_j2_ui.py --check` must pass.
- Why: J2's page is what the J2 side now calls J2 and R3 asks for all of it; keeping the generated-file rule keeps the next sync cheap; every Sutra part that needs a seam in the page gets a numbered edit instead of a runtime hook that `--check` cannot see.
- Rejected: keeping the old page (R3); hand edits (R5); runtime patching from `dept_ext.js` of the Map flyout, the rail and the chat panel (the edits would be invisible to `--check`); taking only Run settings and the catalog (the Artifacts tabs and the function chat need the rest).
- Diagram: label "J2 page re-ported (33 edits, D137)", pill on User chat interface · J2 Simple view.

## D138 — Home and Console keep a frozen copy of the previous J2 stylesheet; only the team page takes the new one
- Sources: D74 (one stylesheet for both pages: superseded in that one respect), `docs/notes/J2S4-e1.md`, and the check made for this design: Home is built on the old J2 Simple skeleton (`#sview`, `.shead`, `.smain`, `.schat`, `.stoggle`, `#dview`, `#rail`, `.barbadge`, `.railoff`, `.has-site`), five classes Sutra code uses (`barbadge`, `has-site`, `railoff`, `smain`, `stoggle`) exist only in the old stylesheet, 24 rules of the old sheet that mention `.rail`, `.tabs` or `#rail` are not in J2's new `<style>` verbatim (among them the generic 240 px `.rail.tabs` rule), and the new `#rail.rail.tabs` rule (84 px) would apply to Home's `<nav id="rail">`.
- Decided: `sutra_app/ui/static/css/j2-shell.css` is a byte copy of the committed `j2.css` of the previous sync (J2's style block as it was ported before sync 4), under one comment line of its own that says Sutra owns it from now on and names this decision (it names no commit); `home.html` and `console.html` link it instead of `j2.css`; the team page links the regenerated `j2.css`; the port never writes it. `test_port_edits.py` pins the copy's sha256 (and the old sheet inside it) so it cannot drift by accident, and `tests/ui/test_shared_css.py` fails when a class a page uses is styled only by a sheet that page does not load (at the Studio pin it also found `sstatus`). Home and Console keep their look; moving them to J2's new look is a design pass of its own (not done). The frozen sheet is also why `tests/ui/js/motion_css.test.js` records the new `j2.css` findings exactly instead of forbidding them (D155).
- Why: regenerating the one shared sheet would have restyled Home and the console with rules written for another layout, and nothing in the sync asked for that; a frozen, hashed copy costs one 90 KB file and makes both pages independent of J2's next redesign.
- Rejected: restoring the 24 lost rules by hand into `sutra-shell.css` (Home also depends on the changed `#sview` and `#dview` rules: a partial copy breaks at the next J2 change); re-skinning Home now (a design task with its own risk, not part of taking J2's changes); keeping D74 as it was (it cannot hold when J2 replaces its shell).
- Diagram: label "Home and console keep J2's previous stylesheet", pill on band:EXPERIENCE.

## D139 — The per-request output choice moves to the new start page as a Sutra module; Settings keeps the default
- Sources: D129 (`auto` is the default, the owner overrides it anywhere), the new page (the start select, `/api/output` and `OUTS` are gone from it), `docs/notes/J2S4-e2.md`.
- Decided: one page edit (E19) makes `<div class="soutpick" id="soutpick"></div>` the first child of the start page's row of options (`div.slrow`, beside Decide for me; J2's chips are examples that fill the box), and a new Sutra module `static/js/output_pick.js` fills it: "What should we make?" with Let the team decide (`auto`), Website, PRD document and Project work (a value outside them, an HLD for instance, is added so the choice in force is shown). It is hidden whenever the landing is hidden (the landing hides itself once a run exists), and nothing is built for an archived team (the stylesheet hides the placeholder). It shows `next_run.output` of `GET /api/engine/settings?dept=` (new, additive: what the team's next run would use, the start page's choice included: `ctx.effective_settings`, which already layers `engine.json`, the team's file and `page.json`), so a reload shows what was picked; a change is `POST /api/output {type}` (the J2 route, sent through `SutraCtx.api.post`, which adds the token and the team), which stores `page.json next_settings.J2_OUTPUT` as before; a 409 puts the old choice back, locks the select and says "The team has started; choose before the first message.". Settings, Engine keeps `CHOICES.output` (the default for the team). `engine/api/commands.py` does not change for it; `engine/api/settings.py` `settings_view` gains `next_run` when a team is named.
- Why: the choice belongs where the request is typed; a module is testable in node and needs one edit instead of three edits that patch J2's state code.
- Rejected: the three-edit E19 (it patched code that no longer exists); a control inside Run settings (it is not where the owner types the request, and the pane is for finished and saved runs); dropping the choice (D129 says the owner overrides anywhere).
- Diagram: label "start page: choose what to make", pill on band:EXPERIENCE.

## D140 — The new read routes are ordinary views; patch P13 is retired into Sutra-owned code
- Sources: R1, D25 (one implementation behind two adapters), D114 (P13), `docs/notes/J2S4-b.md`.
- Decided: `engine/views.py` gains `plan_map`, `activity`, `catalog`, `artifacts_groups` and `ask` (D147), and the Studio's `record`, `doc`, `view` and `settings` (D153; `view_page` is the app's own copy of J2's function, which lives in J2's server file, not vendored); `runlist` and `changes` stay, but their code moves out of J2's `j2.py` into the Sutra-owned `engine/runlist.py` (the same logic as `run_row`, `run_list` and the inline `/api/changes` of J2's server file, over `j2.saved_dir`, `j2._read`, `j2._jsonl`), so patch P13 and its test are removed and `PATCH_VIEWS` goes. The table of J2's routes is now 34 GET rows and 7 POST rows (`HANDLER_COUNT = 41`; `VIEWS` has 40 entries because `site` has two rows). `artifacts_groups` is a live view (`LIVE_VIEWS`) like `artifacts_tree`. The FastAPI adapter registers the GETs and the POSTs (`ask` and `settings` need the token); the stdlib adapter (`j2_server.py`) registers the same rows plus `/library` as J2's page, a records-only `ask`, and `settings` on `RUN.set_auto`; `sutra_ctx.js` `J2_EXACT` holds 33 names and `J2_PREFIXES` five (`PATCHED_EXACT` is removed: `runlist` and `changes` are J2's routes now). `record_api_shapes.py` records every shape from J2's own server, so the engine-recorded `patched` section is gone.
- Why: one patch fewer in the vendored file (P13 changed no J2 behaviour), and the routes are J2's now, so recording their shapes from J2's own server replaces the engine-recorded `patched` section of the shape golden; a view is two to four lines on the pattern of `artifacts_tree`.
- Rejected: keeping P13 (its reason, that the routes were not yet in the J2 source, is gone); a second module path for the new routes (D25: one implementation); serving them from the stdlib adapter only.
- Diagram: label "J2 routes: one implementation, P13 retired", pill on J2 chips.

## D141 — The shared template library is served under the team: `/d/{dept}/library`, over the one global library
- Sources: R1, D26 (the library is one global store behind a file lock), D72 (pages), D79, `docs/notes/J2S4-e1.md`, `docs/notes/J2S4-b.md`.
- Decided: `ui/routes.py` serves `GET /d/{dept}/library` (HEAD too) with the same page file, the same CSP and the same boot block as `/d/{dept}/` (an unknown or malformed team gets the same small 404); `/d/{dept}/library/` redirects (308) to it, keeping the query; there is no global `/library` page. `GET /api/catalog` is a team route (`?dept=`) answering `j2_template_lib.catalog_view()` from `<data>/library` without a lock (the index is replaced atomically, P12). The library views (`library`, `library_item`, `run_templates`, `catalog`) read the data folder's library: `RunCtx.library` (set by `build_ctx`) and `views._library`, which sets `J2_LIBRARY_DIR` for one call under a lock and puts it back (only the engine host sets that variable, so the server read a folder that did not exist before). `sutra_ctx.js` gains `pagePath` (`/d/<dept>/`), `libraryPath` (`/d/<dept>/library`) and `onLibrary()`, and the page edits E20a to E20i replace every root path J2 builds (`/library`, `/?run=`, the two `location.pathname` tests, the Start page's links) with them; in J2's stub DOM (no team) they give `/` and `/library`, so the render harness behaves as J2's. The Start page's own link is relative (`library?view=detail`), because markup cannot hold the team. J2's `--collect` is never run on a data folder by hand: D142 is the only writer.
- Why: the page needs the team shell (rail, token, CSP, node panel) and the data is global, so the path says the team and the library says nothing about it; the stub fallback keeps the harness equal to J2's.
- Rejected: a global `/library` page (no team shell, a second boot block); copying the library per team (D26); a lock on the catalog read (a read of an atomically replaced index needs none).
- Diagram: label "/d/{dept}/library over the one library", pill on Template library.

## D142 — A finished run is collected into the catalog by a Sutra-owned step under the library lock
- Sources: R1, D26, D14, `docs/notes/J2S4-d2.md` (`collect` writes `index.json` and version folders with no lock; P10 wraps only `store` and `mark_used`).
- Decided: `engine/host/finish.py` (new, Sutra's) runs the steps of the end of a run, in the engine process, in `Host._conclude` after `publisher.stop()` and before `heartbeat.stop()` (the heartbeat still beats, and the final publish and `run.ended` come after every file a step writes), only when the run ended `done` or `done_with_open_items` (a stopped or crashed run gets no step and no file). Each step is guarded: an exception, a `SystemExit` (`j2_agent_views.build` raises one for a folder that is not there) or a step that took more than 10 s is one `finish.step` line in `trace.jsonl` (`step`, `result` ok, skipped or failed, `seconds`, its facts, `why` or `error`) and never a failed run; a step over the limit also ends the rest (the limit is checked when a step returns: a step is never cut short from outside, which would leave a half-written file) and no step starts once the process was asked to stop. The `catalog` step calls `j2_template_lib.collect(<team runs dir>, runs=[run_id])` inside `j2_template_lib._library_lock()` (P10: waits 30 s, then skips with a trace line). It is skipped, with the reason in its trace line, when the engine setting `template_library` is off (the default, D18), when the run is a mock run, when the run folder holds no `templates.json` (nothing to collect, so the library is neither locked nor rewritten) and when the lock stays taken for its whole wait. No J2 file changes. `finish.py` imports no J2 module at import time (the host sets J2's environment before J2 is first imported). With `template_library` off (the default) nothing is collected and the catalog's "By run" lists J2's eight development runs only; the Settings text of the flag does not say so yet (a follow-up in the sync note).
- Why: the catalog is only worth having if the owner's own runs reach it; the lock makes the read-modify-write of the index safe against the runtime `store`; running in the engine process keeps D14 (files only, the server never writes the library).
- Rejected: the server collecting after `run.ended` (a second writer of the library); an ops command only (a catalog the owner has to remember to fill); collecting mock runs (a scripted run teaches the library nothing and would fill the owner's catalog with flagged junk); patching J2 to call `collect` itself (a recorded patch for something host code does).
- Amended (0.3.1): the Settings text of the `template_library` flag now says that, while it is off, the catalog's "By run" lists J2's eight development runs only (`settings_view.js`); the sentence of the Decided line that says it does not is out of date.
- Diagram: label "run end: collect into the catalog", pill on Template library.

## D143 — The agent-readable JSON is built at the end of a run by a Sutra-owned step; the two J2 modules are vendored with P1 only
- Sources: R3, `docs/agent-views-j2-proposal.md` of the copy (J2's own proposal: `J2_AGENT_VIEWS`, four hook points, no code), `docs/notes/J2S4-d2.md`.
- Decided: `j2_agent_models.py` and `j2_agent_views.py` join `CODE`. `j2_agent_views.py` needs one P1 edit (its `HERE` / `sys.path.insert` block and three flat imports become `from . import ...`); `j2_agent_models.py` has no sibling import. The `agent_views` step of `finish.py` (D142's runner, first in order) calls `j2_agent_views.build(<run dir>, quiet=True)` for a run that ended `done` or `done_with_open_items`, after the run's last file is written, so the Artifacts pane's Agent-readable tab shows the validated JSON (`agent/*.json`, `agent/audits/A-n.json`, `artifacts/<name>.json`, `site/sections/<id>.json`, the stamped `prd.json`, `hld.json`, `lld.json`, `design.json`, and `<output>.json` of a document-flow output: a project run gets `project.json`) for the runs the app makes. `python -m sutra_app.engine.j2_agent_views build <run dir>` is the manual form.
- Why: cheap and safe: one import edit under an existing patch, no new patch id; the module reads run files and writes new JSON files (and only adds top-level keys to artifact-schema JSONs J2 itself wrote), writes through no `J2Run.save` (so no records gate sees it) and runs under the same guard as every finish step.
- Rejected: leaving the tab with raw run files only (R3: the module is part of the copy and the tab is empty without it); a J2 patch that calls `build` from `j2.py` (the proposal's four hook points: four patches for what host code does); running J2's CLI as a subprocess (its flat imports need J2's folder on the path, the vendored files are a package).
- Diagram: label "run end: agent-readable JSON", pill on Department records.

## D144 — The seed keeps its mock-flagged templates; `sync_seed_library` delivers new versions and provenance to existing installs, never overwriting
- Sources: `docs/notes/J2S4-d1.md`, D26, `ops/doctor.py`, `tests/ops/test_doctor.py`.
- Decided: all 157 seed files are vendored, byte for byte except `library/index.json` (patch P15, D136) (32 templates, 39 versions, 26 of them `mock: true`, 8 `runs` rows); the catalog's "Hide mock" toggle is J2's answer to the mock flag. `sync_seed_library(data_dir, seed=None, *, report=None)` keeps its return value for templates (`artifact/section/slug`) and now also returns `artifact/section/slug@vN` for each version it adds; it fills `report` (`templates`, `versions`, `filled`, `skipped`, `runs`). The rule, under the library lock (the same `<library>/.lock` file and `oslayer` calls P10 uses, not an import of J2; it waits 30 s, then gives up with `LibraryBusy`), the index re-read inside the lock and written once, last: (1) a template the data library lacks is copied whole; (2) for a template it has, per seed version the library's state decides and the seed's number never changes: the same spec sha anywhere counts as present (its provenance is filled in, and a lost folder at the seed's own number is restored); a row at that number with another sha is left alone and reported as `skipped`, never renumbered; a folder at that number with no row is adopted when its own `meta.json` holds the sha, otherwise skipped; a free number gets the folder copied (a temp folder, then one rename) and its row appended; (3) provenance keys (`business`, `made_by.date`, `mock`, `collected_from`) are added to a present version's `meta.json` and index row only where absent (a `meta.json` that does not hold the row's sha, an owner's edit, is left alone and reported), and `used_by` and a template's `description` are never touched; (4) the seed's `runs` (an object keyed by run id, as J2's `collect` writes it) whose id is absent are appended, and a `runs` that is not an object is left alone and reported. Every write is a temp file and `oslayer.replace`. An index that is not an index is never replaced (`LibraryUnreadable`: the CLI exits 1 with "nothing was changed", the start logs it, the doctor warns). `sync_seed_once` runs at server start once per seed (a marker `<library>/.seed-sync` holds the sha256 of the seed's `index.json`), never raises (a warning and no marker: the next start tries again), and a start with no library gets the whole seed and the marker; by hand it is `sutra-pro config sync-library`; `doctor` reports missing seed versions with that fix (`missing_seed_versions` runs the same code path with `apply=False`, so the report and the fix cannot disagree).
- Why: shipping new seed versions only to new installs would contradict "an existing install keeps working and gains what is new"; the rule only adds, so nothing the owner's runs wrote is touched; the marker makes the start cheap (about 1 ms with the marker in place).
- Rejected: replacing the library from the seed (overwrites the owner's versions); renumbering a conflicting seed version (changes what an old run's `templates.json` points at); syncing only by command (the owner would never see the new versions); running the sync from the engine process (two writers per start); replacing an index that is not an index with one rebuilt from the seed (it drops the owner's entries).
- Diagram: label "seed library: add, never overwrite", pill on Template library.

## D145 — The late judge stays on by default with no patch; its records are accepted; its cost stays readable in the usage ledger
- Sources: D131 (picks), D59 (every call is charged to one atom), D47 (records are checked through one catalogue), `docs/notes/J2S4-a.md`.
- Decided: realization stays `True` by default, so the late judge runs as J2 wrote it; there is no `J2_LATE_JUDGE` flag and no patch. Its calls are tagged `judge` or `plan` with the turn's function, so `PICK_PURPOSES` gives them the function's own pick and `LIVE_PURPOSES` shows them as that agent's live work (D131, D133): no code. Records, so that strict mode accepts a run with the judge on: `MessageMeta` gains `judge` and `auto_label` (the second is J2's Decide for me label, D152); `RealizationDoc` gains `late` (a list of a closed `LateEntry`: `workflow`, `revises`, `t`, `cycle`, `calls`, `seconds`, `score`, `weakest`, `missing`, `rounds`, `ready`, `zone`, `stage`, `note`), and `docs/schemas/realization.v1.json`, `run_message.v1.json` and `workflow.v1.json` are re-exported (additive: the family versions stay 1); `settings.json` is catalogued as an open run object (Decide for me writes it, D152) and `RegisteredWorkflow.version` may be 0 (J2 gives the Plan workflow of a realization run version 0, which strict mode refused for every run with realization on). Cost: the usage ledger already has `purpose`, `fn`, `session_id` and `atom_id`, so the judge's calls are rows with purpose `judge` or `plan` and the function's name, and the owner reads them there. The views by purpose (`GET /api/usage/summary?by=purpose`, `usage.by_purpose` on the Agents rows, "judging N calls, $x" under an agent's usage) and `scripts/live_smoke.py --scenario late-judge` are not built in this release: they are Sutra-side extras cut to finish sooner, listed in the sync note.
- Why: the owner wants J2's change taken whole and the judge is part of it; a flag or a patch would be a J2 difference nobody asked for; the ledger is the one place usage is priced.
- Rejected: switching the judge off by default (a hidden difference from J2); a new vendored patch with an `EngineSettings` flag (a recorded J2 change for a cost question; if the live numbers say the cost is too high a later sync can add it as P17, since P16 is Decide for me's); measuring cost inside the engine (the ledger is the one place usage is priced).
- Amended (0.3.3): the views by purpose are built, as D159 (`GET /api/usage/summary?by=purpose`, `usage.by_purpose` on the Agents rows, "judging N calls, $x" and "questions N calls, $x" under an agent's usage, and the console's table); the sentence of the Decided line that says they are not built is out of date. `scripts/live_smoke.py --scenario late-judge` is still not built.
- Diagram: label "late judge on, no patch", pill on Usage and cost ledger.

## D146 — A report document of an engine output is shown in Artifacts' Documents group through a capability hook
- Sources: R1, D126, D128, `docs/notes/J2S4-b.md`, `docs/notes/J2S4-d2.md` (`project.md` is in no group: `TYPES_PATH` is a constant and `types.json` has no type for it).
- Decided: `Capability.documents(output, run_dir)` (new, default in `capabilities/base.py`): the document rows of an output this capability registered, built from its `OutputSpec` (`file`, `label`): one row of J2's own document shape (`id`, `kind: "document"`, `name`, `file`, `doc_file`, `maker`, `made_in`, `sections`, `nodes: []`, `versions`, `docs`, `ok`, `views`, where `views` is `j2_artifacts.views(d, row)`). `capabilities.documents(output, run_dir)` asks every capability and `views.artifacts_groups` merges the rows into the `documents` group and the top-level `documents` map of `artifacts-groups/1` for a run whose output has a `file` and is not one of J2's own. The default implementation is inherited, so `project.py` (which the 0.2.1 fix owns) is not edited, and every future output with a report file appears the same way. `served()` already serves the file because the tree lists every file of a run folder as a run record. A project run made through the host also has `project.json` (the agent-readable form of `project.md`, D143), so the row's `views.agent` is that file (`format: "json"`, `schema: "agent-views/1"`, model `GenericGuide`), and `format: "text"` only for a run no finish step ran on (an older run).
- Why: the owner sees the project report where the PRD, HLD and LLD are, with its three tabs, with no J2 patch and no edit of `types.json`; the hook is the registry's, so the next output needs no work.
- Rejected: a recorded patch on `TYPES_PATH` (a J2 change for a row Sutra can add after the fact); a second `types.json` merged at run time (J2 reads the constant in four places); editing `project.py` (owned by D135's fix).
- Diagram: label "report row from the capability", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D147 — A function's model-backed answer is a turn of its own agent session, through one port; `POST /api/ask` stays the one route
- Sources: R1, R2, D130 to D134, D9, D14, `docs/notes/J2S4-b.md`, `docs/notes/J2S4-c.md`.
- Decided: phase 1 and phase 2 are one route. `POST /api/ask {who, q, run}` (token required) is `views.ask`: 400 `{"error": "not a function"}` unless `who` is one of J2's six `FUNCS`, then `ctx.commands.ask(who, q, run)`. In the app `DeptCommands.ask` asks the orchestrator for `services.agent_chat(dept, run, who)` (a new optional method of `EngineServices`, with the `AgentChatTurn` protocol and `AskFailed` in `engine/api/ports.py`, implemented by `orchestrator/agent_ask.py`): a turn object for the function's session of that run, or `None` when the function has no session of its own for that run (it never worked in it, or `who` is the validator, which is code): then J2's own `j2_agent.answer(d, who, q, ask=None)` answers from the records, nothing is stored and no model is called. With a turn, `answer(d, who, q, ask=turn.ask)` is J2's function unchanged, and `turn.ask` is a model call made through the server's `Router` as a call of that session (D149); a refusal or failure of the model (an `AskFailed`, and only that; a reply with no text counts) falls back to the records, with the reason in the reply. The stdlib adapter always answers from the records (`ask=None`): the app never uses it, and no path calls `llm.ask` directly (a test scans the sources for it). A J2 answer is `{who, answer, from}`; the app adds `session_id`, `question_id`, `answer_id` and `note` (additive).
- Why: R2 says a function's answer is a turn of its session, and the engine process cannot do it (`llm.ask` raises `NoModelAvailable` in the server process: no port was configured), so the server's router, which owns the picks, the ledger and the scan, answers; J2's prompt and J2's record-only fallback stay J2's.
- Rejected: `ask=llm.ask` in the API process (cannot work, and would bypass the pick, the ledger and the scan); a new `/api/teams/.../ask` route beside J2's (two doors); calling the engine process (D14: files only, and a finished run has no process); a provider chat session per agent (the answer is self-contained, J2's `answer` carries the records each time).
- Diagram: label "ask a function: a turn of its own session", pill on Chat sessions.

## D148 — One transcript: the question and the answer are lines of the agent's session, and both panels read it
- Sources: R2, D130, D132 (the steer line and its `steer:` marker), `docs/notes/J2S4-c.md`, `docs/notes/J2S4-e2.md`.
- Decided: the owner's question is stored as a `user` line of the function's session with `src_ref` `ask:<id>` and the answer as an `agent` line (`who` = the function) with `src_ref` `ask:<id>:records` or `ask:<id>:model`; `ChatMessageOut` gains `ask` (`"q"`, `"records"` or `"model"`, left out of the wire when absent, as `steer` is), derived from the `src_ref` like `steer`, because `MessageMeta` is J2's closed key set. The lines are stored in the session of the run asked about, an archived one included (a past run's chat is its record), and the session stays `writable = 0`: only this route writes them. They are never given to the engine (`node_context.json` is not touched: a note steers, a question does not). The node panel's agent chat shows them (the question as the owner's line labelled "You asked", the answer with the label that says where it came from) and the box of a LIVE function chat has two modes, Note and Ask (a past run's chat is read, not asked; J2's chat panel asks about a past run with its `?run=`); the Ask mode sends `POST /api/ask?dept=` like J2's panel does. J2's panel keeps its feed (records) and gets its questions and answers from the session: a Sutra module `function_chat.js` loads the function's session (the agents row of the run, then its messages) and fills J2's `QA` list through one page edit in `loadFeed` (E21); a question in flight stays local until its answer arrives, and a question nothing answered says "No answer was stored.".
- Why: R2 asks for the same session in both places, not two surfaces; the session already has the lines, the events (`chat.message`) and the transcript view, and J2's panel needs only its list filled.
- Rejected: J2's page-memory `QA` as the only record (a second transcript that a reload loses); a new messages table; writing the lines through the generic message route (it refuses agent chats on purpose); also feeding the question to the agent as a note.
- Diagram: label "one transcript per agent", pill on Chat sessions.

## D149 — The answer call carries the agent's own pick, is scrubbed, limited and booked to the session, and never runs offline
- Sources: R2, D109, D131, D134, D37, D59, `connectors/router.py`, `docs/notes/J2S4-c.md`.
- Decided: `PICK_PURPOSES` gains `agent_chat`; `RouteContext` gains `pick: Optional[Pick]`; `Router._pick_for` returns `ctx.pick` first for a purpose in `PICK_PURPOSES` (a call that carries a session id no longer loses its pick) and `_plan` lets the pick decide before the session's own provider; the `connector.choice_unavailable` event of a pick that cannot run now carries the call's session id. `orchestrator/agent_ask.py` resolves the pick with `agent_sessions.effective` (own, then run, then team) and passes it only when its source is not `default` (the policy decides then, with no "your choice could not run" note). The call is `Router.ask(AskRequest(..., ctx=RouteContext(tag=CallTag(function=who, purpose="agent_chat", run_id, dept_id, task_id, atom=<the run's overhead atom>, workspace="none"), session_id=sid, node_id=node, allowance=Allowance(max_calls=2, max_usd=0.25, deadline=now+120), pick=pick)))`: so the usage row carries the session, the function, the purpose and the overhead atom, and the provider quota, health and fallback of the router apply. The user text (the run's records and the question) goes through the router's egress in replace mode first (the audit row, which also replaces the app's secret values) and then through `scrubbed(text, redact)` (credential shapes replaced), so a router with no egress is no gap; J2's fixed system text goes through `scrubbed` only; the stored question and answer lines are scrubbed too: the records of a run can hold a pasted secret and this prompt is Sutra's turn, not J2's. Limits: `services.limits.gate(...)` non-empty means the owner's monthly limit or plan window is reached: the answer is from the records, with the words of the gate as the note. Offline (`router.mode == "offline"`, which includes `SUTRA_CONNECTOR=stub`): records only, the lines are stored all the same, with the note "No model is connected". One question at a time per session (a second gets 409 `busy`).
- Why: the pick is the owner's per-agent choice (D131); the ledger and the atom rule are the app's accounting; the scrub is what the egress design (D109) promises for every prompt that is the app's own; a bounded call cannot run away.
- Rejected: booking to the session through `RouteContext.session_id` alone (then `_pick_for` ignored the pick, and the model and effort of the session were lost); charging the answer to a work atom (D59: an answer is overhead); observing instead of scrubbing (D109 reserves observe-only for J2's own prompts); no ceiling (a question is one short call).
- Diagram: label "answer: own pick, scrubbed, booked", pill on Connector router.

## D150 — Tests move only through the recorders; the shape golden stops depending on the seed; J2's own new test blocks run on the vendored engine
- Sources: R5, D84, D66, `docs/notes/J2S4-a3.md`, `docs/notes/J2S4-e3a.md`, `docs/notes/J2S4-l.md`.
- Decided: goldens are re-recorded only by `scripts/record_engine_golden.py`, `scripts/record_fn_golden.py`, `scripts/record_api_shapes.py` and `scripts/capture_j2_fixtures.py`, after the vendoring commit and never by hand; each takes an extraction of `prototypes/j2` (`--source` or `--j2`, with `--commit`; no default folder outside this repository) except the function-node recorder, which reads the vendored engine. `record_api_shapes.key_paths` collapses `enums.<Name>` to `enums.*` (and `MAPS` gains `enums`), so a new seed template cannot move `api_shapes.json` again; its route list is J2's own server's, in three modes (live, saved, idle), and the `patched` section and `--patched-only` are gone. `record_engine_golden.py` gains a seventh scenario, `agent_answer`: the one prompt J2's agent view adds (`j2_agent.answer`'s system and user text around a stub `ask`, with J2's time-of-day stamps frozen so two runs give one prompt). The capture of the page's fixtures (`tests/ui/fixtures/j2`, 80 files) is deterministic (two captures are byte equal), proves the extraction untouched, and applies P15's rule to the six home paths of the seed index before it writes a file. The legacy flow test is Sutra's port of J2's flow test, brought to the pin in two merges inside this repository's history (no commit holds the file the port was made from, so the base was built from the difference) and runs J2's new blocks (`late_judge_run`, `decide_for_me`, `studio_model`, `studio_views`, `plan_map_view`, `simple_redesign`, `chat_panel`, `function_chat`, `view_kept`, `run_settings`, `artifacts_groups`, `agent_views`) on the vendored engine; a block that needs J2's saved runs or Chrome prints why it did not run, and `SUTRA_J2_CHECKOUT` is the plain extraction of `prototypes/j2`, which holds no saved runs. `EXPECTED.json` records the pin, the sha256 of J2's flow test and the minimums (70 `ok` lines without a checkout, 109 with an extraction).
- Why: the tests that fail on a new pin fail for stated reasons (seed counts, two prompt goldens, function-node goldens, library key paths, one meta key, a header pin); each becomes the new expectation by a recorder, so a reviewer reads a diff of recorded output, not of edited numbers.
- Rejected: editing golden files; pinning `enums.<Name>` keys again (they are data); skipping J2's new blocks (R3 asks for cross-verification); a composite folder with saved runs from another place (the only J2 source is this repository, D154).
- Diagram: label "goldens only by recorder", pill on Department records.

## D151 — Closed library models for the four new view shapes wait; the shape goldens and J2's own blocks hold them
- Sources: D39, D46, D47, R3, `docs/notes/J2S4-b.md`.
- Decided: the library's closed wire models (`MapDoc`, `ArtifactsTreeDoc`, `StoryDoc`, ...) are not extended in this release to `plan-map/1`, `activity/1`, `artifacts-groups/1` and the catalog view. The record changes that strict mode would reject are made (D145). The four shapes are pinned by `api_shapes.json` (key paths, three modes, recorded from J2's own server) and by J2's own flow blocks. Follow-up W-WIRE: closed models and `tests/library/test_lib_wire.py` cases for them, when J2's Artifacts rows stop moving (7 commits touched `j2_artifacts.py` between the first import on 2026-11-06 and 2026-11-10, five of them on 2026-11-09).
- Why: a closed model of 13 row kinds that J2 changes every day would fail the next sync for no gain to the owner; the shape golden already fails on any key that appears or goes.
- Rejected: models now (a rewrite at the next sync); no pin at all (R3).
- Diagram: key only, label "new J2 views pinned by shape goldens".

## D152 — Decide for me is one switch in one `settings.json`: patch P16, the eighth task key, an inbox command the host applies, and the page's switch through `POST /api/settings`
- Sources: R1, R3, D14 (one process per run, files only), D132 (the inbox is the one door to a live run), D136, `docs/notes/J2S4-a.md`, `docs/notes/J2S4-b.md`.
- Decided: J2's switch is `J2Run.auto`, set by `POST /api/settings {decide_for_me}`, and J2 saves it as `{"decide_for_me": X}` in `<run>/settings.json`, the file the engine host already writes its resolved engine settings to before J2 starts (`HOST_FILES` in `rehydrate.py`). There is one `settings.json` per run: patch P16 makes J2's two writes (`set_auto`, and the one at the start of `_run_safe`) add `decide_for_me` to what the file holds instead of replacing it (a missing or torn file reads as empty), and the library catalogues the file as an open run object so strict mode accepts it. **The setting**: `EngineSettings.decide_for_me: bool = False` (environment variable `J2_DECIDE_FOR_ME`, exported by the host before J2 is imported; the host's `settings.json` carries the field name) and the eighth task key `J2_DECIDE_FOR_ME` (`"0"` or `"1"`: `TASK_SETTING_KEYS` and `TaskSettings` agree, `docs/ENVIRONMENT.md` has the row): a run started with the key has the switch on from its first question. **The command**: `POST /api/settings` (token required) is `views.settings` and `DeptCommands.settings(on)` (`None` only reads); the live run is another process, so a live run gets the inbox line `{"kind": "setting", "decide_for_me": bool}`, written by the task service (`TaskService.send_setting`, wired as the optional `services.send_setting`) and applied by the host (`engine/host/inbox.py`) at once, ahead of the FIFO, whatever the run is doing, with `J2Run.set_auto` (which persists it through P16). **The team's choice** is also kept in `page.json` `next_settings.J2_DECIDE_FOR_ME`, so a team with no run, and every later run, start with it; unlike the output choice, a delivery does not clear it (J2's switch stays through Start over and follow-ups). **The state**: `/api/state.decide_for_me` is J2's own snapshot field while a process holds it; the idle snapshot takes it from the effective settings (the page's choice included); a run whose process has gone shows the latest choice. The stdlib adapter applies the switch to `RUN.set_auto`.
- Why: J2's own write replaced the file the host had written, so the engine lost its resolved settings; one file with merging writes keeps both, and J2's `saved_snapshot` still reads the field from the same place. The engine runs in its own process (D14), so a live switch can only reach it as a line in its inbox, applied at once because the owner's switch must not wait behind a queued message. Decide for me is J2's feature and R3 asks for all of it.
- Rejected: a second file for the switch (two truths about one run); the server writing the run's `settings.json` itself (D14: the engine process is the one writer of a run's files, and the running J2 object would never see it); a new run event for it (the event protocol stays as it is); leaving the field out of `EngineSettings` (the host exports only the settings it knows, so a task setting `J2_DECIDE_FOR_ME` would never reach J2); clearing the choice after a delivery as the output choice is.
- Diagram: label "Decide for me: one settings.json, P16", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D153 — The pin moved to the Studio tip: `j2_studio.py` is vendored, the Studio routes are views, and the preview frames keep Sutra's sandbox
- Sources: the owner's move of the pin to the latest `prototypes/j2` while the lanes ran (2026-11-10: it holds `origin/studio-ui`), D83 (the preview sandbox), D136, D137, D140, `docs/notes/J2S4-e1.md`, `docs/notes/J2S4-a3.md`.
- Decided: the pin moved from `00efb179` to `025e265c594564f47a2365874cd5a5cc5d0d20a6` (tree `21c67045fe3230c1ab9be381c2f21465ea91849c`) after the first lane work. What the Studio commits add: `j2_studio.py` (a new module: the Studio's parts, records and stream), Decide for me in `j2.py` (D152), a fix in `j2_realization.py`, the routes `GET /api/record`, `GET /doc/<name>`, `GET /view` and `POST /api/settings` in J2's server file, and a redesign of the Simple view (`j2_ui.html` grew from 4,589 to 5,524 lines). Sutra takes all of it: `j2_studio.py` joins `CODE` (17 code modules, 208 files) with its import under P1; the four routes are ordinary views (D140: `view_page` is the app's copy of J2's function) and `sutra_ctx.js` `J2_EXACT` gains `/api/record`, `/api/settings` and `/view` and `J2_PREFIXES` gains `/doc/` (33 names, five prefixes); J2's snapshot gains `decide_for_me`, `auto_answers` and `studio` (56 keys became 59); the page is re-ported with 33 edits (D137: E2c for the Studio's second frame, E5 and E19 moved, E20g to E20i and E23 new). The preview frames keep Sutra's sandbox (D83: no `allow-same-origin`, and the engine's sandbox header on what it serves): the Studio reads its frames' documents (`sDoc`, `sFrameSetup`, `sDecorate`: tags on the parts of a preview, a glow on a new version, scroll restore across versions, link interception), which under the sandbox it cannot, so those features stay off; the code is guarded, the frames still show the output, and the list is "not taken" in the sync note. The Studio delta moved none of the seven prompt goldens and none of the two function-node goldens, only the `state` shapes of `api_shapes.json` (D150).
- Why: R3 and the owner's rule that the latest of `prototypes/j2` is the source; the sandbox is the app's promise about generated sites (D83) and a decoration is not worth removing it.
- Rejected: staying at `00efb179` (the owner asked for the latest); `allow-same-origin` on the preview frames (a generated site would then run with the app's own origin: its token and storage); a patch that makes the Studio decorate through messages instead of the frame's document (a J2 change for a cosmetic feature).
- Diagram: label "pin at the Studio tip, sandbox kept", pill on User chat interface · J2 Simple view.

## D154 — `prototypes/j2` of this repository is the only J2 source; no other J2 repository is read, run or compared
- Sources: the owner's rule of 2026-11-10 (the one J2 reference is the folder `prototypes/j2` of this repository, pinned by commit and tree), D114, D136, `docs/notes/J2-DIFF-OF-RECORD.md`.
- Decided: from sync 4 on, every J2 file Sutra Pro carries, every golden, fixture and test copy, and every comparison is made against an extraction of `prototypes/j2` of this repository at a commit and a tree id written down (D136; the two scripts and the recorders take the extraction with `--source` or `--j2` and `--commit`, and none has a default folder outside this repository). No separate repository of the J2 prototype is cloned, fetched, read or run, and none is named in a new document, script or test. This supersedes the part of D114 that allowed a second source beside the copy (it took a file from the separate source once the change had reached it): the copy is the only source and a later sync is one more pin of it. D114's other parts stand (the vendoring script and the port script are the only way in, and each change is a recorded patch with a sync note). The standing guard is `scripts/j2_diff_of_record.py --check`: exit 0 only when `vendor_j2.py --check` and `port_j2_ui.py --check` pass and every file of the pinned folder is vendored, a generated page file, a legacy test copy, or excluded with a reason; its report is `docs/notes/J2-DIFF-OF-RECORD.md`. The notes of the earlier syncs keep their history as written.
- Why: the owner's rule is one J2 reference; a commit plus a tree id is reproducible; with one folder the difference of record can say, for every file, whether it is carried and why not.
- Rejected: reading both sources (two truths: what differs would need a ruling at every sync); defaulting `--source` to a sibling checkout (a developer's home path in a tool, R5); rewriting the notes of earlier syncs (they record what was true when they were written).
- Diagram: label "prototypes/j2 is the only J2 source", pill on J2 chips.

## D155 — The Studio sheet breaks two things Sutra's panels need; Sutra's own sheet puts them back, and J2's sheet is never edited
- Sources: D122 (the page moves fluidly: compositor properties only), D137, D138, `docs/notes/J2S4-e2.md`, `docs/notes/J2S4-e1.md`.
- Decided: (1) The node panel owns its bubbles and answer buttons: the Studio sheet scopes `.sb`, `.sb.me`, `.sb.team` and the answer buttons `.opts` to `#sview`, the drawer sits outside it (it opens over every view), so its chat lines were bare text; `sutra-panel.css` carries the same rules for `.nmsg` (proved by the computed style of every element kind in five scenes of the panel under the previous sheet and under the Studio sheet: seven kinds looked different before, none after). (2) The Studio sheet breaks D122 (three `transition: all` rules, thirty layout properties transitioned or animated, six keyframes on filter, box-shadow or left): `sutra-panel.css` has a rule for each, on transform, opacity and colour from the shared scale, and keyframes of the same name replace J2's; `motion_css.test.js` applies its rules 1 and 2 to the sheets that are Sutra's, records J2's findings in `j2.css` exactly, and its rule 8 proves that every offending declaration of the sheets Sutra does not edit has its replacement. (3) `contrast.test.js` judges the text of a rule that paints its own background (the owner's bubble: `--on` on `--accent`) by that pair, not against the three surfaces. `j2.css` is never edited: it is regenerated at every sync.
- Why: a regenerated file cannot carry a hand edit, and the page must stay fluid (D122) and readable whatever J2's sheet does next; one test proves coverage, so the next sync fails loudly when J2's sheet breaks a rule again.
- Rejected: editing `j2.css` (a hand edit of a generated file, lost at the next sync); a numbered patch on J2's style block (a J2 change for what Sutra's own sheet can say); dropping D122 on the team page (the owner asked for a fluid page); restyling the node panel inside `#sview` (the drawer is outside it by design).
- Diagram: label "Sutra's sheet keeps panels and motion", pill on band:EXPERIENCE.

## D156 — The stop ladder signals a process only after it has identified it (amends D23 and D24)
- Sources: D14, D23, D24, `docs/notes/PROJECT-WORK.md` (the caution after the first real run of project work, D135), the 0.3.1 patch.
- Decided: `ProcessLauncher.stop` first asks whether the pid is one of its engine processes. A pid this server spawned (a Process handle in `_handles`) is, with no look at it. Any other pid (a task re-adopted after the app restarted: a number an earlier server recorded) is only when `command_of(pid)` (`ps -p <pid> -o command=`; `oslayer.process_command` on Windows, as for the start-up pass) shows `<module> --run-dir <the task's run folder>`, the module being the launcher's own (`sutra_app.engine` in the app). Anything else is another program's now and is treated as gone: `stop` writes no stop line, waits for nothing, signals nothing, logs one warning and returns, and the owner's Stop, a reset and the app's shutdown go on to settle the task as they do for a process that is gone. The check is made once, before the stop line is waited on and before the first signal; it runs on a worker thread (on Windows it is a PowerShell call). The identity is said once, as `is_engine_command(command, module, run_dir)`, and the start-up pass of D24 calls the same function. It leaves double quotes out on both sides: Windows shows an argument with a space in it in quotes (`subprocess.list2cmdline`), which the old text of the start-up pass could not match, so one text now matches either way. Nothing changes for the engine processes the ladder is for, the spawned ones and the re-adopted ones.
- Why: a pid in a task row can belong to another program by the time the ladder runs (a reboot, or a crash and a restart), and the ladder signals the process GROUP of whatever owns the number: SIGTERM, then SIGKILL, on a program that was never ours. The first real run of project work met it with a copy of a running app's data (the pid there was the owner's own working engine); D24 had the identity check only for a silent pid at start-up, while the owner's Stop, a reset and the app's shutdown reach the same ladder with a pid nobody had looked at.
- Rejected: checking in the three callers (the next one would forget); checking inside `alive` (the watcher's crash test calls it every tick, and a `ps` that cannot be read would crash a live task); signalling when `ps` cannot be read (an engine left running is found by the next start-up pass or by the owner, a killed program of someone else's is not undone); a second look before each signal (the wait polls `alive` every 50 ms, so the engine would have to leave and its number go to another program inside one poll); an identity of the module alone (another run's engine would be signalled for this run).
- Diagram: label "stop ladder: identity first", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D157 — A work atom records what its turn produced, the plain words and the model's last reply; the store scrubs them and the card's Result block shows them
- Sources: D58 (atom events and rows), D59 (every call has an atom id), D47 and D145 (a record change is additive: the family versions stay 1), D149 and D109 (the app's scrub), D150 (goldens only by recorder), D6, the owner's screenshot of the node panel's Details tab (an Adaptation atom, verify failed at attempt 2 of 3: the card showed the checks and the cost, not what the atom made), `docs/notes/PROJECT-WORK.md`.
- Decided: **What a turn produced**, per function (`engine/fnodes/output.py`, pure, from the turn buffer the hooks already fill, nothing new asked of J2): Identity the charter (`the charter: <name>, 7 done-when items`), a rule (`ruled for Adaptation: <clause>`, or a split) or a question (`asked you 2 questions: <first>`), and what it decided on the way (the goal is reached, more budget approved, the owner's answer read); Adaptation a proposal (`a proposal of 2 workflows: W-1 Build the page, Write the copy`), a revision, an accepted or parked proposal, a question to Identity, a dispute, or the verdict on a failed atom; Priority GRANT, COUNTER or REFUSE with the workflows it priced, or HOLD and REQUEST_APPROVAL for an envelope; Coordination the order, its rule and its holds; Audit the count by verdict and each finding that does not pass, with its evidence. Every turn also says what it did: `posted 3 messages to Coordination`, `code posted 1 message: <first line>` (what J2's own code said during the turn: a hand-off by code is a whole turn when the queue is empty), `saved the findings`. A turn that made nothing says `nothing: no usable reply, no message posted, no record saved`, so an atom with nothing recorded at all is only one from before this decision.
