- Why: crash and restart keep text and tools; no FTS churn; no pending-row hazards in `reconcile`; the provider consumer is never blocked by JSON or SQL.
- Rejected: a `pending` placeholder row; a write per token; serialising under the lock.
- Diagram: label "checkpoint table, not messages", pill on Chat sessions.

## D212 — New turns store the ordered block list under a sibling meta key; the fold counts a call and its result as one unit
- Sources: analysis section 5 (stored text, block cap), `models.py` 409, `chats.py` blocks write, critique point 13.
- Decided: `ordered: true` is written as a sibling key next to `blocks` (`MessageMeta` is J2's closed key set) and read into an additive `ChatMessageOut.ordered`; the `text` column stays `result.result`, so text is stored twice (bounded by `MAX_TEXT`, asserted); the fold closes the list past 400 elements with one `truncated` "more tool calls" block, counting a `tool_use` and its `tool_result` as one unit and never separating them, so call and result stay paired and the 500 read cap cannot fail a store.
- Why: the settled view equals the live view; legacy messages and search are untouched.
- Rejected: a key inside `MessageMeta`; lifting the read cap; emptying the `text` column (breaks search); folding results separately.
- Diagram: label "ordered blocks, fold by pairs", pill on Chat sessions.

## D213 — Stop is two-phase and honest: the server freezes at the request, the route never blocks, `stopped:false` means nothing is running
- Sources: analysis section 2 row 18 and risk 9, `connectors/process.py`, critique points 9, 16, 17 and 18.
- Decided: the client shows `stopping` at once; `LiveTurn.request_stop` sets the token, freezes the accumulator (no more text emitted or kept) and publishes `turn.stopping` in one ordered step, so every tab and the stored text end at the same character; the stop route schedules the process cancel in the background and answers at once; the UI ends `stopping` only on a terminal event, and a `stopped:false` answer triggers the snapshot resync; `sending` to `stopping` queues the stop until the 202; `turn.stopped` is sent only after exit and store; open tools end `interrupted`; a stop that races completion ends `done`. The POSIX ladder is SIGINT 5 s, SIGTERM 5 s, SIGKILL 2 s; nothing is claimed for Windows (different ladder, abrupt, unverified). The stalled copy shows the real idle limit (`idle_limit_s`), and the idle-timeout failure message names it.
- Why: Stop feels immediate and never claims more than happened; the 12 s ladder must not hold the UI.
- Rejected: optimistic "Stopped"; a client-side freeze that disagrees with the stored text; the UI waiting on the stop response; marking interrupted tools failed; Windows timing claims.
- Diagram: label "two-phase stop, freeze at request", pill on Connector router.

## D214 — Rollout is behind `SUTRA_LIVE_V1`, with the legacy path kept for one release
- Sources: `architecture.md` Rollout.
- Decided: default off until the client lands, then on; off restores today's `chat.partial` path unchanged (read through the D221 adapter); the checkpoint table is ignored when off; the ref and `ChatMessageOut` changes are additive and read the same either way.
- Why: the change spans server, parsers and four views; a switch makes rollback a config change.
- Rejected: big-bang replacement; a permanent dual path.
- Diagram: label "SUTRA_LIVE_V1 rollback flag", pill on Orchestration.

## D215 — One stop owner: a per-turn `CancelToken` through `ChatPort`, `Router` and `base._events`
- Sources: `connectors/router.py` (`_chat`, `_chat_turn`, `chat_stop`, `_drive`), `connectors/base.py` (`_events`, `_track`), critique points 1 and 18.
- Decided: `direct_running.begin_turn` creates the token; it is passed `ChatPort.stream(cancel=)` to `Router` to `connector.stream(cancel=)`; `base._events` binds the process to it right after `_track` and a token already set cancels it at once; it is also checked before session setup, before spawn and between events; the Router back-off waits on the token and ends at once on Stop; `Router.chat_stop` returns True iff a turn is running. `_chat`'s entry `discard` cannot erase a token; the legacy early-flag set stays for callers without one.
- Why: today Stop is lost between `_chat_active` being set and the process being tracked (`stopped:false` is read as "nothing to stop"); a flag read before spawn cannot cover that window.
- Rejected: a `LiveTurn` flag checked before spawn; keeping `_chat_stop_early`; returning False when the process is not yet tracked.
- Diagram: label "CancelToken: one stop owner", pill on Connector router.

## D216 — Attempt-scoped refs; a superseded reply stays visible and leaves the model's history
- Sources: `chat_messages_src` unique index, `direct_turns.Ending`, `chats._turn_of`, `chat_v2.recover_interrupted`, composer `reconcile`, critique point 2.
- Decided: attempt 1 keeps today's refs (historical rows stay valid); attempt N >= 2 appends `.N` to every reply, note, error and switch ref; `N` is the server-computed ordinal fixed at generation start and returned as `attempt_no`; the `Ending` pattern, `_turn_of`, `recover_interrupted` (`= ... OR LIKE '....%'`) and `reconcile` (ends on `turn === id` and the running attempt) change together under a table test; `ChatMessageOut` gains `attempt` and `superseded`; superseded replies are collapsed under "Earlier try" and excluded from carry-over and history.
- Why: a retry on the same owner line would reuse refs, `post()` would return `None` and the reply would be lost; the replaced reply must not be told to the model as if it stood.
- Rejected: deleting the first attempt's reply; reusing refs; a new owner line per retry.
- Diagram: label "attempt-scoped refs", pill on Chat sessions.

## D217 — The Router emits `route.*` events; connecting, failing and restarting are shown only from them
- Sources: `Router._drive`, `ChatPort.stream`, critique point 4.
- Decided: on the chat path only, `_drive` yields `route.plan`, `route.attempt` (provider, model, attempt number, idle limit) right before spawn, and `route.failed` (reason, retryable, action, delay, next provider) before the sleep; `turn.connecting`, `attempt.failed` and `attempt.restart` derive from them; the label for the first is "Starting <provider>", never "connected" (that is `turn.started`, from the CLI's init line).
- Why: nothing before the CLI's init line was observable, and the retry back-off was silent, leaving a stale "Writing" with frozen text.
- Rejected: a `connecting` event with no source; a `restart` with no data; building the note only after the turn.
- Diagram: label "route events: plan, attempt, failed", pill on Connector router.

## D218 — `BlockAccumulator` is the sole owner of block structure
- Sources: `ChatPort.stream` (blocks, `saw_partial`, restart), critique point 7.
- Decided: one pure class in `connectors/chat_blocks.py` owns block ids, per-message index scoping (Claude block indexes restart in each assistant message), `saw_partial` per message, matching of the later `tool_call` event to the open block by `tool_id`, restart, the redaction hook, the pair-aware fold and `stored_order`; `ChatPort.stream` uses it for every caller and `LiveTurn` consumes its output and adds only envelope, timing, checkpoint and publishing.
- Why: two accumulators diverge on restart, tool ids and indexes.
- Rejected: `LiveTurn` re-implementing accumulation; a global `saw_partial`.
- Diagram: label "BlockAccumulator: one block owner", pill on Connector router.

## D219 — The stored message is the final attempt; earlier tries are a stored note; `stored_order` maps live keys
- Sources: `ChatPort` restart (clears blocks), D207, critique point 5.
- Decided: the stored reply holds only the final attempt's blocks (matching the `text` column and the live reply item); earlier attempts with tool blocks are summarised, from block titles and redacted, in the turn's switch note (at most 20 lines), so they stay visible after a reload; `turn.done`/`stopped`/`failed` carry `stored_order` (live `block_id`s in stored order) so promotion maps by position, never by assumed index.
- Why: live "Earlier try" blocks and a stored list with only the last attempt cannot share index keys; the note keeps the honest record without breaking the mapping.
- Rejected: storing all attempts' blocks in one list; dropping earlier tries at settle without a record.
- Diagram: label "final attempt stored, earlier as note", pill on Chat sessions.

## D220 — Idempotency lookup and claim come before `begin`; a duplicate is 200, not 409 or 500
- Sources: `api/chat_v2.direct`, `direct_running.begin`, `_post`, `ChatMessageOut`, critique point 6.
- Decided: the route validates, looks up `client_id` (a stored `direct-ask:<client_id>` line or a running claim), and only then calls `begin_turn(sid, client_id)`, which also covers two concurrent POSTs; a duplicate returns `duplicate:true` and starts nothing; `_post` returns `Optional[int]` and `None` means a replayed ref; `ChatMessageOut` exposes `client_id` (not `src_ref`) so the optimistic owner line and the stored line reconcile to one bubble, in the sending tab and in a second tab.
- Why: today `begin` refuses first so a double POST is 409, and a duplicate ref would be `None.id` (a 500); without `client_id` on the read model a page shows two bubbles.
- Rejected: exposing `src_ref`; reconciling by text; relying on the unique index alone.
- Diagram: label "client_id before begin", pill on Chat sessions.

## D221 — AI workers and the legacy path are read through a `chat.partial` adapter with `stream_mode:"snapshot"`
- Sources: `chat_composer.js` (`state().turn`), `agent_live.json` path, critique point 8.
- Decided: `fromPartial` turns `chat.partial {text, reset}` into `partial.snapshot` actions on a synthetic generation with one text block (cumulative, replaced per snapshot, fresh on `reset`); `source:"partial"` switches off seq gap detection and snapshot resync; no tool activity and no label claim; turn end stays the existing reconcile; no server change. The flag-off direct path uses the same adapter.
- Why: the composer's turn is a projection of the new store, and the polled path has no seq, generation or blocks; without an adapter AI-worker chat would regress.
- Rejected: leaving the composer with two turn models; converting the AI-worker server path in this scope.
- Diagram: label "chat.partial adapter (snapshot)", pill on Chat sessions.

## D222 — Seq and publish happen under one lock, every event goes through `LiveTurn`, `turn.accepted` carries `client_id`, every reconnect resyncs
- Sources: `orchestrator/events.py` (`publish_one`), `sutra_events.js` (hello after an error becomes `server.resync`), critique point 10.
- Decided: producing an event takes `_pub_lock`, assigns seq and publishes before releasing, so bus order equals seq order and a flushed delta cannot overtake a later one; the route's `turn.accepted` and `turn.stopping` are published through `LiveTurn`; `turn.accepted` carries `client_id` so the sender matches it before it knows `generation_id`; the page resyncs (stream open, buffer, snapshot) on every `server.resync` its own handle synthesises, with no 3 s threshold, and polls the snapshot every second while `polling`.
- Why: seq assigned apart from publish can reorder on the wire; the first event precedes the 202; a lost terminal event is otherwise found only by the 15 s stall rule.
- Rejected: assigning seq under the data lock and publishing after; a time threshold before resync; route-side publishing.
- Diagram: label "seq+publish one lock; resync on reconnect", pill on Chat sessions.

## D223 — A tool line's title and target are set when the provider's `tool_call` arrives, not at `content_block_stop`
- Sources: `tests/fixtures/connectors/claude/stream_partials_tool_ok.jsonl` (the real capture of 2026-11-14, STR-1), `docs/design/streaming/capture-notes.md`, `connectors/chat_blocks.py`.
- Decided: Claude sends the complete `assistant` message (the whole `tool_use` with its input) BEFORE the block's `content_block_stop`, so the title and target are computed at the existing `tool_call` event and matched to the open block by `tool_id`; `block_stop` only completes the input and carries the privately buffered input as a fallback when no `assistant` message came; the tool stays open until its `tool_result`. A Codex command starts at `item.started` (with its command text) and ends at `item.completed` with its exit code; an `agent_message` is only ever a whole `item.completed`, so Codex is `stream_mode: "activity"`. Neither parser changes an existing event: block kinds are new events, gated on a partials turn (Claude) or a chat turn (Codex).
- Why: the design assumed the input was complete only at the stop event; the capture shows it is complete earlier, and an assumption that costs the page a delay it does not need is worth correcting; older parser tests assert exact event data and stay green.
- Rejected: computing the title in the parser (the existing `tool_call` data is asserted exactly by the older tests, and `ChatPort` can reach the turn's folder through the router anyway); sending the buffered input to the page.
- Diagram: label "tool title at tool_call (real order)", pill on Claude connector.

## D224 — The live turn uses one lock for sequence, accumulator and publish, and every live kind is opt-in
- Sources: `orchestrator/live_turn.py`, `connectors/chat_port.py` (`acc=`), `connectors/router.py` (`routes=`), STR-5 and STR-6.
- Decided: a `LiveTurn` guards the accumulator, the sequence number and the bus call with one re-entrant lock, so a snapshot taken under it is exactly "everything up to seq N" (the design had two locks and needed the pair consistent anyway); the JSON of a checkpoint and every SQL statement run outside it, on a separate writer thread that is skipped while the previous write runs. `ChatPort.stream(acc=)` yields the live kinds (`started`, `route`, `block_*`, a `restart` with data, a `turn_done` with ordered blocks, the settle map and earlier tries) and `Router.chat_stream(routes=True)` yields the route events; without them every older caller sees exactly the events it always saw, which is what keeps the existing connector and port tests unmodified (one test that called the private `_map` now asks the accumulator, which owns that mapping).
- Why: the second lock bought nothing a snapshot could use; making the new kinds opt-in is cheaper than auditing every consumer of `ConnectorEvent` and `ChatEvent`.
- Rejected: two locks with a copy for the snapshot; adding the live kinds to every chat stream.
- Diagram: label "one lock; live kinds opt-in", pill on Chat sessions.

## D225 — Stop is truthful and instant at the route, and the new refusal is `not_retryable`
- Sources: `orchestrator/api/chat_v2.py` (`_stop_live`, `_retry_live`), `orchestrator/errors.py`, `connectors/router.py` (`chat_stop`), STR-2 and STR-7.
- Decided: with the live chat on, `POST .../direct/stop` calls `LiveTurn.request_stop()` (flush, freeze, token, `turn.stopping`, in that order) and schedules `port.stop` (the cancel ladder, up to 12 s) on a background task; the response `{session_id, stopped, state}` returns at once, and `stopped: true` means a turn was running and Stop is requested, `false` that none is. With the flag off the route is what it was. A retry of an owner line that was answered or is not the chat's newest is `409 not_retryable`, a code added to the closed list of error codes (and to TROUBLESHOOTING) rather than overloading `busy`, which means "a turn is running". The idle-timeout sentence names the limit and still starts with the words the older tests assert ("<Provider> did not answer within L s: it sent no output for that long, so it was stopped").
- Why: a Stop that waits for the ladder looks like a hang; a refusal that means one thing must not share a code with one that means another.
- Rejected: waiting for the process in the route; reusing `busy` for "already answered".
- Diagram: label "stop answers at once; not_retryable", pill on Connector router.

## D226 — A requested Stop always ends the turn stopped, the ticker never spins on a slow checkpoint, and the chat is freed before the ticker is joined
- Sources: `orchestrator/live_turn.py` (`_next_timeout`, `stopping`), `orchestrator/api/chat_v2.py` (`_answer_live`), review of STR-6 and STR-7.
- Decided: when a checkpoint is due but the previous write is still running, the ticker looks again after `CKPT_RETRY_S` (0.05 s) instead of at a deadline already past (it had spun about 700,000 times a second while holding the lock the provider events use). When Stop was requested, `_answer_live` takes the stopped ending even if the provider returned a result (the process had already exited when the cancel ladder ran): the stored text is what every tab saw, and `turn.done` is never sent after `turn.stopping`. A Stop asked after the turn ended still ends `done` (D225). `direct_running.end` runs before `LiveTurn.close`, so the chat accepts the next send while the join of a slow write is still waiting.
- Why: the stored reply must equal what was shown; a slow disk must not cost CPU or streaming latency or a spurious 409.
- Rejected: dropping the checkpoint deadline while a write runs (nothing would wake the ticker when it ends); closing without joining (the join is bounded and keeps tests and shutdown clean).
- Diagram: label "stop wins over a late result" on Live turn.

## D227 — Client layers: intake, store, client, view
- Sources: streaming design D202, D220, D221, D222; `sutra_events.js` (the page's own SutraEvents handle stays the transport: an EventSource, so the browser frames the bytes and a frame that arrives in pieces or several in one read reaches the page whole and in order); `tests/ui/js/chat_live_*.test.js`.
- Decided: no layer reads the clock or the network except the client; the reducer takes `now` on the action. A hole (a `seq` that skips, a text `offset` that is not the code-point length already shown, a first frame that is not seq 1, an oversize piece) makes the intake buffer every later frame and the client read `GET /api/chats/{sid}/turn`; the buffered frames newer than the snapshot follow it in order. Every reconnect (`server.resync`), the stream being down (polling, every second) and 15 s of silence run the same sequence. A terminal event, a snapshot that says nothing runs, or 20 s of silence releases the stored reply that was held back.
- Why: each piece has one input and one output and is tested without a page; the holes the design lists (offset, seq, first frame) are exactly what a chunked or dropped stream produces, and the cursor is the seq the snapshot carries.
- Rejected: a fetch-based SSE reader of our own (it would repeat SutraEvents' retry and polling and the server sends `chat.live` with id 0, so there is no byte cursor to resume from); counting UTF-16 units for `offset` (the server counts code points, an emoji would look like a hole).
- Diagram: label "client layers: intake store client view", pill on band:EXPERIENCE.

## D228 — The composer is live only when the page asks for it
- Sources: `chat_composer.js`, `tests/ui/js/chat_composer.test.js` (unchanged), `tests/ui/js/chat_composer_live.test.js`, streaming design D220.
- Decided: without `live` every older caller and test sees the composer it always had (the existing 43 composer tests pass unmodified); with it, a Stop pressed while the POST is in flight shows Stopping at once and sends the stop only after the 202 gives the turn an id, a stop answer of `stopped:false` never clears Stopping (the snapshot decides), a failed stop request brings the button back, and the owner's line shows at once and is replaced by the stored line with the same `client_id` (one bubble, also with a second tab). Retry uses `/direct/retry` (no second owner line) and falls back to sending the words again on a server without the live chat.
- Why: the old composer tests encode behaviour the legacy path must keep; a switch is cheaper than two controllers.
- Rejected: replacing the composer's turn object (twenty tests assign and compare it); a Retry that re-sends the words on a live server (a second owner line and a repeated side effect).
- Diagram: label "composer live opt-in; stop queued", pill on band:EXPERIENCE.

## D229 — A reply is one node, painted from a model, live or stored
- Sources: `chat_view.js` (`shell` option), `chat_live_view.js`, streaming design D207, D212, D219; `tests/e2e/test_browser_live_chat.py` (a node of the reply is never recreated; the item's top does not move at settle).
- Decided: the shell's html is a function of (turn, try) only, so `Fluid.patchList` never replaces it; a stored `id` and every state is painted into it. The status and the actions share one row under the blocks, present live and settled, so nothing above it moves at settle. Historical messages (ordered blocks, a text-only message, a legacy message with its tools folded in one row) go through the same model; a superseded try is collapsed under Earlier try. The live item exists only until a stored message has its key.
- Why: a reply that is built twice (a streaming row, then a bubble) flashes and jumps; one node painted by one function cannot.
- Rejected: keeping the streaming row outside the scroller and moving its text into a bubble at the end (the swap is the jump); putting the status above the text (it disappears at settle and the text moves up).
- Diagram: label "one reply node; settle in place", pill on band:EXPERIENCE.

## D230 — Text blocks are drawn by appending; a code fence is one node
- Sources: `fluid.js`, `chat_markdown.js`, `tests/ui/js/fluid_text.test.js` (a 200 KB reply in 4,000 pieces: no settled block and no fence recreated), the browser test of a long reply with a table and a fence.
- Decided: the tail is split into text and fence segments and reconciled with what is drawn (a text segment is drawn again when it changed, a fence only changes its text); an unfinished table row and an unclosed span are held or shown as text by `ChatMarkdown.hold` as before; the highlighter is a small DOM-building one (no innerHTML from text); the copy-code button lives inside the settled `<pre>`.
- Why: a streamed fence that is recreated on every piece loses its scroll and selection and costs a layout per piece.
- Rejected: highlighting while streaming (the colours change as a string closes); a third-party highlighter (a dependency for a feature a hundred lines cover, and the page stays offline).
- Diagram: label "append-only text; one node per fence", pill on band:EXPERIENCE.

## D231 — The activity line says only what the wire says, and the number that changes is not announced
- Sources: `chat_live_store.js` (`activity`, `label`), `chat_live_activity.js`, streaming design "Activity timeline" and "Message state machine".
- Decided: stalled shows the real silence and, when the server sent it, the real idle limit ("Still working, no output for 25 s", "stopped after 120 s without output"); a backoff shows the server's reason and counts down the server's delay; a tool shows its name until the title arrives; thinking shows presence and its real duration, never its text; a provider that sends its answer whole says so and gets a fade, never a typewriter.
- Why: an invented progress bar is the one thing that makes a user stop trusting a live view; a label that changes every second is read out every second.
- Rejected: an elapsed-time estimate or a "step 2 of 5" line; one live region for text and clock.
- Diagram: label "honest labels; clock not announced", pill on band:EXPERIENCE.

## D232 — The data of an event keeps its white space
- Sources: `orchestrator/models.py` (`Event`), `library/base.py` (`Value`: `str_strip_whitespace=True`), `tests/orchestrator/test_event_whitespace.py`, the browser test of a long reply (`tests/e2e/test_browser_live_chat.py`).
- Decided: `Event` sets `str_strip_whitespace=False`. `Value` strips the white space around every string it validates, nested strings of `data` included, and a live chat piece ("the ", " price") is nothing but white space at its edge: the page received "aprice in rupees" while the server's offset counted the lost space, so the page's own offset check found a hole at nearly every piece boundary and read the snapshot again and again (the text came out right each time, which is why the earlier tests passed; the reply remounted its code block, which is how the browser test found it).
- Why: a streamed word must reach the page as the provider sent it; the repair by snapshot hid the fault and cost a request and a redraw per piece.
- Rejected: leaving the strip and making the page tolerant of a short piece (the text on screen would differ from the stored text until the next snapshot); a new model for live events (the bus has one `Event` and one data shape).
- Diagram: label "event data keeps its white space", pill on Chat sessions.

## D233 — Stop and Retry live in the reply, and a replaced try collapses at once
- Sources: `chat_live_view.js` (`plan`, `supersede`, `paintActions`), `home.js`, `node_panel.js`, `tests/ui/js/chat_live_view.test.js`, `tests/e2e/test_browser_live_chat.py` (stop, retry, backoff).
- Decided: Stop is a button inside the running reply (named for a screen reader, disabled and worded "Stopping…" at once, Esc as before); a reply that was stopped (a note was stored with it) or whose error row is the reply item offers Retry while it is the owner's newest line and no turn answers; a reply whose failure has its own error line leaves Retry to that line (one button, not two). A retry that follows a try that may have run an edit or a command says so first. A page that read the first try before the retry's reply was stored works out `superseded` from the rows it has, by the server's rule, so the first try is collapsed under Earlier try when the retry's reply arrives, not after the next reload.
- Why: the owner's eyes are on the reply, not on the box; a button that repeats, or a warning that comes after the click, is worse than none; the page never re-reads old rows, so a flag that changes later on the server would otherwise never reach it.
- Rejected: re-fetching the whole chat after a retry (a request per retry for one boolean); a confirm dialog on Retry (the words are in the reply, the choice is one click).
- Diagram: label "stop and retry in the reply", pill on band:EXPERIENCE.

## D234 — Only what arrives moves, the loops stop under reduced motion, and the reader keeps their place
- Sources: `static/css/sutra-panel.css` (the live reply block and the motion tokens), `chat_scroll.js` (`grow`, `keepPlace`, `pillHtml({live})`, `behavior({streaming})`), `tests/ui/js/chat_scroll.test.js`, `tests/ui/js/motion_css.test.js`, `tests/e2e/test_browser_live_chat.py` (scroll, reduced motion, phone width).
- Decided: two tokens join the scale (`--dur-pulse`, `--dur-blink`, both 0 under `prefers-reduced-motion`); a block that arrives fades or rises over `--dur-fast`, a whole text block of a provider that sends its answer whole fades over `--dur-base` (never a typewriter), text that streams does not animate, the presence dot breathes while the turn waits, thinks, starts or is between tries and stops when words flow, the caret is a pseudo-element of the last open block (no node is made for it) and blinks, and under reduce no animation or transition runs inside a reply at all. A reader at the bottom follows the growing reply; a reader who moved up is not moved, a pill says "Reply in progress", and the jump scrolls with `auto` while the reply still streams (the target moves) and `smooth` otherwise unless motion is reduced; a line inserted above a parked reader moves the scroll by its height (`keepPlace`).
- Why: motion that is not attached to an arrival is noise, and a smooth scroll to a target that keeps moving always lags; the existing intent machine of `chat_scroll.js` already told following from reading, so the reply only needed to count as one growing item.
- Rejected: animating every text piece (80 paints a second for nothing), a typewriter for a provider that did not stream (it would claim a stream that did not happen), a count of "N new pieces" on the pill.
- Diagram: label "motion on arrival; keep the reader's place", pill on band:EXPERIENCE.

## D235 — The live chat is on by default; `SUTRA_LIVE_V1=0` goes back
- Sources: `config.py` (`live_v1`), `docs/ENVIRONMENT.md`, D214, the browser tests of `tests/e2e/test_browser_live_chat.py` (17 scenarios on and off) and the unchanged older ones.
- Decided: `live_v1` defaults to `True`. Off is the legacy `chat.partial` path with the same reply node drawn from it (no tool lines, no claim of streaming); the checkpoint table is ignored; the ref and `ChatMessageOut` changes read the same either way. The AI-worker chat keeps its own live row (`AgentChats`, fed by `chat.partial`): its tool line and "writing its reply" rule are unchanged, so it is not read through the adapter after all (D221 is narrowed to the direct chat's flag-off path).
- Why: every package is built and the eight scenarios pass in a real Chrome with the flag on and off; a switch is the rollback.
- Rejected: leaving the default off for another release (the legacy row has none of what the owner asked for); removing the legacy path now (rollback should stay a setting).
- Diagram: label "live chat on by default", pill on Chat sessions.

## D236 — A reply says the same live, settled and after a reload: how it ended, what it said, who wrote it
- Sources: the review of the live chat (findings on Stop, Copy, the failed turn and the header), `chat_live_view.js`, `chat_blocks.py`, `direct_turns.py`, `chats.py`, D212, D220.
- Decided: (1) a tool that never returned a result is `Interrupted` whenever the turn ended with Stop or an error, decided from the stored ending (a `Stopped` note or an error row beside the reply, or the note or error that is the reply), not only from an error row. (2) Text blocks that a tool or thinking block divides are joined with a blank line, in Copy and in the stored `text` (`join_text`); a provider text that is only the blocks run together is replaced by that form, a provider text that differs (a real final answer) is kept as it is. (3) A turn that failed before it wrote anything has no card of its own: its ending is the stored error line, and the tries the router gave up on are stored as a note ("Earlier try (Claude) failed: reason") beside it, so a reload says what the live page did. (4) A reply stores `{provider, model}` as a sibling key `via` of its meta (never a `MessageMeta` key), read back as the additive `ChatMessageOut.via`; the header is "via Claude model" live, at settle and after a reload, and plain "Sutra" for a message stored before. (5) An open code fence does not carry the newline of its last line, so its height does not change when the closing fence arrives.
- Why: the owner reads the reply later, in another tab or after a restart; what the page said while it ran must still be true then, and a tool that did not finish must never read Done.
- Rejected: storing the interrupted status per block on the server (the ending already says it and old messages have no such field); a second card for a failed turn that wrote nothing (it repeats the error line under a provider that never answered).
- Diagram: label "ending, text and author read the same after reload", pill on Chat sessions.

## D237 — The live turn's lock covers the feed, and the ticker waits only for work that is due
- Sources: `chat_port.py`, `live_turn.py`, `chat_blocks.py` (`guard`), D201, D204.
- Decided: `ChatPort.stream` holds the live turn's lock (`acc.guard`) from `acc.feed` until its consumer has handed the block events on, and lets go as soon as the generator resumes (never while it waits for the provider), so a Stop cannot freeze the accumulator between the two and what is stored is exactly what every tab was shown. The ticker adds a checkpoint deadline only when a checkpoint is due (a database, and a dirty state or `checkpoint_chars` unsaved characters); fewer characters wait for the next structural change.
- Why: the unlocked gap let stored text hold words no tab saw after a Stop, and a deadline that nothing could satisfy made the ticker spin at full speed during every quiet stretch of a reply, slowing the very stream it serves.
- Rejected: a second lock inside the accumulator (two orders to keep); a minimum sleep as the only cure (it hides the loop, the deadline is the bug).
- Diagram: label "feed and publish under one lock; ticker sleeps when nothing is due", pill on Chat sessions.

## D238 — The chat page keeps the owner's place: terminal frames, Stop, mode, width
- Sources: `chat_live_events.js`, `chat_live_store.js`, `chat_composer.js`, `chat_draft.js`, `home.js`, `sutra-panel.css`.
- Decided: a terminal frame held while a snapshot was read is applied before `snapshot.none` when the server says nothing runs, so a failed or stopped turn ends as that; a snapshot of a turn still running does not undo a Stop that was pressed (Stopping stays until `turn.stopping` or an ending); the Ask directly / Plan with Sutra choice is kept per chat beside the draft (`sutra:mode:<sid>`, 14 days, 30 chats, storage guarded) and wins over the page's default; the release of a stored reply repaints the bar and box in the same step as the reply, so the box never says Stop beside a settled reply; the `main` of `#sview` has a definite row (in `sutra-panel.css`; `j2-shell.css` is frozen) so the chat column reaches the bottom of the window between 901 and 1150 px; under 600 px the mode and provider toggles and the attach link are 40 px tall and the limits note is one line (its whole text is the link's title).
- Why: each was a place where the page told the owner something that had stopped being true, or made a control hard to hit.
- Rejected: persisting the mode on the server (a per-device convenience, like the draft); hiding the limits note on a phone (it stays in the page and is read aloud).
- Diagram: label "terminal frame kept; Stop stays; mode remembered", pill on band:EXPERIENCE.

## D239 — The pin moves to J2 main `dee83d5c` (sync 7): Disha's remove-restrictions (#202) and group C (#203)
- Sources: D176, D198, `prototypes/j2/docs/remove-restrictions-plan.md`, `prototypes/j2/docs/remove-group-c-plan.md`, `docs/design/j2-sync-7.md`.
- Decided: `scripts/vendor_j2.py` takes `BASELINE = dee83d5c565fe7d82631a4f7eb555543160a6d41` and tree `b9a14370c35a6309062821b549b311d662a8ed3d`; `1adb8ecd` joins `PREVIOUS_BASELINES`. The `artifacts/` folder is vendored as a tree (it gains `real_run.md`); the three plan documents of the two PRs join the reference docs; `fixtures/run-blockers/` is excluded as J2 test data read from the extraction. Re-anchored, intent unchanged: P1 (`j2_artifacts.py` has seven sibling imports), P7 (the agent call passes `tools` on), P8 (the import block), P17 (`create` takes the planner's `form`). No patch was dropped and none was added. The prompt and API goldens are re-recorded from J2 at the pin; the legacy flow test is a three-way merge (base 1adb8ecd, ours the sync 6 copy, theirs dee83d5c) with three conflicts, each resolved to J2's new text plus the engine port's existing edit.
- Why: the owner asked for the app to run Disha's latest engine; the pin is a fixed commit of J2 main.
- Rejected: waiting for a tag; vendoring the PR branches (their merge commits are in main).
- Diagram: label "J2 pin: main dee83d5c", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D240 — Builder tools stay off for a live run of the engine; J2's section path builds, J2's general path is carried inert
- Sources: D17, D170, J2 `llm.tools_on` and `ask(tools=...)` (remove-restrictions steps 1 and 2), J2's "no sandbox by default" (2026-11-15).
- Decided: Sutra's `engine/llm.py` gains J2's new surface: `TOOLS` (empty), `tools_on()` (False live; offline only with `J2_AGENT_TOOLS=1`, as J2's mock), `can_use_tools()` (False), `sandbox_on()` and `inside()` (J2's own), and `ask(..., tools=None)` (a live tools call raises; mock ignores tools as J2 does). So a live run takes J2's section path, as J2 does with `J2_AGENT_TOOLS=0`; folder outputs (the `agent` form), the planned real run (`RUN <code>`) and the builders' test rerun are not offered, because `j2_realization.type_list` drops a folder form when tools are off. The sandbox setting is J2's (off by default) and has nothing to confine while tools are off.
- Why: the router and the connectors have no seam for a tools call inside a run folder, and with J2's sandbox off by default a builder's command could change any file the desktop user can; giving builders tools is a product and safety decision for the owner, not a sync step.
- Rejected: shelling out to `claude -p` with tools from the engine (a second way to a model beside the connector layer, D17); turning tools on with no sandbox in the desktop app.
- Diagram: label "builder tools off live (section path)", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.
- Superseded by D245 (2026-11-17): builder tools are on in a live run with the Claude CLI.

## D241 — J2's forms are data; a folder form is allowed at registration, a new file form still stops it
- Sources: D188, J2 7f11f5de (the agent form as data; `j2_types.FORMS` removed), `engine/forms.py`.
- Decided: `forms.check_forms(j2_types)` reads `j2_types.forms()`: `page` and `document` must be there, and every other form must have `"output": "folder"` (`forms.FOLDER`), which J2 builds only with builder tools on (D240). A third form that makes one file still stops the engine at registration by name. The library's closed models learn J2's new reply and record fields (`item_kinds`, `exact_facts`, `date_rules`/`date_rule_items`, `email_updates`, J2.h question objects, `plan_option`, `about`, `ready_now`; Coordination's schedule has no `next`), and the charter stamp counts the form's `min_done_when` only, as J2's `_stamp_check` does. The capability rows of the Documents group carry J2's new Summary keys (`conditions`, `warnings`, `failed`, `failed_checks`, `retried`, `real_runs`, `template`, `type_label`), read with J2's own helpers.
- Why: J2 no longer names its forms in code ("J2 does whatever a prompt asks"); the check keeps its purpose (no form quietly treated as a page) without fighting a data-defined form.
- Rejected: dropping the check (a new file form would render as a page); listing `agent` by name (it is J2's data).
- Diagram: label "forms are data; folder forms pass", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D242 — A beta app is tried beside the stable app, and every AI agent is told so by AGENTS.md
- Sources: owner, 2026-11-16: add a protocol so that any AI provider that changes the repository knows a change can be tried by installing a beta locally, that a release is published on GitHub and then installed locally, that the beta is installed locally only, and that the beta app and the Sutra app can both be installed on one Mac; D100, D116, D119, D124, `docs/RELEASE.md`.
- Decided: the desktop app has two channels (`desktop/src/channels.json`, read by the window, the packager and `scripts/build_desktop_app.py`): the stable app (`Sutra Pro.app`, `os.sutra.pro`, data `~/.sutra-pro`, updates itself) and the beta (`Sutra Pro Beta.app`, `os.sutra.pro.beta`, data `~/.sutra-pro-beta`, own Chromium state and so its own single-instance lock, an icon with an orange BETA band, never updates itself). A build's channel is written into the package.json inside the app by the packager (`--channel beta`, a Mac only) and read from nowhere else: an environment variable cannot turn a stable app into a beta one, so one can never reach the other's data. `scripts/install_app.py beta` builds the wheel of the working tree, the app, signs it ad hoc, proves it starts (D120, throw-away data, no network), quits a running idle beta and installs it into `/Applications`, opens it and waits for `/api/health`; `install_app.py release [TAG]` downloads a published release, checks its checksum, replaces only the stable app and the `sutra-pro` command, and opens it; both refuse to quit an app that is working (exit status 3) and never use SIGKILL. `scripts/release.py` is the step list of `docs/RELEASE.md` as one command: version in the four files and `docs/release-notes/X.Y.Z.md`, a green `sutra-pro-ci` run on exactly the tagged commit, a fast-forward to `main`, the annotated tag, the three release runs, the assets, the notes in front of the release text, never Latest, then the install. `sutra-pro/AGENTS.md` carries the loop (change, test, try the beta, push, release only when asked) and the rules; `sutra-pro/CLAUDE.md` imports it and short pointers at the repository root (`AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `GEMINI.md`) send every tool there. `tests/test_agent_protocol.py` fails when a script or document the protocol names is gone.
- Why: the owner wants every change tried on the real machine before it is released, by whichever AI tool made it, without each session rediscovering the steps; the beta must not put an unreleased build, its schema changes or its test data into the app the owner uses every day, so it differs in every name a second copy could collide on and is never published; a release is the one thing that replaces the stable app, so it goes through the published, checksummed artifacts and not through a local build.
- Rejected: a beta that shares `~/.sutra-pro` (an unreleased build could migrate or damage the owner's real data; there is no undo); a channel chosen by an environment variable or a menu (one stray variable would point the stable app at beta data); publishing betas as GitHub pre-releases for this (`-beta.N` tags stay what `docs/RELEASE.md` says, a check of the release workflows, and the owner asked for local only); installing the stable app from a local build (it would not be what a download gives); one shell script (the version arithmetic, the checks and the order of the steps are what the tests cover, and they are Python); a hook or a plugin per AI tool (a Markdown file every tool already reads is one place to keep true).
- Diagram: label "beta beside stable; AGENTS.md protocol", pill on Desktop app · Electron window.

## D243 — The Windows app carries its own icon, the woven lotus; the Mac keeps the old Sutra's
- Sources: owner, 2026-11-17 (the Windows build of Sutra Pro uses the refined blue woven-lotus icon; the macOS icon stays as it is); D117, D242.
- Decided: `assets/icon/sutra-pro-windows.ico` (16, 24, 32, 48, 64, 128 and 256 px) and `assets/icon/sutra-pro-windows-1024.png` are hand-made artwork, committed, and are what the Windows exe (the packager's `icon`), the installer (`/DIconFile`, `SetupIconFile`), the shortcuts and the window (`extraResource`, `src/main.js` `ICON_NAME` on win32) carry. Each channel names them in `desktop/src/channels.json` (`ico`, `windowsIconName`) beside its Mac files, so the packager and the window read one place; the beta names the same files, since a beta is a Mac app (D242) and no Windows beta is built. `scripts/make_icons.py` lists them as `WINDOWS_ICON_FILES`, never derives, writes or checks them, and refuses to write them; `tests/release/test_icons.py` pins their sizes and that a regeneration leaves them alone. The derived `sutra-pro.ico` stays committed for the record.
- Why: the owner wants the Windows app recognisable as its own on a Windows desktop, where the black rounded square reads as a generic tile at small sizes; the Mac app keeps the old product's logo (D117) because the two apps replace each other in place there.
- Rejected: deriving the Windows icon from the old logo with a colour change (it is new artwork, not a derivation, and `make_icons.py` would overwrite it); a Windows icon for the beta (a beta is never built for Windows); renaming the files after the artwork (the names are what the packager, the installer scripts and the release workflow reference; the artwork can change under them).
- Diagram: label "Windows icon: woven lotus; Mac keeps old logo", pill on Desktop app · Electron window.

## D244 — The pin moves to J2 main `ee659fb5` (sync 8): Kaivan's reuse fixes (#205) and the dry-run fix (#206)
- Sources: D239, D240, `prototypes/j2/docs/remove-restrictions-plan.md` ("Dry run on the real folders"), `docs/design/j2-sync-8.md`.
- Decided: `scripts/vendor_j2.py` takes `BASELINE = ee659fb54aec75bf7f5139516791aee20a0474fa` and tree `ddc9a7875b4e4d0d2d6af1d8f8764e1f0ddd2d37`; `dee83d5c` joins `PREVIOUS_BASELINES`. Every patch applies unchanged (none re-anchored, none dropped). The page is unchanged: J2's page at the pin equals `133942b1`, which sync 7 ported. The reuse changes are carried inert behind J2's library flags; builder tools stay off (D240).
- Why: the owner approved lifting D240 (builder tools in live runs); that must not come before the engine has #206, which keeps a dry run off the Requester's real folders (`runs/j2-20261117-114654` wrote into a real folder before approval). The sync goes first and alone, so the app is checked working as before with tools still off.
- Rejected: carrying only #206 as a new patch (a large patch on `j2.py`, against "patches are small, located by anchor"); lifting D240 in the same change (one review would mix an engine sync with a safety switch).
- Diagram: label "J2 pin: main ee659fb5", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D245 — Builder tools are on in a live run with the Claude CLI; the sandbox is on for macOS and Linux when srt is found (supersedes D240)
- Sources: the owner (2026-11-17: Sankalp approved lifting D240, builders get tools in live runs); D240; D244 (sync 8 brings J2's dry-run fix #206); J2 `llm.ask(tools=...)`, `cli_settings`, `_tool_meta`; D127 (the per-call folder grant this follows).
- Decided: a J2 tools request becomes a `ToolGrant` on `CompletionRequest.run_tools` (the grant's tools, permission rules, folder inside the run, cost limit, log size, and the sandbox choice). The router plans it only on a connector whose capabilities say `run_tools`, which is the Claude CLI alone, so a tools call never falls back to Codex: with the Claude CLI missing or signed out it fails, and Codex runs nothing. It is tried once, as J2 runs it (a retry would start over on the files the first try wrote). The Claude connector runs it with J2's own command line (`--tools`, `--allowedTools`, `--restricted`, `--safe-mode`, `dontAsk`, `--settings` with the CLI sandbox when on, `--max-budget-usd`) in the builder's folder, and reads every tool call back in J2's `_tool_meta` shape (`CompletionResult.tool_record`, then `LAST_META`). The shim's `tools_on()` is true live when the port has a provider that can run the call (`RouterPort.tools_provider`), unless `J2_AGENT_TOOLS=0`; otherwise J2 takes its section path, and the startup line says why ("Codex has no J2 tools path"). The sandbox (`llm.sandbox_status`): `J2_SANDBOX` decides when set; unset and live, on for macOS and Linux when the sandbox runtime (`J2_SANDBOX_CMD`, default `srt`) is found on PATH, else off with the install hint; off on Windows; offline it stays J2's default (off). Two locked engine settings force either (`agent_tools`, `sandbox`: `J2_AGENT_TOOLS`, `J2_SANDBOX`, from `engine.json` or the server's environment; unset leaves the rule), since a run's engine reads its settings and not the server's own variables. One startup line says both, for example "Builder tools: on (the Claude CLI). Sandbox: on (srt at …)". The library accepts the general path's records: a call log's `tools` record and the real-run receipt (bounded open JSON, as the send record).
- Why: Sankalp approved builders with tools in live runs. The engine now has #206 (D244): a real run passes its folders as arguments, a build that holds a real folder is refused, and the real folders are read before and after the dry run. The sandbox needs the runtime J2's test rerun and real run call (`srt`), which works on macOS and Linux and is not bundled, so it is on only where it can run; elsewhere J2's permission rules, `--restricted`, the run folder's before/after check and the `RUN <code>` approval still hold.
- Rejected: a fallback to Codex for a tools call (Codex has no J2 tools path, and its own sandbox has other rules); a Codex tools path in this change; the sandbox on whenever the platform allows it (the test rerun and the real run would fail without `srt`); shelling out to `claude -p` from the engine (a second way to a model beside the connector layer, D17).
- Follow-up: bundle `srt` with the app, so the sandbox is on for every macOS and Linux install without a separate `npm i -g @anthropic-ai/sandbox-runtime`.
- Diagram: label "builder tools on live (Claude CLI); sandbox where srt is", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D246 — Patch P22: the workflow without a real run that covers a real run's item is refused, and the same validator rejection twice in a row stops the cycle
- Sources: the owner (2026-11-18), on beta run j2-20261118-110800-0c75 (software-engineering): the validator refused "Build sort_files.py" four times, saying done-when item 3 must leave its covers, while item 3 was covered by W-3 "Write run_on_sort_me.md" and not by the script; D13 (the vendored engine is changed only by numbered patches); J2 step 7, the real run (`real_run.checks`).
- Decided: P22 in `_register`: (1) an item a proposal's real run checks is decided by the run's receipt, so a proposal without a real run that covers it is refused ("it covers done-when item(s) 3, which the real run of 'Build sort_files.py' checks"), and its covers no longer count against the real-run workflow, which registers; (2) when the real-run workflow is refused because another workflow covers its item, the message names that workflow and says the conflict is there (`_cover_conflict`): one registered or held is to be superseded (its id in `replaces`) or replaced by a workflow that doesn't cover the item, and a waiting workflow a proposal of the same batch supersedes no longer counts against the real run, so following that advice registers it; one running or done can't be superseded (`_supersede` takes only registered or held), so the message says the item can't be met by that real run in this run and no new rule is added for it: the repeat stop in (3) hands it to the Requester; the old message stays when the real-run workflow covers the item itself; (3) a rejection the validator's last call also made, same workflow and same reason, is a repeated failure: `_agree` stops the cycle with an Identity limit ("The same failure came back twice in a row", `_repeat_stop`), and when it registered something, "keep going" resumes where the validator was going (Coordination, or Adaptation for a plan gap).
- Why: the real-run workflow was the right one and the conflict was elsewhere, but the message sent Adaptation to change the script's covers, which held no item 3, so it proposed the same thing four times and the cycle never stopped.
- Rejected: dropping item 3 from W-3's covers by code (code validates, agents decide); superseding a done workflow (it was built and charged; not a rule for this patch); counting only identical whole batches as a repeat (the run's repeats came beside new work).
- Diagram: label "P22: real-run cover conflict; repeated rejection stops", pill on J2 chips.

## D247 — A run counts as ended workable only when it ended clean ("Done:"); a run with open items is never saved or reused (patch P23)
- Sources: review of J2 `cac601c1` (Kaivan's auto-save of a run template, `j2.py` `_save_run_template`), 2026-11-17; D239 (folder reuse from a source run that ended workable); D244 (sync 8 vendors the auto-save); J2 `workable()`.
- Decided: patch P23 (`scripts/vendor_j2.py`) makes J2's `ended_workable(run_dir)` true only when `summary.json`'s outcome starts "Done:" (never "Done with open items") and the last audit passes `workable()` with `accepted` and `after_real_run` emptied: no item the Requester accepted as it is, and no item still waiting for a real run, counts as passing. It covers the three callers of `ended_workable`: `_save_run_template` (a run that ends is saved as a run template), `_reuse_folder` (a run template's folder is copied from its source run) and the content door (`_route_content`: a source run that did not end workable is not served).
- Why: J2's `workable()` takes accepted items and items waiting for a real run as passing, so a run that ended "Done with open items" (finished as it is, or a planned real run that never ran or did not pass) counted as workable. Such a run must not become a run template, have its files copied into a new run, or have its sections served: the next run would start from work that was never finished.
- Rejected: checking the outcome only at `_save_run_template` (the same hole stays in `_reuse_folder` and the content door); changing `workable()` itself (it decides whether a running run may ask for its real run, where pending items must count).
- Diagram: label "ended workable = clean Done: only (P23)", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D248 — A trusted certificate may end a TLS chain (OpenSSL 3.5 on Windows)
- Sources: the owner's Windows copy of 0.6.1 on 2026-11-19: About said "Could not reach GitHub" on every click, while curl and the system Python reached it; `desktop.log` 08:07Z and 08:27Z.
- Decided: the update check's own TLS context, `ops/update_check.py` `tls_context()` (handed to its `urlopen`), sets `ssl.VERIFY_X509_PARTIAL_CHAIN` on `ssl.create_default_context()`. The system trust store stays the only trust, the chain is still verified and the host name still checked; the flag only lets a trusted certificate that is not self-signed end the chain. `tests/ops/test_update_check.py` pins the flag and that the fetcher hands the context to `urlopen`. The STARTTLS context of `engine/j2_send.py` has the same exposure and is left alone here: it is vendored J2 code (D13), so that change is made in `prototypes/j2/j2_send.py` and arrives with the next sync.
- Why: GitHub's chain since late October 2026 ends in the Sectigo Public Server Authentication Root E46, cross-signed by the USERTrust ECC root. The owner's Windows store holds that USERTrust name twice: the self-signed root and a copy cross-signed by AAA Certificate Services, which the store lacks. The bundled OpenSSL 3.5.8 picks one by its own order, per process: in 4 of 10 processes it walks into the cross-signed copy, cannot find AAA, and reports `unable to get issuer certificate` for every request of that process's life, so the running server failed every click until a restart. OpenSSL 3.0 (the owner's system Python) never does. With the flag the copy it finds is trusted and the chain ends there.
- Rejected: shipping `certifi` (a second trust store beside the owner's, to keep current, and one more file in the bundle; verified in the failing processes too, but wider than needed); loading only the ROOT store (also verified, but drops intermediates the owner's organisation may have trusted on purpose); telling the owner to restart (a 60 percent chance each time, no fix).
- Diagram: badge on Desktop app · Electron window.

## D249 — The engine's prompts may differ from J2 through recorded patches; the prompt goldens are the engine's (amends D13)
- Sources: D13 (every change to a J2 file is a numbered patch; prompts stay byte-identical); ENG-01 (`tests/engine/test_engine_prompts.py`); J2 frozen at `cut-off/2026-11-17`; the owner's decision of 2026-11-19 on P24 and P25.
- Decided: J2 is frozen and the app is the product, so a recorded patch may change what the engine asks the model. The rest of D13 stands: every change is a numbered patch in `scripts/vendor_j2.py`, and `vendor_j2.py --check` re-derives the vendored files from the pinned extraction. A patch that changes a prompt says so in its VENDORED.md note (P24: Priority's reply format; P25: the raise question's text). The seven prompt goldens are recorded from the engine with its patches applied (`scripts/record_engine_golden.py --engine --out-dir tests/engine/golden`, `j2_commit` = the pin), and J2's own goldens in the legacy test copy (`DOC_RUN_GOLDEN`) are re-recorded with a note naming the patch, as J2 itself did for its intended text changes. Re-recording is on purpose, never to pass: outcomes, spend, call order, workflows and documents must stay equal unless the patch's decision says why they move.
- Why: ENG-01 pinned the engine to J2's exact prompts, which made any fix to an agent's contract impossible without first changing a frozen prototype (P24 needs Priority to send only an id and an envelope).
- Rejected: a host switch with the patches off by default (the goldens would test a mode the app never runs); changing `prototypes/j2` and syncing (J2 is frozen; outside `sutra-pro/`).
- Diagram: label "prompts may differ from J2 via patches (amends D13)", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D250 — Priority's move is a workflow's id and its envelope; the validator charges the money limit or rejects the move with a reason (patch P24)
- Sources: beta run j2-20261118-090053-4a9c (people-hr): Priority COUNTERed three fixes from 24 to 18 (6, 7, 5) and also added F-10 to their `fixes`; the validator logged "Priority's or the ruling's version dropped or changed atoms, estimated_resources, fixes; kept as Adaptation proposed it" as an autofix (chat 33 to 35, `autofix.json`). The owner's rule: the validator accepts or rejects and never rewrites a move.
- Decided: patch P24. Both pricing turns of Priority ask for `[{id, envelope}]` (`PRICE_FMT`). `_carry` registers Adaptation's proposal (the table Priority was shown, then the proposals, by id then name) plus the move's envelope. A field the move sends with another value than Adaptation proposed (fixes, atoms, sections, estimated_resources and so on) is not taken; the validator posts Adaptation a visible note naming the field and the value, and nothing is logged as a "carried" autofix. A field the move leaves out is not part of it. `estimated_resources` stays Adaptation's estimate. `granted` charges `envelope.money_limit`. A COUNTER without a money limit is rejected ("a counter must state its money limit"). With the rate card on, the rate-card price is a floor: a limit below it is rejected with the reason, never raised to it. J2's mock Priority sends `{id, name, envelope}`; its cycle-1 COUNTER now sets the animations' limit to 10 and the mock Adaptation revises their parts to `interactive_feature`, so the Brew Lab runs keep their spend and calls.
- Why: in the run the money charged (18) matched the counter only because `granted` read the envelope first; Priority's change to `estimated_resources`, `fixes` and `atoms` was undone in silence, and with the rate card on a counter's price was replaced by the card's.
- Rejected: rejecting a move that also changes a planning field (it would have rejected all three funded fixes of the run over F-10); adding `estimated_resources` to what Priority owns (it is Adaptation's estimate); keeping the merge with a better log line (still a rewrite).
- Diagram: label "Priority's move = id + envelope (P24)", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D251 — The budget cap only when J2_BUDGET_CAP is set; every raise is the Requester's own; a Budget field on the start page (patch P25)
- Sources: the owner's direction of 2026-11-19 (part 2(b), the budget changes); J2's `BUDGET_CAP` (twice the budget when unset); J2's Decide for me (`_auto_choice` answers an approval or a ruling_budget question "Yes, approve it." and a limit question with its first option, which adds credits); `TaskSettings.J2_BUDGET` and `EngineSettings.budget` (1 to 100000).
- Decided: patch P25 (`scripts/vendor_j2.py`, prompt-changing per D249: the raise question's text). `BUDGET_CAP` comes from `J2_BUDGET_CAP` only when it is set; unset means no cap, so an approval adds what the Requester approved and a raise option says "Or type your own number" without "up to N". Every raise needs the Requester's own approval: Decide for me waits instead of answering an approval, a ruling_budget question or a limit option that adds credits (what the Simple view suggests is unchanged). A question that asks for more credits ends with the credits approved so far ("So far: 100 + 30 = 130 work credits approved."). The start page gets one optional field, "Budget (work credits)", 100 by default (port edit E25; D252 makes it empty by default, "team default", and adds it to the home screen): `POST /api/say` takes `budget`, checks it as a whole number within `EngineSettings.budget`'s limits (400 otherwise) and makes it this delivery's task setting `J2_BUDGET`; the page keeps nothing of it. There is no cap field on the page; the team's Settings keep their Budget cap ("empty means no cap").
- Why: a cap of twice the start budget stopped approved work for no reason the owner chose; Decide for me could spend money nobody approved; the Requester saw each raise alone, not the total they had committed.
- Rejected: a default cap (the owner wants none unless set); Decide for me choosing a non-raise option instead (it would decide scope for the Requester where they were asked about money); a cap field on the start page.
- Diagram: label "budget cap only if set; raises need the Requester (P25)", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D252 — The Budget (work credits) field is on the home screen too, and on both pages starts empty: "team default" (amends D251)
- Sources: the owner's direction of 2026-11-19: most requests start on the home screen (home.html, front_door.js), which had no Budget field; D251 (the field on a team's page, 100 by default).
- Decided: the home screen's request box gets the same optional "Budget (work credits)" field (`#fdbudget`). On both pages the field starts empty with the placeholder "team default": empty sends no budget, so the run takes the team's Settings budget (100 if none is set); a typed number is checked as a whole number from 1 to 100000 (`FrontDoor.budgetOf` on the home screen, `lbudget` on a team page, port edit E25) and a bad one stops the send with a sentence on the field. The home box sends `budget` with its new request in `POST /api/chats/cs_org/messages` (`ChatPost.budget`, 1 to 100000, 422 otherwise; refused in a team chat and with a card answer). The chat agent's runner keeps it with the owner's line (`chat_messages.meta_json.budget`) and every task that request makes, also after a team pick or an approval, gets it as the task setting `J2_BUDGET`.
- Why: a field that always sent 100 overrode a team's own Settings budget without the owner choosing to; and the home screen, where most requests start, could not set a budget at all.
- Rejected: prefilling the team's Settings budget (the home screen has no team until the chat agent picks one); a new column for the budget (the owner's line already carries `meta_json`, as `via` and `blocks` do).
- Diagram: label "Budget field on home + team page, empty = team default", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D253 — A node is an ordinary run plus an identity row
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: A node is an ordinary run (a `tasks` row, a run folder, a process) plus an identity row in `agent_nodes`. No new process model. Built in package MAN-1, MAN-5.
- Why: a run already has files in and out, a heartbeat, a stop ladder, recovery and cost by task, so the cheapest way to get nodes is to give that thing an identity.
- Rejected: threads or tasks inside the server (J2's configuration is per process, there is no OS isolation and no kill without taking the server down); splitting J2's five functions into five processes (they share one `J2Run`, and it is a rewrite of a frozen engine); a broker with remote workers (a new service, and it breaks local-first).
- Diagram: label "node = a run + an identity row", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D254 — Everything is behind SUTRA_NODES_V1, default 0
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Everything is behind `SUTRA_NODES_V1`, default 0. With it off no new table is read, no new column is used, and `task.json` is byte-identical to today. Sub-flags `SUTRA_NODES_LANES`, `SUTRA_NODES_REVIEWER`, `SUTRA_NODES_SLOTS` also default 0 and only count when the master is on. Built in package MAN-1.
- Why: the owner wants today's behaviour kept and every change additive, and a flag is the way back.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "SUTRA_NODES_V1, default 0; off = today", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D255 — A project groups the tasks of one request
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: A project is a new row that groups the tasks of one request. It is made only when the flag is on and the plan has two or more steps, or when the owner starts one with `POST /api/projects`. A one-step request keeps today's path. Built in package MAN-5.
- Why: one budget, one stop and one teardown for the nodes of a request, without touching the simple case.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "project groups one request's nodes", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D256 — Roles and runtimes: planner inline, worker is a J2 run, reviewer is light
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Roles and runtimes: planner inline (today's org chat agent stage), worker on the engine runtime (an unchanged J2 run), reviewer on a new light runtime, Conductor and Gatekeeper as server code. J2's five functions are not split into nodes. Built in package MAN-5, MAN-11.
- Why: the engine is frozen and its functions share one `J2Run`, so a team run is the smallest honest worker.
- Rejected: splitting J2's five functions into five nodes (a rewrite of a frozen engine, D13 and D249).
- Diagram: label "planner inline, worker = J2 run, reviewer light", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D257 — One scheduler: the Conductor is a task gate, not a second queue
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: One scheduler. The Conductor is a task gate inside `_prepare_next` (next to `start_gate`) plus a tick, not a second queue. Built in package MAN-5.
- Why: FIFO, `SUTRA_MAX_RUNS`, `SUTRA_MAX_LIVE`, the spending limit and "waiting" sentences then apply to nodes for free.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "one scheduler; Conductor is a task gate", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D258 — Same-team parallelism uses a lane column and a (team, lane) unique index
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Same-team parallelism uses a `lane` column on `tasks`. The unique index becomes `(dept_id, lane)` for live statuses. Every ordinary task has lane `''`; `live_in`, `deliver_to_department` and `stop_live` see lane `''` only. Built in package MAN-1 (column), MAN-10 (use).
- Why: it widens the "one live task per team" rule only for nodes and leaves the rule for everything else exactly as it is.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "lane column; (team, lane) unique", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D259 — Plan edges plus conflict edges; a conflict edge only delays
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Parallelism comes from plan edges (`depends_on`, as today) plus conflict edges the Gatekeeper derives from write scopes. A conflict edge delays a step; it never cancels it. Built in package MAN-5, MAN-10.
- Why: a step that only waited for a folder must not die because a sibling failed.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "plan edges + conflict edges (delay only)", key only.

## D260 — Write scopes are exclusive leases; the planner may only narrow
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Write scopes are exclusive leases on paths. The planner may only narrow a scope, never widen it. No declared scope means the whole folder, so that writer runs alone. A folder that is not a git work tree means writers run one at a time. Built in package MAN-10.
- Why: the safe default is serial, and the after-run check needs git.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "write scopes: exclusive leases; narrow only", key only.

## D261 — No git worktrees in v1: scoped leases and a path check
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: No git worktrees in v1. Parallel writers share the owner's folder, fenced by scope rules in the CLI, with a path-attribution check after each run. A path changed that no running node owns pauses the project and asks the owner. Built in package MAN-10.
- Why: worktrees need a merge step and a merge policy; scoped leases are simpler and the check catches what the fence misses.
- Rejected: a git worktree per node (it needs a merge step and a merge policy).
- Diagram: label "no git worktrees; scope fence + path check", key only.

## D262 — The server is the only database writer; nodes write files
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The server stays the only database writer (D16 kept). Nodes never open the database and never call the server. They write files in their own run folder. Built in package all.
- Why: it keeps SQLite safe and leaves nothing for a node to authenticate to or steal.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "server is the only DB writer; nodes use files", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D263 — Hand-off, verdict and needs are files; the eight run events stay closed
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Structured payloads (`handoff.json`, `verdict.json`, `needs.json`) are files in the node's run folder. No new run event type is added; the eight stay closed. Built in package MAN-6, MAN-11.
- Why: it extends D133 ("a published file, not a ninth run event") and keeps the engine event contract and its goldens unchanged.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "handoff/verdict/needs are files; eight events stay", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D264 — Messages between nodes: an append-only table with a gap-free seq per project
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Messages between nodes live in `node_messages`: append-only, written only by the server, gap-free `seq` per project (how: D297), `src_ref` unique per project. Built in package MAN-6.
- Why: the same de-duplication pattern as `chat_messages.src_ref` and `usage_ledger.call_ref`, and the same catch-up pattern as the streaming snapshot.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "node_messages: append-only, gap-free seq", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D265 — Upstream data reaches a node through the upstream folder, never through framing
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Upstream data reaches a node through the existing `upstream` folder mechanism (checked paths) plus the validated hand-off, and never through `framing` or system text. Built in package MAN-6.
- Why: `framing` is context from the chat agent, and mixing node output into instructions is how injection works.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "upstream via folder + hand-off, never framing", key only.

## D266 — The Gatekeeper is deterministic code: a model never approves a model
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The Gatekeeper is deterministic code. A model never approves a model. Only a rule or the owner says yes. Built in package MAN-2, MAN-8.
- Why: an approval a model can give is an approval a prompt can steal.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "Gatekeeper is code; a model never approves", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D267 — A grant is an intersection, frozen at spawn
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: A grant is the intersection of the role table, the plan step, the department's output spec and the owner's folder access (the owner's access caps the folder part of the grant only: D298). It is frozen when the node spawns. A node cannot raise it. A node that lacks a tier ends with `needs.json`; that becomes a card and, if allowed, a new attempt. Built in package MAN-2, MAN-8.
- Why: no process waits alive for a human, and content can never change a permission.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "grant = intersection, frozen at spawn", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D268 — Six tiers by blast radius
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Six tiers by blast radius: 1 read, 2 write files, 3 run commands, 4 network, 5 spend, 6 external publish; tier 0 is thinking only. Built in package MAN-2.
- Why: the owner named these six, and each maps onto a control the connectors already have.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "six tiers by blast radius", key only.

## D269 — Deny by default; tier 6 is never granted
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Deny by default. Defaults give tiers 0 to 2 where the role, the output spec and the owner's access allow. Tier 3 is only the four git reads (amended by D299: J2's builder tools are not a tier). Tier 4 needs the owner and does not depend on the folder (D298). Tier 5 is a slice, never extra. Tier 6 is never granted in v1. Built in package MAN-2.
- Why: today's apps cannot send for real (`J2_SEND_MODE` defaults to `dry-run` and is not a task setting) and the design must not open that.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "deny by default; tier 6 never granted", key only.

## D270 — Approvals at three gates, stored as rows, expiring after 24 hours
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Approvals: asked at the plan gate, after a `needs.json` end, and for budget raises. Stored as rows. "Allow for this project" only up to tier 4; tier 5 is per raise; tier 6 is not offered. They expire after 24 hours and expired means denied. With the project's Decide for me switch on, the Gatekeeper's rule table answers them instead (D294); the owner's own answer is still the only other thing that can. Built in package MAN-8.
- Why: three places are enough to protect the owner without wearing them out, and the answer must be the owner's own (P25 principle).
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "approvals at three gates; 24 h; expired = denied", key only.

## D271 — Project credits are the sum of the node slices
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Project credits: the ceiling is the sum of the node slices (so total spend equals today's separate tasks). A node's `J2_BUDGET` and `J2_BUDGET_CAP` both equal its slice, so it cannot raise itself. A spawn that does not fit the unspent slices is held. Built in package MAN-9. (Amended by D320: a slice is final in v1, a retry has the same slice so the ceiling counts each attempt, and the fit check is a guard.)
- Why: P25 says every raise is the Requester's own, and the sum of slices makes the project ceiling true by construction.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
