- Diagram: label "budget slices; sum never over the ceiling", key only.

## D272 — A NodeMeter makes the router's allowance cumulative per node
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: A `NodeMeter` in Sutra's own `engine/llm.py` shim makes the router's per-request `Allowance` cumulative per node: calls, dollars where known, and a wall-time deadline. Past a cap the host stops the run and the outcome names the limit. Built in package MAN-9.
- Why: the allowance exists and is enforced by the router but the engine never sent one (`allowance=None`); Codex has no dollar figure, so calls and time are its bound.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "NodeMeter: cumulative calls, dollars, time", pill on Connector router.

## D273 — Default caps for projects and nodes
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Default caps: 3 parallel nodes per project, 12 workers, 60 calls and 30 minutes per node, 1 retry per node, 2 review loops, 2 hours per project, lease 90 s, approval expiry 24 h, spawn depth 1, and the plan gate's confirm threshold of 500 work credits (D300). All in `caps_json` and `config.py`. Built in package MAN-1, MAN-9. (Amended by D318 and D321: the confirm threshold is 400, a new cap `project_credits` is 1000, and the 60 calls and 30 minutes are to be measured (P4).)
- Why: they are in line with today's plan limits (12 steps, 3 runs) and are easy to raise later because they are data.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "default caps: 3 parallel, 60 calls, 30 min", key only.

## D274 — Nodes never spawn nodes (depth 1)
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Nodes never spawn nodes (depth 1). Only the Conductor spawns, from the accepted plan. A changed plan goes through the plan gate again. Built in package MAN-5.
- Why: this is the loop guard; with no code path for a node to spawn a node, no text can make one.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "nodes never spawn nodes (depth 1)", key only.

## D275 — Leases: 90 seconds, a silent node is stopped and retried once
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: A lease is `last_beat_at + 90 s`, taken inside the transaction that marks the task `starting`. Any event extends it. In project mode only, a node silent past its lease and still alive is stopped with the ladder and retried once. If the Conductor's own tick gap shows the machine slept, the lease is extended once instead. Built in package MAN-7.
- Why: today a hung run is never recovered while the app runs (a deliberate choice for sleeping laptops); the sleep check keeps that kindness and fixes hangs.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "lease 90 s; silent node stopped, retried once", key only.

## D276 — Teardown keeps the run folder and releases everything else
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Teardown after a node ends: stop the process if needed, sweep its temp files (`choices.json`, MCP config), release scope and slot, write the audit, keep the run folder. Project teardown writes `audit.jsonl` and a receipt. Built in package MAN-5, MAN-7.
- Why: nothing the owner could want is deleted, and nothing stays held.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "teardown keeps run folders", key only.

## D277 — Recovery: reconcile first, then reconcile_projects
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Recovery: `reconcile` runs first and unchanged; `reconcile_projects` then rebuilds leases from rows, re-adopts live nodes, and retries or fails the rest. Leases are rows, not memory. Built in package MAN-7.
- Why: a crash must lose nothing and double-start nothing.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "reconcile, then reconcile_projects", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D278 — Failure policy per project, never a silent done
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Failure policy per project, default `stop_branch` (a failed node cancels its dependants, siblings go on, as a failed task does today); `stop_all` is the other choice. A project with a failed node ends `done_with_open_items` or `stopped`, never `done`. Built in package MAN-7.
- Why: it is today's rule for tasks and it never claims success it did not have.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "failure policy stop_branch; never a silent done", key only.

## D279 — Kill switch in four levels, and a file that works when the UI does not
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Kill switch in four levels (node, project, pause all, kill all) using the one stop ladder; a file `<data>/nodes/KILL` works when the UI does not; automatic triggers on caps, scope violation, repeated egress hits, repeated lease loss and the monthly limit. Built in package MAN-4 for the four levels and the file; the automatic triggers are built with their causes (D303).
- Why: the owner must be able to stop everything by hand, and a stuck UI is exactly when that is needed.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "kill switch: 4 levels + KILL file", pill on Operator.

## D280 — The audit is an append-only table of names and shapes
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The audit is an append-only table `node_audit` (update and delete triggers), one row per decision or action, names and shapes and hashes only, exported to `<data>/projects/<id>/audit.jsonl` (0600) at close. Built in package MAN-2, MAN-14.
- Why: it copies `atom_ledger` and `egress.jsonl`, so the rule "never a payload" stays true.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "append-only node_audit, no payloads", key only.

## D281 — Prompt-injection boundary: what a node reads is data
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Prompt-injection boundary: hand-off and read text is data in a labelled block in the user part; hand-offs are typed JSON; only enums touch control flow; no content can change a grant, an edge or a budget; paths are checked; the reviewer is read-only. Built in package MAN-6, MAN-11, and tests in MAN-2.
- Why: a fooled model should only be able to do what its grant allows and show words to a human.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "injection boundary: hand-off is data", key only.

## D282 — Egress scrub per node: observe for engine nodes, replace where the app owns the text
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Egress per node: `observe` for engine nodes (J2 prompts stay byte-identical), `replace` for hand-offs, stored messages and light-node prompts. Audit rows carry node, project and role. Three secret-shaped hits by one node stop it. Built in package MAN-3, MAN-6.
- Why: it keeps D109 and D249 and adds scrub exactly where the app owns the text.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "egress per node: observe / replace", pill on Connector router.

## D283 — Node identity travels in the tag, task.json and the environment
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Node identity is an optional `node_id` and `node_role` on `CallTag` and an optional `node` block in `task.json`, plus `SUTRA_NODE_ID` in the child environment. Empty by default; the block is left out of the dump when the flag is off. Built in package MAN-3.
- Why: usage, egress and allowance rows then carry the node with no new column, and `TaskSpec` already ignores unknown keys.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "node identity in CallTag, task.json, env", pill on Connector router.

## D284 — Provider slots shared by the whole machine
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Provider concurrency becomes machine-wide with file-lock slot files (`oslayer.try_lock`), one slot file per allowed process, shared by every router. Sub-flag `SUTRA_NODES_SLOTS`. Built in package MAN-12.
- Why: the limit is per `Router` and each engine process has its own, so N processes multiply it; a file lock also vanishes when the process dies.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "machine-wide provider slots (file locks)", pill on Connector router.

## D285 — Reviewer independence across processes
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Reviewer independence across processes: the Conductor records which provider served each worker and writes the reviewer's run pick (`choices.json`, existing agent picks) to the other provider; if the other is unavailable the review still runs and says it was not independent. Built in package MAN-11.
- Why: `Independence` is in one process's memory and cannot see a worker in another process.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "reviewer independence across processes", pill on Connector router.

## D286 — The reviewer is a read-only light node with a verdict enum
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The reviewer is a read-only light node with a verdict enum (`approve`, `changes_requested`, `reject`) and at most 2 review loops; then the owner decides. Built in package MAN-11.
- Why: an independent reader that cannot write cannot be turned into a writer, and a loop cap ends ping-pong.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "reviewer: read-only, verdict enum, 2 loops", key only.

## D287 — Project and node changes are ordinary store events
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Project and node changes are ordinary store events (`project.updated`, `node.updated`, `approval.opened`, `approval.decided`, `project.message`) on the existing bus. The page refetches the snapshot. Heartbeat-only batches touch the database at most once per 15 s. Built in package MAN-6, MAN-12.
- Why: it reuses the bus ring and replay and keeps write pressure down; the replay of a few heartbeat lines is harmless.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "project events on the bus; heartbeats coalesced", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D288 — SQLite, WAL and one writer stay; a load test decides
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: SQLite, WAL and one writer stay. The change is an additive migration v6. A 12-node load test is the acceptance test. If it fails the next step is a 250 ms batch window per project, not a new database. Built in package MAN-1, MAN-12.
- Why: the numbers are guessed, not measured, so measure first and add the smallest fix.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "SQLite stays; the load test decides", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D289 — Nodes are local child processes only
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Nodes are local child processes only. Remote nodes are a non-goal; the protocol stays files, so it remains possible later.
- Why: local-first is an owner rule and a broker would be a new service and dependency.
- Rejected: a broker and remote workers (a new service and dependency, and local-first is an owner rule).
- Diagram: label "nodes are local processes only", key only.

## D290 — Words: runtime node and graph node are kept apart
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Words: "runtime node" (a running agent) is kept apart from "graph node" (a box in the graph). The table is `agent_nodes`. The UI says "agent". The engine's `project` output type is unrelated. Built in package MAN-1.
- Why: the word node already means three things in this repo, and a clear name saves mistakes.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "'runtime node' vs 'graph node'", key only.

## D291 — The planner is the chat agent's staged planning with an identity
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The planner is the org chat agent's staged planning with a node identity and a small slice. It may add a write scope and a `needs` list per step, only narrower than the department's. The planner call gets a `CancelToken` if `call_json` has none today (it had none; how it must stop a running call: D301). Built in package MAN-3, MAN-5.
- Why: it keeps the planning flow as it is and makes it auditable and stoppable.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "planner = chat agent with identity and slice", pill on Chat agent (orchestrator).

## D292 — Tests use the fake engine and the scripted connector
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Tests use the fake engine and the scripted connector; the gate never calls a model; `live_smoke.py` is not run. Each package is tried in the beta app. Releases, packaging and version files are not touched. Built in package all.
- Why: `AGENTS.md` forbids spending the owner's quota in tests and reserves the stable app and releases for the owner.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "fake engine tests; no live smoke", key only.

## D293 — The decisions and the flag table are registered in the first package
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The decisions, the design documents and the flag table are registered in `docs/DECISIONS.md`, `docs/diagram/decisions.json`, `docs/design/traceability.json`, `docs/ENVIRONMENT.md` and `sutra_app/ops/envfile.py` in MAN-1, not at the end. Built in package MAN-1.
- Why: the repository's own tests fail on a decision without its diagram entry, and the docs check fails on a setting nobody documented.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "registered in log, diagram, traceability, env docs", key only.

## D294 — Decide for me works for projects: a rule table answers the gates
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Decide for me works for projects: one switch (`projects.decide_for_me`, set from the existing `J2_DECIDE_FOR_ME`) means "do not wait for me". It is passed to every worker as the existing setting, and the three node gates are answered at once by a fixed rule table in the Gatekeeper (code, never a model), each answer audited as `decided_by: rule:decide_for_me`. Tiers 0 to 3 are allowed within the role's grant; tier 4 only as read-only GitHub tools for this project; wide or missing write scopes run alone; a credit request is never raised (P25), but unspent slices may move inside the approved ceiling; a scope violation fails and quarantines the node; tier 6 is never granted and never asked. The owner's folder access (it caps the folder part of every grant, D298), all caps, the depth rule and the kill switch stay in force. The owner reads a "Sutra decided for you" list on the project page. Built in package MAN-8 (the table and the switch), MAN-13 (the "decided for you" list). (Amended by D319: the switch is the request's or the owner's own setting; a permission of the computer, an estimate over `project_credits` and tier 5 stay open for the owner.)
- Why: the owner wants projects that run with no input; a rule table keeps "a model never approves a model", and every wall that protects spend and the outside world stays where D251, D268 and D269 put it.
- Rejected: letting an AI pick the answer for node gates (a model would approve a model); having Decide for me raise credits (breaks P25); answering tier 6 "yes" (the wall in D268).
- Diagram: label "Decide for me for projects: a rule table", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D295 — Operating-system permissions are checked per system before a node starts
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Operating-system permissions are checked per system (macOS, Windows) before a node starts. A node's reach is its grant intersected with what the computer has allowed. A new `sutra_app/syspermissions.py` beside `oslayer.py` probes the folders the plan names (a listing, and for write steps one small temporary file in the node's own scope), explains what is missing in plain steps for that system, opens the right settings page and checks again. Sutra never changes a system setting, never asks for an administrator password and never runs a node elevated. Decide for me cannot grant these. Only the steps that need a missing permission wait (state `awaiting_system`); the rest of the project runs. Skipping cancels only those steps. A late "Operation not permitted" or "Access is denied" ends a node as `needs_system_permission` with the same card. The broadest permission (macOS Full Disk Access) is a last resort and names the path. Camera, microphone, screen, accessibility and other-app control are never requested. Linux has nothing to ask. Built in package MAN-15. (Amended by D322: probe in the folder root, a 45 s limit, the item list as built, the late card re-probes.)
- Why: macOS protects Desktop, Documents and similar folders per app and Windows can block writes with Controlled folder access, so a node would otherwise fail halfway with a vague error; asking up front, in system-specific words, keeps the owner in control and keeps the sentence "a model never approves" true for the machine too. (Amended by D296: the card does not outlive a closed laptop.)
- Rejected: asking in the middle of a run (a node would hang behind a hidden system dialog); Sutra changing the setting itself (a system setting belongs to the owner, and it would need an administrator password); one generic card for both systems (the steps are different); asking for Full Disk Access by default (far wider than needed).
- Diagram: label "OS permissions: check first, the owner clicks", pill on Desktop app · Electron window.

## D296 — A closed laptop is a refusal
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: A closed laptop is a refusal. If the computer sleeps, or Sutra is closed, while any gate is open (an approval card or a system permission card), every open gate of that project counts as refused: the waiting nodes are `cancelled` with the reason "the computer went to sleep before you answered" (or "Sutra was closed before you answered"), the running nodes of the project are stopped with the existing stop ladder, the project is `stopped`, and one receipt card says why. Detected by the Conductor's tick gap (more than half a lease) and by `reconcile_projects` after a restart. A project with no open gate keeps the one-time lease extension for a sleeping laptop (N2). An open card still ends after 24 hours. This amends D270 and D295 (the system permission card no longer waits for ever). Built in package MAN-7 (detection and recovery), MAN-8 and MAN-15 (the two cards).
- Why: the owner's rule: nobody is there to answer, so the work must not go on and must not wait unseen; stopping is the safe reading of silence.
- Rejected: waiting until the owner returns (the work and any held lease would sit unseen); stopping every project on every sleep (a project with nothing open can safely continue, as today).
- Diagram: label "closed laptop = refusal; gates end, project stops", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D297 — node_messages seq is 1 to n per project, with no gap
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: `node_messages` is keyed `(project_id, seq)` and `seq` is 1, 2, 3 ... per project with no gap and no repeat. The writer (`NodeStore.post`, MAN-6) works in ONE write transaction: it looks for `(project_id, src_ref)` first and, if the message is there, returns that row and takes no number; otherwise it takes `MAX(seq) + 1` for the project and inserts. A table-wide AUTOINCREMENT is not used. There is no global sequence for the page. Built in package MAN-1 (key, done), MAN-6 (writer and its tests: two projects interleaved, a repeated `src_ref`, a rollback, and 50 posts that read back as 1 to 50).
- Why: Guarantee the resume contract needs: at every committed state of the database the `seq` values of one project are exactly 1 to n. It holds because (1) the primary key forbids a repeat; (2) the server is the only writer and a write is `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, so two posts cannot read the same maximum; (3) the number is taken and used in the same transaction, so a rollback gives it back; (4) a repeated `src_ref` takes no number; (5) the triggers forbid UPDATE and DELETE, and a project is never deleted in v1 (D305); (6) `project.message` is published after COMMIT, and a reader sees one committed snapshot, so it sees a prefix 1 to k, never a hole. So `GET /api/projects/{id}/messages?after=k` returns k+1, k+2, ... in order; a page that gets anything but k+1 first has lost a message and refetches from its last contiguous number; the snapshot carries `last_message_seq`. Why: the page's catch-up is the streaming contract's (D201 to D238), which only works when a gap means loss.
- Rejected: a table-wide AUTOINCREMENT (project A would see 1, 3, 4: a false loss); a global sequence for the page (the page would have to stop treating a gap as loss, and the bus already has its own `seq`).
- Diagram: label "message seq: 1..n per project, no gap", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D298 — Tier 4 does not depend on the folder; the tool profile is split into sutra and github
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Tier 4 (the GitHub read tools, through the owner's own `gh`) does not depend on the owner's folder access. It depends on the role (a worker only) and on an approval (the owner's, or the Decide for me rule table's). The owner's folder access caps the FOLDER part of a grant (tiers 2 and 3, and what the folder lets a node read) and nothing else. The tool profile is split into its two parts: `sutra` (read-only app data, tier 1) and `github` (tier 4). A grant's `tool_profile` is `none`, `sutra`, `github` or `sutra+github`; a tier-4 grant holds `github` only, and holds `sutra` only if the step asked for it separately. Tiers are not a ladder: a grant lists each tier it holds. Built in package MAN-2 (grant rules, a fix), MAN-8 (delivery and the question).
- Why: the GitHub tools touch no file in the folder, so "read only" says nothing about them; the approval is the owner's real control over the network. The `sutra` part opens the whole app database read-only (known limit), so a tier-4 request must not carry it along. Delivery (a gap the code showed): today the engine path has no way to give a call a tool profile (the policy says `none`; `CallTag` and `CompletionRequest` carry no tools), so MAN-8 adds a `tool_profile` to the `node` block and an optional `PortSpec.tools` (empty by default) that `RouterPort` puts on a node's agent-turn calls only (never on a builder-tools call, which has its own grant); the plan gate does not ask the tier-4 question before this works (same package). A reviewer gets the `sutra` part at most; the planner none.
- Rejected: tying tier 4 to folder `edit` (it refuses a read-only owner a read-only tool and mixes two controls); keeping the bundle `sutra+github` for tier 4 (it hands out the app database read with it).
- Diagram: label "tier 4 independent of folder; sutra / github split", key only.
- Corrected by: D386 (tier 4 opens no web tool of a builder call).

## D299 — J2's builder tools are not a tier: the grant describes them as run_tools
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: J2's builder tools are not a tier and not part of the owner's-folder reach. A grant describes them in one separate field, `run_tools`: `engine` for a worker (the engine's own rule, unchanged, in the run folder) and `none` for the planner and the reviewer. The field is a description, written in the `grant.issued` audit row so the record is true; the grant does not enforce it, because the engine decides. Tier 3 becomes only the four git reads on the owner's folder: `commands` is `none` or `git_reads`. A worker gets `git_reads` when its folder level is `edit` (its Audit agent runs `inspect`, as `workspace_for` decides); a reviewer when its level is `inspect`. "A worker whose output only reads has tiers 0 and 1" stays, and means: nothing in the owner's folder beyond reading. Its builder tools in its own run folder are as they are today. Built in package MAN-2 (grant fields, a fix), MAN-5 (the `grant.issued` row says `run_tools`).
- Why: What the code showed: a builder-tools call (`CompletionRequest.run_tools`, a `ToolGrant`) runs in a folder strictly inside the run folder; the router gives it no owner's folder and no MCP profile; the engine switches it on (`llm.tools_on`: live with the Claude CLI unless `J2_AGENT_TOOLS=0`) for the folder-type outputs of `types.json`; the department output spec's `workspace` (`read` or `edit`) says only what builders may do in the OWNER's folder. So the two reaches are independent, and calling the builder tools "tier 3" made a document or website run (`read`) look as if it had no tools when it has them today. Known limit, written on purpose: `types.json` gives those builders Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep, Bash, WebSearch and WebFetch; they are fenced by the CLI sandbox when `srt` is found (macOS, Linux) and by J2's check that a file outside the folder fails the workflow. A node's grant does not narrow them in v1 (`agent_tools` is a locked engine setting, not a task key; a per-task key is a J2 sync); the owner can still turn them off for all runs in the engine settings. So tier 4's words "no other network tool" mean Sutra's own tools.
- Rejected: leaving the grant silent (the audit would not say what a worker can do); a tier 3 that depends on the output level (it would remove today's tools from document runs, which is a replacement); narrowing the tools per node now (frozen engine).
- Diagram: label "builder tools = run_tools, not a tier", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.
- Corrected by: D380 and D386 (the host narrows a step's builder tools: reads, own-folder writes, no shell, no web).

## D300 — The plan gate's confirm threshold is 500 work credits
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The plan gate's confirm threshold is 500 work credits. It is `NODE_DEFAULT_CAPS["confirm_credits"]` in `config.py`, copied into `projects.caps_json["confirm_credits"]` at plan time like the other caps. The plan's estimate is the sum of its steps' `estimate_credits`; a step with no estimate from the planner counts as its slice (the team's `J2_BUDGET`, default 100). `Plan.confirm_credits` defaults to the configured number, and `None` (never ask about spend) only when the owner started the project with their own `budget`: that number is the owner's answer already. Decide for me answers this gate "allow up to the ceiling" as before (D294). Built in package MAN-1 (the key, a fix), MAN-5 (estimate and ceiling), MAN-8 (the gate). (Amended by D318: the default is 400 work credits so that five default steps ask.)
- Why: a full documents-plus-website run needs 500 or more (`docs/ENVIRONMENT.md`), so one ordinary run never trips it, but five default runs or two heavy ones do, which is more than the owner would start by hand in one go.
- Rejected: no number (a project could cost any amount with no question); asking on every plan (wears the owner out).
- Diagram: label "plan confirm threshold: 500 credits", key only.

## D301 — A planner Stop must end the running call
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: A planner Stop must stop the running call, not wait for it. MAN-5 adds an optional `cancel` to `call_json_result` and the one-shot plan of the base connector, the Claude and Codex connectors and the stub, so the token is bound to the spawned process exactly as a chat turn's is (D215) and the cancel ladder ends it in at most 12 s. A small registry of the in-flight planner tokens (in `ProjectService`, built in MAN-5) is what node stop, project stop and kill-all ask to stop. Pause-all does not (what runs finishes). A cancelled planner call ends the request as stopped; no plan is adopted. Built in package MAN-5 (connector change, registry, planner wiring), MAN-4's `NodeControl` gets the registry then.
- Why: the planner's call is a CLI child inside the server that the stop ladder never sees; the cooperative token of MAN-3 (checked before each attempt) lets it run up to the call's timeout (120 s) and spend quota after a kill, which breaks "kill all stops everything" (D279). The mechanism exists; the cost is four signatures and one argument. D296 is not involved: the planner has no open gate, because the plan gate comes after planning.
- Rejected: accepting the wait; cancelling the asyncio task only (it would orphan the child).
- Diagram: label "planner Stop ends the running call", pill on Connector router.

## D302 — A node with no valid grant never starts
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: A node whose grant is missing, unreadable or wider than the role table never starts, not even as an ordinary task. The task gate holds it with a sentence ("Held: this agent has no valid grant") and `_run_spec` has no fallback. MAN-5's Conductor then fails the node with a `grant.denied` audit row. Built in package MAN-4 (fix: the gate checks `node_block_of`), MAN-5 (the fail).
- Why: an ordinary task has the reach of a team run, which is wider than a reviewer's; "deny by default" must hold for the broken case too. Holding one task does not stop the queue: the start loop moves on to the next (`continue`).
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "no valid grant, no start", pill on band:ORCHESTRATION.

## D303 — The kill switch: one owner, four rules
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The kill switch has one owner and four rules. (a) A KILL file made by hand is enforced by `NodeControl`'s watcher (one `lstat` every 2 s, plus once at start); it stays when the Conductor arrives, so a stuck tick cannot hold back a kill; the tick may call `enforce()` too (it deals with each file once). (b) The Conductor's task gate (MAN-5) asks `NodeControl.gate` first and adds its own checks: composed, never replaced. (c) Kill-all and node stop run the stop ladders together (as app shutdown does), so the whole kill takes about 12 s, not 12 s per node. (d) A kill ends the project: with MAN-5, every project with a live node becomes `stopped` ("stopped by the kill switch"), its queued and blocked nodes are `cancelled`, and nothing starts by itself after resume; the owner starts again. Pause only holds: nothing is cancelled and what waited starts at resume. A node that the owner or the kill switch stopped is never retried or restarted by the Conductor. (e) The automatic triggers of D279 are built with their causes, not in MAN-4: caps and three egress hits in MAN-9, a node that loses its lease twice in MAN-7, a scope violation in MAN-10; the monthly limit is the existing `Limits` hold. Built in package MAN-4 (fix c), MAN-5 (b, d), MAN-7, MAN-9, MAN-10 (e).
- Why: the owner must be able to stop everything even when the page or the tick is stuck, and a stop that restarts work by itself after resume is a surprise (the closed-laptop rule, D296, reads silence as stop in the same way).
- Rejected: letting the tick be the only watcher; queued nodes starting again by themselves after a kill (the dev's N24 reading, right for MAN-4 alone, where there are no projects yet).
- Diagram: label "kill switch: watcher stays; ladders together; kill ends project", pill on Operator.

## D304 — Developer findings of phase 1 adopted as design
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Developer findings adopted as design (N2, N3, N7, N11, N14, N16, N17, N21, N22, N23, N24, N25): `projects.decide_for_me`, `projects.paused`, `projects.review_loops` and `approvals.created_at` are columns of migration v6; `node_messages` has the append-only triggers; a grant's folder level is `none`, `read`, `inspect` or `edit` (`inspect` = read plus the four git reads, as `Router.folder_for` decides); the plan gate asks about a write scope of MORE THAN ONE PATH, and a writer with no declared scope is never asked about but runs alone (D260, D294); `IdFactory` makes `prj_`, `nd_`, `gr_` and `ap_` ids; the egress row carries `node` and `role` (the project is one join away); the usage row carries the runtime node as `detail.agent_node` and `detail.agent_role` (`usage_ledger.node_id` stays the graph node); the `task_gate` hook is in MAN-4 (pulled forward); the routes `GET /api/nodes/status` and `POST /api/nodes/resume` exist beside the designed ones; a stopped node is `cancelled` and the projector owns node state from MAN-5; the D296 words and audit names (`gate.refused`, `project.stopped`) are constants in `nodes_store.py`. Built in package MAN-1 to MAN-4 (done), documented in architecture and guardrails.
- Why: each is additive, safe and was needed by the code; none replaces anything.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "phase 1 findings adopted (N2..N25)", key only.

## D305 — Small rules found while building phase 1
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Small rules found while building phase 1. (a) A reviewer's output level is the strongest level among the steps it reviews (`edit` if it reviews a writer), so it gets the git reads exactly when the work it checks changed files. (b) `max_nodes` (12) counts workers; reviewers come on top (as D273 says). (c) `agent_nodes.task_id` points at the node's CURRENT attempt; an earlier attempt stays in `tasks` through `parent_task_id`, and a retry re-points the row in the same transaction that makes the new task. (d) Projects are never deleted in v1 (the append-only tables hold them); archiving is a later decision. (e) Reads of the new routes need no token, as every read of the app; every write needs it. (f) The new flags stay out of `Settings.public()` and `GET /api/config`; the page learns the flag from `GET /api/nodes/status` (404 means off). Built in package MAN-2 (b, fix), MAN-5 (c), MAN-11 (a), MAN-13 (f).
- Why: each closes a gap the code showed and none changes a rule the owner set.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "small rules: workers counted, task_id = current attempt", key only.

## D306 — The node plan: scope and needs come from a separate small planner call
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The plan carries a step's write scope and its `needs` through a separate small planner call, the "node plan" (stage D), not through the planner's existing schema. It runs only with `SUTRA_NODES_V1` on and only for a request with two or more steps, after the assignments are final and before `ProjectService.reserve`. Reply: a new closed family `node_plan` (`steps: [{ref, scope: [<= 5 relative paths], needs: [sutra | github]}]`), registered in `agent/replies.py` like `chat_turn`; prompt: a fifth template `nodeplan.md`. The call carries the planner's identity and stop token (D301), has its own cap of 2 attempts, and is only a request: `scope` goes through `normalize_scope` into the step's request, `needs` becomes its tool profile (`github` also asks tier 4); the Gatekeeper and the owner decide; a planner can never add a tier. If the stage fails the defaults apply (no scope, no needs) and an audit row `plan.defaults` says so; the project never fails because of it. `AssignmentDraft`, the `chat_turn` schema, the decide and assign prompts and every flag-off golden stay byte-identical; `docs/schemas/` only gains `node_plan.v1.json` and one line in `index.json`.** (Amended by D316: there is no `node_plan.v1.json` in `docs/schemas/`.)
Why: the planner's step model is derived into the pinned `chat_turn` schema and prompts, so adding fields there moves flag-off goldens, and a flag-aware schema would make flag-off bytes depend on a runtime switch; the output specs say only `read` or `edit`, so nothing can be derived from them. Before it exists the plan gate can ask the estimate question only; it cannot ask tier 4 or a wide scope because no step carries them, and a writer holds the whole folder. Rejected: fields in `AssignmentDraft`; a flag-aware schema generator; the Gatekeeper deriving scope from the output specs (nothing to derive); asking the owner to type scopes (MAN-13 may add that later). Package: MAN-8 (stage, tier-4 and wide-scope questions, `scope` carried and shown), MAN-10 (`scope` enforced).

**D307. What a grant says about an output nobody knows yet, and what the planner may touch. (a) A team whose output is `auto` is read as `read` for the grant while the grant is a description (MAN-5 to MAN-9), and the `grant.issued` row says "output auto (decided in the run)". (b) From MAN-10, with lanes on, a worker whose output is `auto` is treated as a possible writer: no declared scope means the whole folder and it runs alone (D260). With lanes off, different teams run in parallel exactly as today. The page draws a conflict edge only when lanes are on. (c) The planner has no stored grant. With the flag on its calls are tagged `workspace="read"`: it may read the owner's folder as the org chat agent does today and can never edit it. With the flag off it stays as today (`edit`, D127, capped by the owner).**
Why: nothing enforces the grant's folder yet, so `read` is only a record, but a record must not claim more than is known; for scheduling the safe reading is "may write". The planner's calls were tagged `edit` (D127), which the design had not noticed: a folder file with an injected instruction could have made the planner write. Rejected: `auto` as `edit` everywhere now (it would serialise every default team before anything uses it); a planner with no folder at all (it would change how it plans). Package: MAN-5 (record, G10; planner tag, G6), MAN-10 (possible writer, conflict edges).

**D308. A gate that nothing can answer yet is held and said out loud, never skipped and never silent. Until MAN-8 a plan over the confirm threshold makes the project `awaiting_approval`, the questions are stored, every node waits, and the reply to the owner says so in plain words (the Sutra line `project_waits`); the 24 h end or a restart ends it with the D296 note. The flag stays off in every beta the owner is given until MAN-8 is in. MAN-8 owns the rest: the answer path (re-evaluate the plan with what was approved, rewrite the grants of nodes that have not spawned, move the nodes on), Decide for me (`reserve` gets `decide_for_me` from the request's `J2_DECIDE_FOR_ME`; the rule table answers in the same transaction, no hold), and the estimate question stored as kind `plan_estimate`, tier 5, answered allow once or deny only (D270).**
Why: the stored rows are exactly what MAN-8 reads, so holding loses nothing; refusing would throw the plan away and skipping would start spending nobody approved (D300). Rejected: refuse at planning; treat "no answer path" as yes. Package: MAN-5 (the line, G7), MAN-8.

**D309. Hand-off intake rules. (a) Dependants are given `handoff.accepted.json` (written by the server after the check) through the existing `upstream` listing; `RunSpec.upstream` has no new field (D265). (b) The checksums of one hand-off share a budget of 64 MB in all; past it a file is checked by size only and counted as `unchecked` (the intake runs inside the projector's transaction, so it must be short). (c) For a node the host's `handoff` step runs first among the finish steps: a slow `agent_views` or `catalog` must never leave a node with no hand-off. (d) The host lists the run folder's top-level files; MAN-10 adds an optional `changes` list (paths a worker changed in the owner's folder) to `handoff/1` and the intake's key set grows with it. (e) A body over 16 KB is stored as `{file, sha256, bytes}` with the sha of the FILE; a hand-off with status `blocked` ends the node `failed`, never retried (MAN-11, the first writer of it).**
Why: each closes a gap the code showed in the first hand-off flow; none changes a rule of D263 to D265, D281. Rejected: hashing outside the transaction (the node's state, the message and its dependants must change together); keeping the step last. Package: MAN-6 (b, c as fixes G9, G1), MAN-10 (d), MAN-11 (e).

**D310. Retry and failure rules completed. (a) A node's retry keeps the dependencies of the attempt it replaces (they are done), so the new attempt is given the same upstream; only the owner's own retry of an ordinary task is as it was. (b) A node the task gate holds for "no valid grant" is failed by the tick: its task is cancelled, the node is `failed` with an audit row `grant.denied` (actor conductor), and the failure policy applies (D302 completed). (c) `failure_policy` is set by the owner only: an optional field of `POST /api/projects` (built with the page, MAN-13), default `stop_branch`; the planner never sets it and a request made in the chat is always `stop_branch`. (d) `reserve` is fail-closed: a request that already has a failed or stopped project is not "ok", and a task whose step has no node is an error, never an ordinary task past the gate.**
Why: (a) a retried dependant would otherwise run blind; (b) today such a project sits for ever; (c) a model should not choose how a project fails and the code had no way to set it; (d) deny by default. Package: MAN-7 (a, b fixes G4, G3), MAN-5 (d, G8), MAN-13 (c).

**D311. The closed-laptop check and its receipt. (a) The tick gap is measured on the wall clock (`Clock`), from the END of one tick to the START of the next, so the tick's own stop ladders are not read as a sleep; a monotonic clock stops during sleep on macOS and Linux and could never see one. A clock jump forward reads as a sleep, which is the safe side. (b) The receipt of D296 is a plain note in the org chat (once per project, by its `src_ref`), plus `projects.stop_reason` and the audit rows `gate.refused` and `project.stopped`. No receipt card kind is built in MAN-8 or MAN-15. This amends D275 and D296.**
Why: (a) the first version counted its own work as sleep and could refuse an open gate falsely; (b) a receipt tells and asks nothing, and `needs_you` is for questions. Rejected: a monotonic gap; a card that needs dismissing. Package: MAN-7 (G2), MAN-13 (the page shows the reason).

**D312. The design as built, written down. (a) The Conductor (the task gate, the node state machine, teardown, the tick, retry, the D296 refusal, stop) lives in `projects.py` beside `ProjectService`; C0, a move with no change of behaviour, splits it into `conductor.py` before MAN-8 so later packages land where the design says. (b) The stored node states are `planned`, `awaiting_approval`, `awaiting_system`, `queued`, `leased`, `running`, `expired`, `done`, `failed`, `cancelled`; `ready` is the moment an answer arrives (the node goes to `planned` or `queued` by its task) and `torn_down` is the column `torn_down_at`; `handing_off` is only a step inside one transaction. (c) A project is made by `reserve` before its tasks and each task is linked by `link_task` in the transaction that makes it (the chat agent's runner makes tasks one transaction each); `reconcile_projects` cancels a node whose task was never made. (d) The flag-off scheduler has the same behaviour as before, with one fix for every task: a process that began for a task that ended meanwhile is stopped. (e) Known limit: the append-only triggers stop UPDATE and DELETE, not `INSERT OR REPLACE` (SQLite fires delete triggers on a replace only with `recursive_triggers`); the guard is a test that scans for the spelling, and a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger goes into v7 if the schema is touched again. Package: MAN-5 to MAN-7 (done), C0 before MAN-8.
- Why: the design and the code should say the same thing, and each point was a place where building showed a simpler or truer shape. Package: MAN-5 to MAN-7 (done), C0 before MAN-8.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "node plan: scope and needs from stage D", key only.

**D310. Retry and failure rules completed. (a) A node's retry keeps the dependencies of the attempt it replaces (they are done), so the new attempt is given the same upstream; only the owner's own retry of an ordinary task is as it was. (b) A node the task gate holds for "no valid grant" is failed by the tick: its task is cancelled, the node is `failed` with an audit row `grant.denied` (actor conductor), and the failure policy applies (D302 completed). (c) `failure_policy` is set by the owner only: an optional field of `POST /api/projects` (built with the page, MAN-13), default `stop_branch`; the planner never sets it and a request made in the chat is always `stop_branch`. (d) `reserve` is fail-closed: a request that already has a failed or stopped project is not "ok", and a task whose step has no node is an error, never an ordinary task past the gate.**
Why: (a) a retried dependant would otherwise run blind; (b) today such a project sits for ever; (c) a model should not choose how a project fails and the code had no way to set it; (d) deny by default. Package: MAN-7 (a, b fixes G4, G3), MAN-5 (d, G8), MAN-13 (c).

**D311. The closed-laptop check and its receipt. (a) The tick gap is measured on the wall clock (`Clock`), from the END of one tick to the START of the next, so the tick's own stop ladders are not read as a sleep; a monotonic clock stops during sleep on macOS and Linux and could never see one. A clock jump forward reads as a sleep, which is the safe side. (b) The receipt of D296 is a plain note in the org chat (once per project, by its `src_ref`), plus `projects.stop_reason` and the audit rows `gate.refused` and `project.stopped`. No receipt card kind is built in MAN-8 or MAN-15. This amends D275 and D296.**
Why: (a) the first version counted its own work as sleep and could refuse an open gate falsely; (b) a receipt tells and asks nothing, and `needs_you` is for questions. Rejected: a monotonic gap; a card that needs dismissing. Package: MAN-7 (G2), MAN-13 (the page shows the reason).

**D312. The design as built, written down. (a) The Conductor (the task gate, the node state machine, teardown, the tick, retry, the D296 refusal, stop) lives in `projects.py` beside `ProjectService`; C0, a move with no change of behaviour, splits it into `conductor.py` before MAN-8 so later packages land where the design says. (b) The stored node states are `planned`, `awaiting_approval`, `awaiting_system`, `queued`, `leased`, `running`, `expired`, `done`, `failed`, `cancelled`; `ready` is the moment an answer arrives (the node goes to `planned` or `queued` by its task) and `torn_down` is the column `torn_down_at`; `handing_off` is only a step inside one transaction. (c) A project is made by `reserve` before its tasks and each task is linked by `link_task` in the transaction that makes it (the chat agent's runner makes tasks one transaction each); `reconcile_projects` cancels a node whose task was never made. (d) The flag-off scheduler has the same behaviour as before, with one fix for every task: a process that began for a task that ended meanwhile is stopped. (e) Known limit: the append-only triggers stop UPDATE and DELETE, not `INSERT OR REPLACE` (SQLite fires delete triggers on a replace only with `recursive_triggers`); the guard is a test that scans for the spelling, and a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger goes into v7 if the schema is touched again. Package: MAN-5 to MAN-7 (done), C0 before MAN-8.
- Why: the design and the code should say the same thing, and each point was a place where building showed a simpler or truer shape. Package: MAN-5 to MAN-7 (done), C0 before MAN-8.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "unanswerable gate: held and said", key only.

## D309 — Hand-off intake rules
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Hand-off intake rules. (a) Dependants are given `handoff.accepted.json` (written by the server after the check) through the existing `upstream` listing; `RunSpec.upstream` has no new field (D265). (b) The checksums of one hand-off share a budget of 64 MB in all; past it a file is checked by size only and counted as `unchecked` (the intake runs inside the projector's transaction, so it must be short). (c) For a node the host's `handoff` step runs first among the finish steps: a slow `agent_views` or `catalog` must never leave a node with no hand-off. (d) The host lists the run folder's top-level files; MAN-10 adds an optional `changes` list (paths a worker changed in the owner's folder) to `handoff/1` and the intake's key set grows with it. (e) A body over 16 KB is stored as `{file, sha256, bytes}` with the sha of the FILE; a hand-off with status `blocked` ends the node `failed`, never retried (MAN-11, the first writer of it).**
Why: each closes a gap the code showed in the first hand-off flow; none changes a rule of D263 to D265, D281. Rejected: hashing outside the transaction (the node's state, the message and its dependants must change together); keeping the step last. Package: MAN-6 (b, c as fixes G9, G1), MAN-10 (d), MAN-11 (e).

A clock jump forward reads as a sleep, which is the safe side. (b) The receipt of D296 is a plain note in the org chat (once per project, by its `src_ref`), plus `projects.stop_reason` and the audit rows `gate.refused` and `project.stopped`. (b) The stored node states are `planned`, `awaiting_approval`, `awaiting_system`, `queued`, `leased`, `running`, `expired`, `done`, `failed`, `cancelled`; `ready` is the moment an answer arrives (the node goes to `planned` or `queued` by its task) and `torn_down` is the column `torn_down_at`; `handing_off` is only a step inside one transaction. ## D313 — Turning the flag off never lets a project's steps run as ordinary tasks
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Turning the flag off never lets a project's steps run as ordinary tasks. When the app starts with `SUTRA_NODES_V1` off and the database holds a step of a project that has not started (blocked or queued), that step stays on hold with a plain note ("On hold: this step is part of a project, and projects are switched off. Switch them on again, or stop this step") and the owner can stop it like any task. The hold is installed only when such a step exists, so an app that never used projects is exactly as before. A step that already runs is not touched. This amends D254 (what "off" means for a step that waits) and D302. Package: MAN-5 (built as a fix in the phase 2 round).
- Why: an ordinary task has the reach of a whole team run, wider than a node's grant, so letting a waiting step start that way after the flag goes off would give it more than anyone granted; the owner turning the switch off means "stop", not "run it the old way".
- Rejected: starting them as ordinary tasks (the old text of ENVIRONMENT.md); cancelling them at start (the owner may switch the flag on again and want them).
- Diagram: label "flag off: waiting project steps stay on hold", key only.

## D314 — An app that was closed is not a node that failed
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: An app that was closed is not a node that failed. `reconcile_projects` re-adopts a node whose process still lives (its lease starts again from now). A node found dead because the app was closed (its task ended with "the app was closed") gets a fresh attempt as a new task of the same node, audit `node.restarted`, that does NOT spend its one retry; at most 2 such restart attempts per node (counted from the audit rows), after that the closing counts like any failure. A real failure still has its single retry (`attempt - restarts <= retries`). This amends D277 and D305 (c). Package: MAN-7 (built as a fix in the phase 2 round).
- Why: closing the laptop or the app is the owner's own act, and a project that fails because they went to lunch would be a poor product; the cap stops a node that crashes the app from being started for ever.
- Rejected: counting the closing as the one retry (the owner's habit would use it up); no cap.
- Diagram: label "app closed: fresh attempt, retry not spent, cap 2", key only.

## D315 — A no to a gate is as wide as the question
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: An answer to a gate is as wide as the question. (a) A no to the plan's estimate, the running out of any question (24 h), the owner's stop and a closed laptop (D296) stop the project ("not approved"). (b) A no to a question about ONE step (tier 4, or a wide write scope) cancels only that step ("not approved: you said no"); its dependants follow the failure policy; the other steps go on once no question is open; the project ends `done_with_open_items` if another step finished and `failed` if none did. This is D295's "skip" rule for every step question. (c) Until (b) is built (P1) the code stops the whole project on any no; that is accepted for a flag-on beta because it fails to the safe side (nothing spends), and the card must say it in words: "If you say no, the whole project stops." (d) "Allow once" and "allow for the project" mean the same for a plan question in v1: the grant is frozen when the plan is accepted and a retry keeps it; the difference matters from MAN-11 (the grant gate after a `needs.json`). Package: MAN-8 (built as (a) and (c)), P1 for (b).
- Why: one "no" to "may step 3 read GitHub" is not a "no" to the other three steps; D295 already treats a refused step that way.
- Rejected: running the step without the tool (a quiet downgrade nobody chose); stopping everything for good (it throws away work the owner never refused)
- Diagram: label "a no is as wide as its question", key only.

## D316 — MAN-8 as built, accepted
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: MAN-8 as built, accepted. (a) The plan the answer path needs is kept in `projects.caps_json["plan"]` (ref, team, scope, needs, estimate, depends_on). No migration: v6 is unreleased and a plan is never edited after the project is made (D274). It is an internal key: `ProjectOut.caps` and the snapshot leave it out. If a later package needs more than this, a v7 column is allowed. (b) The node plan is one more model call for a request of two or more steps; a flag-on test that scripts the planner scripts it too. There is no `node_plan.v1.json` (amends D306's last sentence). (c) `ChatPost.decide_for_me` is an optional request field read only with the flag on (the same pattern as `budget`, D252); with the flag off it is accepted, ignored and NOT stored (G13). (d) `POST /api/projects/{id}/approvals/{aid}` takes `{decision}`, answers with the project snapshot, and is idempotent by the approval's own id (the first answer wins; the same answer again returns the snapshot; a different one is 409; `allow_project` on tier 5 or 6 is 422; an answer after the expiry says it ran out). There is no `client_id` on it: the row id is the key and is stronger. `POST /api/projects/{id}/system/{item}/check` and `/skip` are added. The snapshot carries `cards` and `system_cards`. (e) Tier 4 reaches an engine worker through `NodeBlock.tool_profile`, `PortSpec.tools` and the `RouterPort`, on agent turns only (D298). A mock or stub connector cannot take tools and refuses such a call: that matters only in a mock run. Package: MAN-8 (done).
- Why: each point is a place where the code was simpler or truer than the design and none weakens a rule.
- Rejected: a v7 column for the plan (a migration for a table nobody has yet); `client_id` on the answer (a second key for a thing that can be answered once)
- Diagram: label "MAN-8 as built: plan in caps_json, route shape", key only.

## D317 — The approval card is a message of the org chat
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The approval card is a message of the org chat, and MAN-8 is not complete without it. Before MAN-10 and MAN-11 start (G12): (1) when a question opens, the org chat gets a card message of type `node_approval`, once per approval (by its `src_ref`), written in the transaction that opens the question; (2) it counts in `needs_you`, and `needs_you.changed` is sent when a question opens and when it is answered or ends; (3) the chat page draws it with its options as buttons that call the route, and shows it as answered afterwards; (4) its words say what a no does (D315); (5) every existing response keeps its bytes (nothing is written with the flag off, because no row exists); the new kind in the response schemas is added on purpose, as N19; (6) the reply to a request that opens any owner question says so in plain words for every kind (tier 4, wide scope, estimate), not only the estimate; (7) until this is in, a flag-on beta goes to nobody who cannot call the API, except with Decide for me on. A receipt stays a note (D311); only a QUESTION is a card. Package: MAN-8 (finish), tests in `tests/ui/`.
- Why: D308 said "never held and silent", and the card is what replaces the line; today a tier-4 or scope question is stored, holds the project, says nothing and cancels it after 24 h, and the owner has no place to answer. Phase 4 adds two more questions (the scope violation, the grant gate), so the plumbing is built once, first.
- Rejected: leaving it to the project page (MAN-13 is the bigger job and the answer must be possible where the owner already is); the reason given in N47 ("a new ChatCard kind moves flag-off API shapes"): an earlier card kind (`DeliverableCard`) was added the same way, and a response with no such row has the same bytes
- Diagram: label "approval card in the chat, before phase 4", key only.

## D318 — The numbers of the plan gate: 400 and 1000
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The numbers of the plan gate. (a) The confirm threshold is 400 work credits, not 500 (amends D300). The chat plans at most 5 steps and a step with no estimate counts as its slice of 100, so 5 steps are 500 and "over 500" could never be true: the one gate that protects spend would never ask. With 400, four default steps do not ask and five do; the question says "about 500 credits, which is over 400". A request that has its own `budget` still asks nothing (D300). (b) `project_credits` (1000) is a new default cap, listed with D273's: the most a plan's estimate may be for Decide for me to say yes once. Over it, the question stays open for the owner. Package: MAN-8 (G18).
- Why: D300 meant "five default runs ask"; the arithmetic said otherwise.
- Rejected: changing the comparison to "at or over 500" (the sentence "which is over 500" would be false); a threshold the chat can never reach
- Diagram: label "confirm 400, project_credits 1000", key only.

## D319 — Decide for me as built, and what it must also do
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Decide for me as built, and what it must also do. (a) A project's switch is the request's `decide_for_me` OR the owner's own Decide for me setting (the Simple view's switch, `POST /api/settings`), read when the project is reserved (G13). One switch, one meaning (D294): an owner who turned it on in the app must not find a project waiting for them. (b) The rule table is data (`gatekeeper.RULE_TABLE`) and one function answers one question. What it never answers, so the question stays open for the owner, is exactly three things: a permission of the computer (D295), a plan estimate over `project_credits` (D318), and tier 5 otherwise; tier 6 is never asked. So "no hold" in guardrails 5.1 has those three exceptions. (c) The rows `slice_move` and `scope_violation` are data and pure functions until MAN-10 uses them (D320 says why a slice is not moved). (d) The switch reaches the workers as the task setting `J2_DECIDE_FOR_ME=1`; J2's own rule (P25, `_auto_pick`) waits for the Requester on a credit raise, so a worker on Decide for me cannot raise credits and no engine re-test is needed: the proof on Sutra's side is that the two budget settings reach `task.json`. (e) Turning it on later answers the open questions at once and turning it off applies to the next gate (P2). Package: MAN-8 (built; G13 and P2 finish it).
- Why: the built switch only listened to a field no screen sends, so the switch the owner already uses did nothing for the gates.
- Rejected: a second switch for projects (two switches with one meaning); letting the table answer the system permission or an over-ceiling estimate (the walls of D294)
- Diagram: label "Decide for me: also the owner's own switch", key only.

## D320 — Budgets as built: a slice is final
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Budgets as built, accepted with limits written down. (a) The ceiling (`projects.budget_credits`) is the sum of the slices; a slice is the step's estimate, or the owner's `budget` (D252), or 100; the run gets `J2_BUDGET` = `J2_BUDGET_CAP` = its slice. (b) A slice is final in v1. There is no raise card and no budget gate (guardrails 5, third row, moves to Non-goals for v1): J2's raise question can only add up to the cap, which is the slice, and a raise above it would need a Gatekeeper that sees what the other nodes spent. A node that runs out of credits ends the way a J2 run ends today. The owner who wants more asks again with a bigger budget. The owner's own raise inside a step is therefore not possible for a project's steps; that is a difference from an ordinary task and the owner is told in the guardrails. (c) A retry starts with the same slice, so the ceiling is per attempt: the worst case is (1 + retries) times the ceiling; the project's dollar cap (when set) and its wall time bound the rest. (d) The gate's "the slices fit the ceiling" check is a consistency guard only (it cannot fire unless a row was edited); it is kept. (e) Nothing calls `move_unspent`: with no raise there is nothing to move to, so it stays a pure function. Package: MAN-9 (done); amends D271.
- Why: P25 keeps every raise the Requester's own, and giving a node a cap above its slice would let the owner's approval raise the total past a ceiling nobody tracks.
- Rejected: a cap above the slice (the ceiling stops being true); a retry slice of "what is left" (needs J2's credit count, which the host does not see)
- Diagram: label "slice is final; ceiling per attempt", key only.

## D321 — Caps and the automatic triggers as built
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Caps and the automatic triggers as built, and three fixes. (a) A node whose meter fired ends FAILED even when J2 itself would end the run `done` or `done_with_open_items`: the host turns the status into `crashed` when `meter.reached` is set (G14). It is not retried and its dependants follow the failure policy (N3). (b) The project's wall time is counted from the start of its first node, not from its creation (G15): a project that waits for the owner's answer is not running, and the 24 h question expiry already covers waiting. (c) The triggers (a cap reached, three scrubs by one node, project wall time, project dollars) are an audit row plus one plain note in the org chat, not a card: they tell and ask nothing (D311). This amends guardrails 7 ("and a card"). (d) The defaults of 60 calls and 30 minutes per node are guesses from D273; before publish they are measured on real document and website runs in the beta and set from the numbers (P4). (e) The meter counts a failed call as a call; a cap of none is never checked. Package: MAN-9 (G14, G15 fixes; P4).
- Why: (a) otherwise a node that was cut off hands partial work to its dependants as a success; (b) otherwise a project approved after two hours is stopped by the first tick; (c) a note is the receipt D311 chose; (d) a cap below what a normal run needs would fail good work.
- Rejected: stopping the run from outside the engine (J2's own failure path is enough once the status is right)
- Diagram: label "cap hit = failed; wall time from first start", key only.

## D322 — System permissions as built, accepted with changes
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: System permissions as built, accepted with changes. (a) The probe looks in the owner's folder root, not in the node's own scope (a permission belongs to a folder, not to a scope); its one temporary file is `.sutra-probe-<hex>` and is removed at once; MAN-10's path attribution ignores that name. (b) macOS: only Desktop, Documents and Downloads are probed; iCloud Drive and removable or network volumes are found by the late refusal (`needs_system_permission`) and can be added later. (c) Windows: an elevated process makes the plan refused with a plain sentence, not a card (nothing to answer); long paths are a need (with Check again) only when the folder's own path is over 240 characters and Windows is set to refuse them (the design said "warn only"; a step that will fail is better held). (d) The "app is not in the usual place" warning is not built; it moves to MAN-13 as a static note. (e) The probe has a time limit of 45 s (G16): a macOS dialog can stay unanswered, and the runner must not hang for ever. Past the limit the item counts as a need, the card opens and Check again works; the late result of that probe is ignored. (f) A project stopped or killed between `reserve` and the making of its tasks makes no task: `link_task` refuses for a project that has ended (G16). (g) The late refusal makes ONE card per project. "Check again" must probe the folder again and clear only when the probe finds nothing, for the item `unknown` too (G17); after it clears, the failed node gets one fresh attempt that spends no retry (P3), as D314 does for a closed app. (h) Everything is tested with fakes. The manual check on a real Mac and a real Windows computer is a must before publish (P5) and the card words may change after it. Package: MAN-15 (done; G16, G17, P3, P5).
- Why: each is a place where the code showed a gap or a simpler shape; none changes the wall (Sutra changes no setting, asks for no password, never answers for the owner).
- Rejected: probing in each node's scope (one probe per scope, same answer); waiting for ever for a dialog
- Diagram: label "system probe as built; 45 s limit", key only.

## D323 — "Runs alone" is a promise only MAN-10 can keep
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: "Runs alone" is a promise only MAN-10 can keep. (a) Until MAN-10 nothing keeps one writer apart from another, so no card, line or rule text may say that a writer "will run alone" (G19). The question reads "A step changes files in several places of your folder. Allow it?" (b) MAN-10 enforces scope leases, conflict edges and "no scope means alone" (D259, D260) for every project when the master flag is on. `SUTRA_NODES_LANES` only decides whether two nodes of the SAME team may run together. This amends D307(b): "with lanes off, parallelism is exactly today's" is true for ordinary tasks, not for the steps of one project. (c) The page draws a conflict edge whenever edges are enforced. (d) A worker whose output is `auto` is a possible writer (D307) when edges are enforced. Package: MAN-10.
- Why: the first design let a sub-flag switch off a safety rule, so in the default beta the card would promise something nothing does.
- Rejected: keeping the rule behind the lanes flag
- Diagram: label "runs alone is MAN-10's promise", key only.

## D324 — The ceiling is real
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The ceiling is real. `projects.budget_credits` is the project ceiling (the sum of the slices); `make_slices` runs with a ceiling when the project is reserved (the owner's own number, or the estimate the owner approves, or the cap `project_credits`). A retry or a restart (D314, P3) starts with its slice MINUS what the earlier attempts of the same node spent, never a full fresh slice, and a node that has nothing left is not tried again. Until J2 reports its own credits to the server, one credit is counted for each model call recorded in the usage ledger for the task chain of the node (`parent_task_id`). The project's dollar limit and the snapshot's `spend` count every attempt of every node. `move_unspent` is removed: a slice is final (D320 b) and there is nothing to move to. This amends D320 (c). Package: MAN-9 (M5).
- Why: a retry with a full slice made the worst case (1 + retries) times the ceiling, and a dollar limit that ignored the first attempt of a retried node was not a limit.
- Rejected: leaving the slice per attempt (the ceiling would be a label); waiting for J2 credits (nothing would be bounded meanwhile)
- Diagram: label "ceiling real: retry gets slice minus spend", key only.

## D325 — The app's own data is only ever a question to the owner
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: (Corrected by D339: an engine step's prompts are only observed, not scrubbed.) The app's own data is only ever a question to the owner. A planner's `needs: ["sutra"]` (the `sutra` tool profile reads the whole app database) is NOT granted on the planner's say: it becomes a question of kind `sutra_tools`, tier 1, about that one step; it is never answered by Decide for me and never by a role table; only an allow by the owner adds the profile to that step's grant (D298 for `github` is unchanged: a need plus an approval). A no cancels that step only (D315 b).
