J2's own builder tools (`WebFetch`, `WebSearch`, `Bash`, in its `builder_tools` list, D299) still reach the network from inside the CLI; Sutra's egress scrub covers the text of the prompts it sends, not what a tool sends or fetches, and the engine records the tool calls in `calls.jsonl` without scrubbing them (MAN-14 audits them). Package: MAN-8 (fix), MAN-14 (the audit).
- Why: a planner is a model reading the owner's words and files; letting it grant itself a read of every team, task and note is a model approving a model.
- Rejected: granting `sutra` by role (the role table gives it only up to the ceiling and it did, silently)
- Diagram: label "sutra tools only on the owner's yes", key only.
- Corrected by: D380 and D386 (a step's builder call has no web tool).

## D326 — The project page and the owner's route to start a project
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: (Accepted by D334 with a way back into a project added before a release.) The project page and the owner's route to start a project, as built (MAN-13). (a) `/p/{id}` is a page of its own with its own router (`ui.mount_project_page`, called by `create_app` only while `SUTRA_NODES_V1` is on and the project service is wired): with the flag off the route does not exist, no other page or route changes, and `ui.mount` still reads exactly the three members its pinned test names. An id that is not a project, or a project that is not there, is 404. (b) `POST /api/projects` takes `{client_id, text, budget?, failure_policy?, decide_for_me?}` (closed body; `failure_policy` is `stop_branch` or `stop_all`, default `stop_branch`; a request made in the chat is always `stop_branch`) and answers `202 {message_id, turn, duplicate: false}`, not the `201 ProjectOut` of the architecture: the plan is made after the route has answered (a planner call), and a project cannot exist before its plan. The request is the owner's own line in the org chat, so everything that holds for a chat request holds here (budget, Decide for me, the planner, the gate); `failure_policy` is kept in that line and read by `reserve`; `client_id` is the line's `src_ref` (`project:<client_id>`), so a repeated send answers `200 {duplicate: true, message_id}` and starts nothing. A plan the gate refuses is a `failed` project with its reason, found in `GET /api/projects`; the 409 and 422 of the table are not returned. (c) The page follows `project.*`, `node.updated` and `task.updated` through its own `EventSource` (`/api/events?topics=project,node,task`), because the shared client lists only the 18 names of the older pages and changing that moves their pinned test; events are invalidations (one refetch for a burst, 400 ms), the page asks every 3 s while the stream is down and every 20 s as a safety net, and messages are read from the last whole number (a gap is read again). (d) "Sutra decided for you" is the audit rows `gate.decided` whose reason starts `decided_by rule:decide_for_me`: no new route. (e) The note for an app that is not in the usual place is `oslayer.app_location()` and `GET /api/nodes/app-location` (`{usual, note}`; the words only, never a path; only an installed Mac app outside the Applications folders or a Windows install outside Program Files and the per-user Programs folder is judged). (f) A project question card in the chat has an "Open the project" link. (g) Not built: a page that lists the projects (the link and `GET /api/projects` are the ways in) and a browser smoke (by hand, in the beta). Package: MAN-13.
- Why: (a) the flag-off bytes and the pinned mount test must not move; (b) the honest answer of a route whose work is a model call; the chat path already has every rule; (c) the same reason as (a); (d) the audit already says it; (e) the owner needs the words, not the path.
- Rejected: a second planner call inside the route; a project created before its plan; adding the project names to `SutraEvents.TYPES`; reading the project list into Home's navigation (changes a flag-off page)
- Diagram: label "project page /p/id; POST /api/projects, 202", key only.

## D327 — The tool-call audit: names and kinds, never inputs
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: (Amended by D339: `tools.jsonl` is kept out of the hand-off's file list; what the audit cannot see must be said in the owner's documents.) The tool-call audit, as built (MAN-14). The router has an optional hook `on_tool(tag, name, kind, base_name)`, called for each tool call of a call whose tag names a node, only when a hook is set; every other call gets its `on_event` unchanged. The kinds are the six of `tool_kinds.classify` (unchanged) plus `sutra` and `github` for the app's own tools (`mcp__sutra__*`). Only a read or an edit keeps a target, and only the file's base name: a command, a query, a URL and the arguments of an app tool are never kept. The node's host appends one line to `tools.jsonl` in its run folder (at most 2000 lines, best effort: a log that cannot be written never stops a call); the Conductor reads it before the projector's transaction (`prepare_tools`, only when the file changed) and, when the node ends or is tried again, writes one audit row `tool.used` (actor `node`) for each different tool with its kind, the file and how many times (at most 60 rows, the rest counted in one row). The audit says that a tool was used, not what it carried: J2's own builder tools (`WebFetch`, `WebSearch`, `Bash`) still reach the network from inside the CLI and `calls.jsonl` still holds their inputs unscrubbed (D325). `HOST_FILES` is not changed, so `tools.jsonl` is not specially kept out of a follow-up run's copy; a node run has no follow-up. Package: MAN-14.
- Why: the owner asked what a node did; names and kinds answer it without a second copy of anything private.
- Rejected: the tool's input in the audit (a second place a secret could land); a row for each call (a busy node would fill the trail); a log inside the database (a write for every tool call)
- Diagram: label "tool audit: names + kinds, no inputs", key only.
- Corrected by: D383 (tool rows are written at the step's end and say so).

## D328 — Write-scope leases always, lanes behind a sub-flag
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Write-scope leases and lanes, as built (MAN-10, D323). Leases are the rows of `agent_nodes` (`writes_json`, the node's state), so they survive a restart and are read in the transaction that starts a node. A writer with no scope, or in a team whose output is `auto`, holds the whole folder and runs alone; a folder that is not a git repository cannot say who changed what, so writers run one at a time; a node that is `expired` still holds its lease until it is torn down. Scopes are compared without case and with `/` for `\` (Windows and the default macOS file system do not tell `Site` from `site`), and a scope that names `.git` is refused when it is made. These hold for every project with the master flag on: the words "runs alone" and "kept apart from other writers" are true now. `SUTRA_NODES_LANES` only lets two steps of one team run together: a step has its own lane (`tasks.lane`), a step waits only for its own lane and for an ordinary task of its team, an ordinary task waits for any live task of its team, `live_in` and `stop_live` look at lane `''`, and the unique index `(dept_id, lane)` is unchanged, so two live ordinary tasks of a team stay impossible. Package: MAN-10.
- Why: leases without the sub-flag keep the owner's folder safe; the sub-flag only adds parallelism.
- Rejected: a lease table of its own (a second copy of what a node row already says); locks on the folder
- Diagram: label "scope leases always; lanes = sub-flag", key only.

## D329 — Scope enforcement: a path nobody owns is a question
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: (Accepted by D336 with what the check cannot see written down, a visible "could not check" row, and a per-step check added before a release.) Scope enforcement, as built (MAN-10, D303, D317). The host's folder watch names the paths a node changed in the owner's folder and the hand-off carries them (`changes`, at most 100; the system probe's `.sutra-probe-*` file is never a change). A path no writer of the project owns is a scope violation; "owns" is the union of the scopes of all the project's writers (a writer with no scope owns the whole folder, so then nothing can be outside), not only the nodes that ran at the same time: the watch sees the folder, not which process wrote a file, so it cannot say which of two parallel writers wrote a path, and the union is the check that cannot blame the wrong step. The owner is asked (a chat card and the page): keep the work (allow once) or set it aside. While the question is open the step waits, the project is paused and nothing new starts. A no, and Decide for me (which only ever sets the work aside, never keeps it), quarantine the step: its files stay, nothing is handed on, its dependants are cancelled. The edit tools of a step's Claude program are narrowed to its scope (`PortSpec.scope`); the Codex connector cannot be narrowed that way, so it relies on the watch. A violation is a question, so a closed laptop refuses it like any other (D296). Package: MAN-10.
- Why: the folder is the owner's; a model that writes outside its lane is the one case where the code must not trust the model.
- Rejected: attributing a path to one of two parallel writers (the watch cannot know); failing the step without asking (the owner may want the work)
- Diagram: label "path nobody owns: ask, no = quarantine", key only.

## D330 — The reviewer: typed verdict, independent, the owner decides
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: (Amended by D338: independence is the Conductor's own fact, not the verdict file's.) The reviewer, as built (MAN-11, D256, D315, D317). With `SUTRA_NODES_REVIEWER` on, once every worker of a project is over and one finished, the Conductor makes one light node (runtime `light`, `python -m sutra_app.nodes`) with the finished steps' tasks as its upstream; the project is `reviewing` while it runs. It makes ONE model call, reads what the steps handed on and writes `verdict.json`, a closed typed object (`verdict` is `approve`, `changes` or `reject`; `needs_work` names steps `a1` to `a5`; at most 5 points of 200 characters): only the enum and the step names can affect anything, and the words are shown as plain text and never read as an instruction. Its grant is read-only (no write tier) and its output level is the strongest level among the steps it reviews. A verdict that is not the typed object ends the node `failed`. The reviewer is independent of the builders when it can be: the usage ledger says which providers built the work and a different installed, signed-in provider is picked; when there is none, the review says it is "not independent". `approve` changes nothing; `changes` and `reject` open ONE question for the owner (kind `review`, never answered by Decide for me): a yes sends the named steps back as a fresh attempt (two loops at most; after that a plain note says the reviewer still asks for changes and the project ends as it is), a no ends the project as it is. A model never approves a model and never orders work. Package: MAN-11.
- Why: a second opinion that cannot change anything alone is useful and safe; the owner keeps the decision.
- Rejected: letting a verdict redo work by itself (a model ordering a model); a reviewer that edits; the same provider with no word about it
- Diagram: label "reviewer verdict informs; owner decides", key only.

## D331 — Provider slots, heartbeat coalescing and the load numbers
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Provider slots, heartbeat coalescing and the load numbers, as built (MAN-12, D320). With `SUTRA_NODES_SLOTS` on, a small file for each slot of each provider lives under `<data>/connectors/slots`; a process takes a slot by locking a file (`oslayer.try_lock`: `flock`, or `msvcrt.locking` on Windows), so the limit of a provider is shared by every process of the machine and a process that dies frees its slot at once. The router's own semaphore stays and is taken first. The server's router gets its pool in the wiring (the connector factory is pinned not to read extra settings) and a node's router gets it from its port spec. A node's heartbeat-only batch that arrives within 15 s of its last event writes nothing (a lease is 90 s; the same lines are read again later, which is harmless because every projection is idempotent); ordinary tasks are untouched. Measured (fakes, one machine, `tests/orchestrator/test_nodes_load.py`): 24 stand-in nodes for 40 rounds on 8 threads made 960 writes to `tasks` without coalescing and 72 with it; `StoreBusy` 0; the event bus (ring 1000) had no gap for a page that replays after every round; a hand-off was taken in 0.046 s at most. These numbers are not those of real model runs. Package: MAN-12.
- Why: a limit per process lets two processes double the provider's calls; a heartbeat every few seconds from every node is most of the writes.
- Rejected: a lock service in the server (it would not survive the server's restart while nodes still run); a window in the projector for all events (delays a state change)
- Diagram: label "slots by file lock; 960 -> 72 writes", key only.

## D332 — The phase 3 follow-up is built as ruled, with limits written down
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The phase 3 follow-up is built as ruled, with limits written down. G12 to G22, P1, P2, P3, D324 and D325 are in the code and match D315 to D325. Four things the owner must know are kept as decisions and not as open items. (a) A "credit" for a retry is one model call in the usage ledger (D324 says so); J2 does not report its own credits to the server, so the credit ceiling is approximate and the real hard bounds are the per-step caps (60 calls, 30 minutes, one retry), the project's wall time and the owner's own dollar limits. (b) Decide for me answers a read-only GitHub question by rule ("allow for this project", D319); it never answers the app's own data (`sutra_tools`), a permission of the computer, a scope violation by keeping the work, a review question or a plan over `project_credits`. (c) A step that asks for the app's own data waits for the owner's yes (D325) and a no cancels only that step. (d) `make_slices` with no ceiling (the owner gave a budget, or approved the estimate) makes the ceiling the sum of the slices, which is what D324 meant. Package: MAN-8, MAN-9 (done).
- Why: the built code does what the rulings said; what is thin is written here so the owner is not surprised.
- Rejected: waiting for J2's own credit count before a beta (nothing would be bounded by credits, but calls, minutes and dollars already bound it)
- Diagram: label "follow-up as built; credit = one call", key only.

## D333 — m6: iCloud Drive and volumes are skipped for the beta, built before a release
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: m6 (iCloud Drive and removable or network volumes are not probed) is accepted for the beta and built before a release. The late refusal (D322 g: `needs_system_permission`, "Check again", one fresh attempt that spends no retry) is the net: a step that is refused starts, fails and then asks, so nothing is silent. Its limit is real: it sees only a refusal that J2 reports as a Python OSError (`[Errno 1]`, `[Errno 13]`, `[WinError 5]`, `PermissionError`); a tool the Claude or Codex program is refused inside the program is an ordinary failure and the step is tried once more. Before a release the probe also looks at `~/Library/Mobile Documents` (iCloud Drive) and at a folder under `/Volumes` when the owner's working folder is there, with the same temporary file and the same 45 s limit. The Windows probe still tests Sutra's own process and not the `claude` and `codex` programs (the manual check P5 must say whether Controlled folder access blocks them). Package: MAN-15 (R1).
- Why: a wasted first start is a cost, not a danger, and the probe code is small to extend; the owner's working folder in iCloud Drive or on an external disk is not rare enough to leave out of a release.
- Rejected: probing every mounted volume (slow and not the owner's choice); keeping m6 skipped for ever
- Diagram: label "m6 skipped for beta, built for release", key only.

## D334 — The route to start a project and the page are accepted; a way back is added
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The route to start a project and the page, as built, are accepted. (a) `POST /api/projects` answers `202 {message_id, turn, duplicate: false}` (not `201 ProjectOut`): the project is made after a planner call, so it cannot exist at answer time. The architecture table is changed. The caller finds the project in `GET /api/projects` (newest first) and from the question card's link; `ProjectOut.request_id` is the engine's own request id and is not the caller's handle. (b) The page is mounted by `ui.mount_project_page`, only while `SUTRA_NODES_V1` is on, so the pinned three-member `ui.mount` and every flag-off page keep their bytes. It follows its own `EventSource` on `project`, `node` and `task` events because the shared client lists only the older pages' names. (c) A scope violation is "a path no writer of the project owns" (D336). (d) Not accepted as final: there is no list of projects and the page is found only through a question card's link or by typing its address. Before a release the receipt notes in the org chat (`project finished`, `project stopped`) carry the same "Open the project" link and `GET /api/projects` takes a `client_id` filter that finds the project a `POST` started. No new item in the navigation is added. Package: MAN-13 (R6).
- Why: a route must not promise a thing that does not exist yet; the mount rule keeps flag-off pages as they were; an owner who closes the tab must be able to find the project again.
- Rejected: a second planner call inside the route; a project made before its plan; a Projects item in the navigation (a flag-off page changes)
- Diagram: label "202 accepted; way back before release", key only.

## D335 — No grant gate after a needs.json in v1
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The grant gate after a `needs.json` is not built in v1, and that is accepted. No node can ask for more: an engine node's hand-off may not carry `needs` (it is refused), and the one light node (the reviewer) has a fixed read-only grant and no tool. So "allow once" and "allow for this project" mean the same thing everywhere in v1 (D315 d), and the second gate of D270 moves to Non-goals for v1 with the raise card (D320 b). The note was missing from the developer's notes and is added (N65 in `dev-notes.md` points here). A hand-off `status` of `blocked` from a light node does not exist either: the reviewer ends `done` or `crashed`. Package: MAN-11.
- Why: a gate with nobody to ask it is dead code that a test would have to keep alive.
- Rejected: building the gate for a producer that is not there
- Diagram: label "no grant gate in v1: nobody can ask", key only.

## D336 — What the scope check sees, and what it does not
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Scope enforcement, as built, is accepted for a flag-on beta, and what it can and cannot see is written down. (a) The check is made when a writer's hand-off is read. The host's folder watch is `git status` before and after the run (`folder_watch.py`), taken only for a run whose output delivers files; it is never what an agent says. A path the node changed that no writer of the project owns is a violation; "owns" is the union of the scopes of the project's writers (a writer with no scope owns the whole folder, so nothing is then outside). The union is used because the watch cannot say which of two parallel writers wrote a path. (b) What it cannot see: a folder that is not a git work tree (then writers run one at a time, but nothing is checked); a status of more than 5000 entries; git slow, missing or refusing the folder; a path that git ignores (a `.env` or `node_modules` that is in `.gitignore`); more than 100 changed paths (the rest are counted, not named); a node whose output does not deliver files. In all of these the check says nothing today. G26 makes it say so: the node's hand-off gets an audit row `scope.unchecked` and the page shows "Sutra could not check which files this step changed". (c) What it does after a violation: the project is paused (nothing new starts; a writer that already runs is not stopped; this amends the failure table N6), a question is asked in the chat and on the page; "keep" lets the work go on, "no" and Decide for me set it aside (its files stay in the owner's folder and nothing is handed on; Sutra does not undo, move or delete a file). (d) A step that writes inside the scope of ANOTHER step of the same project is not caught by this check. For a Claude step the edit tools are narrowed to its scope (`Edit(path)` and `Edit(path/**)` rules, Bash only for the four git reads), so the program itself refuses; a Codex step has no such narrowing and relies on the watch. The old mitigation "a multi-path writer is routed to Claude" was never built and is removed from the design. (e) Before a release a step that ran with no other writer alive in its time window is checked against its OWN scope (the per-step check D261 meant), and the union is kept only for steps that overlapped in time. Package: MAN-10 (G26, R2, R4).
- Why: the beta runs on the owner's own computer in a folder the owner chose and allowed to be changed (the same reach an ordinary task has today); the check is a second wall and its gaps must not look like safety.
- Rejected: attributing a path to one of two parallel writers (cannot be known); stopping every writer at once on a violation (the owner may want the work); routing scoped writers to Claude without being asked (a provider switch nobody chose)
- Diagram: label "scope check = git only; says so when blind", key only.

## D337 — Leases cover the steps of projects only
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Leases cover the steps of projects, and only them. A writer's lease is read from `agent_nodes` and held until its node is torn down; a writer with no scope or in a team whose output is `auto` holds the whole folder; in a folder that is not a git work tree writers wait for each other (D328). An ordinary task of ANOTHER team has no lease: such a task may change the same folder while a project's step does, as two ordinary tasks of two teams may today. So "runs alone" and "kept apart from other writers" mean "other steps of projects". The owner is told this in `docs/OPERATIONS.md`. Before a release one of two things is done and written down: a project's writer waits while a live ordinary task of a team that may write exists (and the reverse, only while a project writer is live), or the words in the audit row (`kept apart from other writers`) and in every owner document say "other steps of projects". Package: MAN-10 (R3).
- Why: the flag-off scheduler must stay as it is (D312 d), so the first choice changes ordinary tasks only when a project exists; the beta does not need it if the owner knows.
- Rejected: leasing ordinary tasks always (changes flag-off behaviour)
- Diagram: label "leases among project steps only", key only.

## D338 — The reviewer is accepted; independence is the Conductor's fact
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The reviewer, as built, is accepted with two changes. (a) Independence is the Conductor's own fact (`projects.caps_json.reviewer_independent`, set from the usage ledger when the reviewer is made), and the card, the page and the audit read that; the verdict file's `independent` flag, which the host guesses from `connector != "auto"`, is dropped (G25). A reviewer on the same program as the builders is always "not independent", whatever the owner's connector setting says. (b) A verdict of `reject` is `changes` in effect: one question, never answered by Decide for me; a yes sends the named steps back; a no ends the project as it is; nothing is cancelled by a verdict. When the verdict names no step, no question is asked for "send back" and the owner reads the words on the page (R5). The reviewer has no folder, no tools and one model call (it reads only the checked and scrubbed hand-offs), so it is read-only by construction, not by a rule. `approve` is shown as "The reviewer's opinion" and never as an approval; the project ends `done` or `done_with_open_items` by its steps, not by the opinion. Package: MAN-11 (G25, R5).
- Why: a label that says "independent" must come from the program that picked the provider; a verdict is an opinion.
- Rejected: a reviewer that cancels dependants (a model ordering a model)
- Diagram: label "reviewer independence from the Conductor", key only.

## D339 — The tool audit and the network: acceptable for a beta if said
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The tool-call audit and the network, as built, are acceptable for a beta if the owner is told in plain words, and the owner documents say it (OPERATIONS.md, section "Multi-agent projects: turning them on"). What the audit holds: a tool's name, its kind, a file's base name and how many times; never a command, a query, a URL or an argument. What it does not cover: J2's own builder tools (`WebFetch`, `WebSearch`, `Bash`) still reach the network from inside the Claude or Codex program, as they do in an ordinary run today; the egress check of an engine step's own prompts only OBSERVES (J2's prompts are not ours to edit: the text goes out byte-identical and an audit row counts the secret shapes), so a secret that a step reads from the owner's folder reaches the model program as it does in an ordinary run; Sutra REPLACES secrets only in the planner's and the reviewer's text, in what steps hand to each other and in the record; and nothing covers what a tool sends or fetches; `calls.jsonl` in the run folder keeps tool inputs unscrubbed (it stays on the computer and is not handed on). This is no new reach compared with an ordinary run, but a project runs several steps unattended, so it is said. G23: `tools.jsonl` is added to the host's `NOT_HANDED_ON` list (it was handed on as a file of the step, found in this review); `HOST_FILES` (the follow-up copy) stays unchanged. Before a release the release note repeats the limits, and the owner decides whether to scrub `calls.jsonl` when a node ends (R11). Package: MAN-14 (G23).
- Why: the audit says that a tool was used, and a second copy of what it carried would be a second place for a secret.
- Rejected: keeping tool inputs in the audit; hiding the limit because it is not new
- Diagram: label "tool audit and network: say it", key only.
- Corrected by: D380 and D386 (project steps have no Bash, WebSearch or WebFetch).

## D340 — The flag-off promise holds, with two precisions
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The flag-off promise holds in design terms, with two precisions found in this review. (a) D254's "no new column is used" means no new column changes a result: `tasks.lane` is filtered on (`lane = ''`) by `live_in`, `stop_live`, `deliver_to_department` and `_prepare_next`, as D258 designed, and is always `''` with the flag off, so every answer is the one it was. (b) The engine host's `permission_outcome` (the "Your computer did not allow it" prefix) runs for every run, so an ordinary run that crashes with a refusal of the computer would show a different crash text with the flag off. It is limited to node runs (G24). Checked and kept: no nav item, no page, no route (the whole projects router and the project page answer 404 or do not exist), `task.json` without a `node` block, no prompt or schema of the chat agent moved (the node plan family and the `project_*` lines are used only with the flag on), the `ChatCard` and `NeedsYouOut` additions are on purpose (D317) and no flag-off row can carry them, `PortSpec.scope` and `slots` are empty for every run that is not a node. P11 holds: with lanes a team still has at most one live ordinary task (the unique index `(dept_id, lane)` is unchanged, an ordinary task waits for any live task of its team, a step in a lane only for its own lane and an ordinary task). Package: MAN-15 (G24).
- Why: "byte-identical" is the promise and a text of a crash is a byte.
- Rejected: leaving the prefix for all runs (an improvement nobody asked for in a path that is not the feature's)
- Diagram: label "flag-off holds: lane filter, crash prefix", key only.

## D341 — What a flag-on beta is, and what stays true in every release
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: What a flag-on beta is, and what stays true in every release. The flag `SUTRA_NODES_V1` and the three sub-flags stay `0` in every release and every default; a beta turns the master on by the owner's own hand (an environment setting or `config`), in the beta app and not in the stable one (`sutra-pro/AGENTS.md`). A beta is safe to try when G23 to G26 are done, with these conditions, which the owner documents repeat: the working folder is a git folder with its work committed; the owner has set a dollar limit; a project of two to five steps is tried first; the owner watches the first projects; Stop everything and `sutra-pro nodes kill` are known. A release waits for R1 to R12, P4, P5, P8 and P9 and for the owner's go-ahead. Nothing in this design is called verified that was only tested with fakes: the list is in `dev-notes.md` (N51, N58, N59 to N64) and in the OPERATIONS note. Package: all.
- Why: the owner asked for a flag-on beta to try and for every release to stay as it is; the conditions are what makes the risks that are known small.
- Rejected: a beta with no conditions; blocking the beta on the live checks (they need the beta to be done)
- Diagram: label "beta conditions; flag 0 in every release", key only.

## D342 — The Codex limit is kept and told
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The Codex limit is kept and told (R4, the owner's ruling). A Codex step has no limit on its editing tools inside the program (a Claude step has: `Edit(path)` and `Edit(path/**)` rules). For a Codex step the only wall is the check after the run, which names what git saw (D336, D345 d). Scoped writers are NOT routed to Claude: that would switch a provider nobody chose. The release note and `docs/OPERATIONS.md` (section 12) say it in plain words: "A step that runs through Codex can change files outside its own place while it works; Sutra finds out after it ends and asks you."**
- Why: a silent provider switch costs the owner their choice; the check and the question already exist.
- Rejected: routing scoped writers to Claude when both programs are signed in; refusing Codex for a scoped writer. Package: MAN-10 (R4).
- Diagram: label "Codex limit kept and told", key only.

## D343 — Credits are not reported by J2 and no reporting is built
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Credits are not reported by J2, and no reporting is built (R8, the owner's ruling). A credit that a retry's slice is measured in is one model call in the usage ledger (D324, D332 a); the hard bounds are the per-step caps (60 calls, 30 minutes, one retry), the project's wall time (a sleeping computer is left out, D345 h) and the owner's dollar limit. The release note says in plain words: "When a step is tried again, one model call counts as one credit; your dollar limit is the number that holds."**
- Why: J2 is vendored and reports nothing of the kind; calls, minutes and dollars already bound a project.
- Rejected: changing the vendored engine to report credits. Package: MAN-9 (R8).
- Diagram: label "credits not reported; one call = one credit", key only.

## D344 — Tool inputs are removed from a step's record when its run ends
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The inputs of a step's tool calls are removed from its record when its run ends (R11, the owner's ruling). For a run that is a node of a project (flag on only), the host empties `tools.calls[*].input` in `calls.jsonl` and in `snapshot.json` (`call_log`) after J2 has finished with them; the number, the name, the result and every other field stay; a link or a damaged file is left as it was; an ordinary run is never touched (a test compares the bytes). It is done by the host, on the files, so the vendored engine is not changed. What it does not cover: the live view while the step still runs (`agent_live.json`), the prompts and replies (`calls.jsonl` keeps those as for any run), and anything a tool fetched or sent. The release note says so.**
- Why: an input can be a command or a URL with something private in it, and nothing scrubbed it; the audit already lists names and kinds (D327).
- Rejected: scrubbing the inputs (patterns miss things); leaving them (D325 said so, the owner now wants them gone). Package: MAN-14 (R11).
- Diagram: label "tool inputs removed at the end of a step", key only.

## D345 — The fixes of the phase 4 review, as built
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The fixes of the phase 4 review, as built. (a) The scope question's hold is not the owner's pause: `Continue` is refused with a plain 409 while a scope question is open, and the gate holds a step whose upstream step is `awaiting_approval`, so a dependant never gets the work of a step the owner has not kept or set aside (PB-1). (b) The reviewer's host finds the data folder from its run folder (`DataPaths.from_run_dir`, never the environment), passes the usage sink so its call is a usage row of the project, and takes slots; the card says "not independent" from `projects.caps_json.reviewer_independent` only, and the verdict file no longer has an `independent` field (PB-2, G25). (c) The reviewer's question is advice: when it is refused, runs out, or meets a sleep or a restart it counts as a no and the project ends as it is, never `stopped`; `allow_project` on it is 422; a verdict that names no step asks nothing and leaves a note; `approve` is worded as an opinion in the chat and on the page; a reviewer that could not run leaves the project `done` with "The review could not run, so nobody has checked the work" (PB-3, PB-11, PB-13, R5). (d) The scope check reads every changed path up to 5000 from the host's own `changes.json` (outside the files a step hands on, with the first 100 and a count in the hand-off); a step that ran alone (no other writer alive in its time window, writers of other projects included) is checked against its own scope, steps that overlapped against the union; when the check cannot be made (no list, a partial list, no scope of its own, a neighbour with the whole folder, a link that leaves the folder) an audit row `scope.unchecked` says why in plain words and nothing is held (PB-4, G26, R2). (e) Scopes compare an accent written either way (NFC) and without case as one place; a link inside the folder is followed and one that leaves it makes the scope the whole folder (a moment in time: a link made later is not followed); `!`, `(` and `)` are refused in a scope (PB-17). (f) The tool log is `<data>/nodes/tools/<run id>.jsonl`, outside the step's own folder, made safe again when read; it is a record kept by the step's host, not a proof: a step with a shell runs as the same person and could still reach it (PB-6). (g) No file is read inside a write transaction: the folder setting is read once in 2 s and the tool rows of a node that was not read ahead are written by the next tick (PB-16). (h) A gap the tick finds (the computer asleep) is written as `project.slept` and left out of the wall time; a closed app is not (the tick's clock is in memory) (PB-12). (i) A step keeps its lane for every attempt, and the reviewer is in the lane of the first step it checks (PB-5). (j) A stop, a kill or a closed-laptop refusal ends a step held for a scope question (PB-10). (k) Slots: the waiters of one process are served in order, and every waiter looks again after a random short pause (a file lock has no queue between processes); a wait that gave up is not a call for the node's meter; interactive chat calls are NOT given a slot of their own (no decision asks for it): with `SUTRA_NODES_SLOTS` on, a chat turn may wait behind long step calls (PB-14). (l) The ceiling is the number the owner agreed to; the dead `min(sum, cap)` scaling in `reserve` is removed (PA-2). (m) The words: no "node", "lease", "ceiling", "hand-off" or machine rule name in what the owner reads; "other writers" is "other steps of projects" everywhere (leases look at project steps only, D337; no waiting between teams is added) (m13, R3, R10). (n) The Mac probe also looks at iCloud Drive and at a folder on another disk; with Controlled folder access on, Windows says once that the programs `claude` and `codex` are judged apart from Sutra Pro (a notice, not a question: Sutra cannot test them without running a model) (R1, R10). (o) A note about a project ends with its page address, shown in the chat as an "Open the project" link; a finished project leaves one note; `GET /api/projects?client_id=` finds the project a `POST` started (R6). (p) `home.js` and `chat_view.js` differ in bytes with the flag off: the added code does nothing unless a `node_approval` card or a note with a project address exists, and tests show both (PB-19). (q) The G24 prefix is written by node runs only; an ordinary run's crash text is what it was. Package: MAN-8, MAN-10, MAN-11, MAN-12, MAN-14.
- Why: each answers a finding of the review with the smallest change that closes it, and records the limit that is left.
- Rejected: a second hold flag separate from `paused` (the gate already holds the dependants, and Continue is refused); a cross-process queue for slots; reading the tool log through a pipe the step cannot reach (the same person could still reach it); routing steps to a provider.
- Diagram: label "phase 4 review fixes as built", key only.

## D346 — The nodes fix round: the deviations are accepted, four limits are written down
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, dev-notes.md, build-plan.md, beta-run-checklist.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The deviations of the fix round (D342 to D345) are accepted, with four limits written down. (a) The tool log is `<data>/nodes/tools/<run id>.jsonl`, outside the step's own folder (PB-6): accepted as a record kept by the step's own host and not a proof (a step with a shell runs as the same person); nothing removes the files yet (F-3, small, before a release). (b) R11 is done by the host on the files when it ends the run (D344): accepted, the vendored engine stays as it is. The limit, now written in the owner documents: a host that is killed hard or crashes hard does not run it, so a step stopped by force may keep its tool inputs; F-1 (the Conductor also empties them at the node's end, outside any transaction, idempotent) is a release item; the real shape of `tools.calls[*].input` in a real Claude run was matched to J2's source and not seen (check C10). (c) PB-12 is accepted: the sleep the tick sees is left out of the project's wall time and a closed app is not; the error runs toward ending a project sooner, never toward spending more. (d) PB-14 is accepted: no reserved slot for interactive chat; the pool is 4 for Claude and 2 for Codex, so a project of two to five steps cannot hold every Claude slot and a Codex chat turn can wait behind two Codex steps; if the beta shows a chat turn waiting more than 10 seconds, the reserve is built (F-2). (e) PB-19 is accepted after reading the diff: `projectAddress` rewrites only the exact shape `/p/prj_` and 12 hex digits, `projectLink` runs only for a `node_approval` card, and no test pins these two files' bytes; after the Ask branch (which also changes both files) meets them, `chat_view_node_approval.test.js` and `chat_ask.test.js` run together. (f) R1 is accepted with fakes: it covers iCloud Drive and a folder under `/Volumes`, and the owner words say only that; other cloud-file folders (for example `~/Library/CloudStorage`) are not known and are not claimed (P5 will show). Found and corrected in the documents in this check: the D342 to D344 sections of `DECISIONS.md` carried the text of the decisions after them; `OPERATIONS.md` still listed `tools.jsonl` in the run folder; `guardrails-and-permissions.md` still said `calls.jsonl` keeps tool inputs. "Before publish" is restated in three groups: closed (stand-ins only), what the first real-model run can close, what waits for a release.
- Why: each deviation was chosen for a reason the design shares (the simplest thing that closes the finding), and each has a limit that the owner would otherwise find out by surprise; the limits are now in the documents and each has an owner and a trigger.
- Rejected: re-opening the deviations (none breaks a rule of the design); marking the boxes of R2, R4, R8 and R12 as seen (they are built or decided, and each still has a live or a release-time check).
- Diagram: label "fix-round deviations accepted; limits written", key only.

## D347 — The first real-model beta run: go, with conditions and the checks
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, dev-notes.md, build-plan.md, beta-run-checklist.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: A flag-on beta with a real Claude and a small read-only project is a GO, under conditions; a release is not. Conditions: a throw-away git folder with its work committed; the working folder's access in Settings is **Read only** (the owner's access caps every step, so the wall is a setting the owner made and not the words of a request); a low dollar limit (suggest $3); Claude signed in; the beta app built from the nodes branch (the code of `24c7c1be` to `c138f633`) with `SUTRA_NODES_V1=1` and `SUTRA_NODES_SLOTS=1`, the reviewer, lanes and every Devin-parity flag at `0` in the first run; the owner at the computer; Decide for me off in the first run. Three runs: the two-step request (checks C1 to C11); Stop everything, the kill switch, Decide for me on and then the reviewer (C12 to C14); a closed-app restart (C15). The checks, the commands, what is expected and the abort rules are in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/beta-run-checklist.md`. A pass says only that the feature worked once, on a Mac, with Claude, on a read-only folder; it does not clear writers, Codex, Windows, lanes or a release. Not part of this run, and still open: P4 (a document and a website run), P5 on Windows, R7 (the rest), R9, R12, M1, F-1 to F-3, the integration gate.
- Why: the fix round closed G23 to G26 and every medium finding; what is left cannot be learned with stand-ins (a real planner, a real tool-call shape, a real process, a real stop, a real restart, a real cost); read-only access, a git folder and a dollar limit keep the worst case to a few dollars and nothing changed.
- Rejected: waiting for P4, P5 and R7 before any real run (they need the beta to be done: D341); a first run with a writer (the scope check sees only what git sees, D336); the reviewer and lanes in the first run (more unknowns in one run than one person can watch).
- Diagram: label "real-Claude beta: go, read-only, 3 runs", key only.

## D360 — The Work list, tags and How did it go? (DV-2)
- Sources: `docs/design/devin-parity.md` and `docs/design/devin-parity-dev-notes-dv2-dv3.md`; the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (take inspiration from Devin, decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The Work list, tags and "How did it go?" are built behind `SUTRA_WORK_V1`: one list of tasks and (with the nodes) projects, tags as the owner's plain words through the note gate, and a project summary computed by code; no navigation item, no model sentence.
- Why: the owner wants one place to find work and to see what a project cost and where it stuck, without a model guessing and without a changed Home page.
- Rejected: a model-written summary (quota, and a model's guess where code can count); a navigation item (changes the flag-off Home).
- Diagram: label "work list + tags + code-made summary (DV-2)", key only.

## D361 — Recipes (DV-3)
- Sources: `docs/design/devin-parity.md` and `docs/design/devin-parity-dev-notes-dv2-dv3.md`; the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (take inspiration from Devin, decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Recipes are built behind `SUTRA_RECIPES_V1`: six parts and up to nine blanks, every line through the note gate, versions that only grow (triggers), four read-only starters in code, Markdown in and out by the owner's action (an import is a draft), a code-made draft from a project, and starting writes a plain request that is planned and asked like any request.
- Why: a way of working the owner saved should start again with one click without freezing a plan or skipping a question; only the owner writes a recipe, so it is not a door for injected text.
- Rejected: a frozen graph (it would skip the plan gate); a standing rule made from Forbidden (a rule the owner did not choose); a model that drafts and saves a recipe.
- Diagram: label "recipes: owner-written, versions only grow (DV-3)", key only.

## D362 — Rules for the Devin-parity packages
- Sources: `docs/design/devin-parity.md` and `docs/design/devin-parity-dev-notes-dv2-dv3.md`; the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (take inspiration from Devin, decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Each Devin-parity package has its own flag (default 0); its routes and page are mounted only while it is on (404 before any token check otherwise); its migration is additive and in order; one `db.tx()` writer; owner words only; pure view plus controller with node tests.
- Why: flag off must be byte-identical: no route, no table read, no page, no nav item, no prompt or golden change.
- Rejected: routes always mounted with a guard (they would be in the route table with the flag off).
- Diagram: label "flag per package; routes mounted only when on", key only.

## D363 — DV-2 and DV-3 accepted with changes; the order and the gate of DV-4 and DV-5
- Sources: `docs/design/devin-parity.md`, `docs/design/devin-parity-dev-notes-dv2-dv3.md` and `docs/design/merge-plan-nodes-devin.md`; the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (take inspiration from Devin, decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: DV-2 and DV-3 as built are accepted with changes; the five departures from `devin-parity.md` stand, two with a condition. (1) No navigation item: accepted, but D360 d says the pages are reached from links on a project's note and card and the code has no such link, and the desktop window has no address bar, so the pages cannot be reached in the app; DV-2b and DV-3b add a way in that exists only with a flag on: at serve time the server adds one line of links to the Home HTML, with both flags off the served bytes are what they were and a test compares them. (2) No model sentence and no model draft: accepted (the sentence was optional and off by default; a model draft would put a model's words where a model must not write); the code draft is shallow and says so. (3) "Forbidden" as "Never do these" lines: accepted, better than a standing rule the owner did not choose; the page says it is told to the planner and is not a lock, and a test shows that starting a recipe adds no standing rule. (4) The summary on `/work`: accepted; its minutes are worded "from the first start to the end" (they include any time the computer slept); a link on the project page shows only when the route answers. (5) The starters in code: accepted; the list says "cannot be changed", not "read only". Also: the "newer database" test pins `user_version = 9`; it uses `SCHEMA_VERSION + 1`. Nothing of DV-2 or DV-3 was seen in a real browser or with a real planner. Order: DV-1 (finish), DV-2b and DV-3b, then DV-4 and DV-5, then DV-6, DV-12, DV-7, DV-11, DV-10, DV-9, DV-8; DV-13 not now. DV-4 and DV-5 start only when the nodes' first real-model run has passed, the nodes and DV-1 are on `main`, and (DV-4) the first real P4 numbers or (DV-5) a real reviewer run are written down.
- Why: the departures keep the flag-off promise and the rule that a model never approves a model; the missing way in would leave two finished pages that nobody in the app can open.
- Rejected: a navigation item (changes the flag-off Home); a model sentence and a model draft (quota, and a model's words in the owner's place); a standing rule made by a recipe; starters as seed rows (a sync nobody needs).
- Diagram: label "DV-2/DV-3 accepted with changes; way in needed", key only.

## D364 — Merge and release order, conflicts, decision blocks
- Sources: `docs/design/devin-parity.md`, `docs/design/devin-parity-dev-notes-dv2-dv3.md` and `docs/design/merge-plan-nodes-devin.md`; the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (take inspiration from Devin, decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Merge and release order, conflicts and decision numbers (`docs/design/merge-plan-nodes-devin.md`). Order: nothing lands now; a throw-away integration rehearsal once DV-1 is finished; nodes lands first after its real-model run passed, the gate and CI; the Devin-parity branch second (stacked, a fast-forward); DV-1 when ready (no migration, no dependency), the later of them merging `origin/main` first; one release for all three, flags `0`, never Latest, beta first, after M1, R12, P9 and the owner's word. Conflicts are in registers, flags and one union type, not in a migration, a route or a service: `DECISIONS.md`, `decisions.json`, `ENVIRONMENT.md`, `OPERATIONS.md` (Ask becomes section 14), `config.py`, `envfile.py`, `test_config.py`, `models.py` (both cards in the union), `chat_view.js`; the two traceability files are regenerated and never merged by hand. The parity dev-notes file is renamed `devin-parity-dev-notes-dv2-dv3.md` so it does not meet the Ask branch's file of the same name. Decision numbers are given in blocks (D346 to D349 nodes, D350 to D359 DV-1, D360 to D379 the rest of Devin parity); the gaps matter to no test (headings are compared as sets, traceability wants a row per heading); no renumbering is ever needed; the only trap is a heading below the highest recorded id with no traceability row, so the generated files are rebuilt after every merge. The developer of the branch that lands later resolves; the architect reads the registers.
- Why: main stays releasable; one release is one one-way door for the owner's data and three are three; blocks keep numbers unique without anyone renumbering.
- Rejected: landing DV-1 first by default (it is not ready); three releases; renumbering at merge time; resolving the generated files by hand.
- Diagram: label "merge order; decision blocks; one release", key only.

## D348 — The wall time leaves out time once, and a closed app counts as a sleep
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The wall time leaves out time once, and a closed app counts as a sleep (N-1, N-2). `_paused_seconds` takes the UNION of the intervals of the pauses (`project.paused` to `project.resumed`, one still on counts until now) and of the gaps (`project.slept`: its `target` is when the gap ended, its `ref` the seconds), clipped to the time since the first start, so a project paused overnight with the laptop closed is away once. A closed Sutra is found at restart (`reconcile_projects`): one `project.slept` row with the time from the project's newest audit row or task event to now, so the first tick does not stop a restarted project with "ran longer than 120 minutes". The dollar limit and the per-step caps are not affected. A tick gap over half a lease that is not a sleep (the server's loop stalled) is still read as one: the rule D311 already used for leases.**
Why: the project's promise is that its limit is about time spent working; counting a gap twice stretched it, not counting a closed app stopped a project the restart rule had just revived. Rejected: not writing `project.slept` while paused (the sleep is true either way and the page shows it); a gap kept in memory (lost at restart). Package: MAN-7, MAN-9.

**D349. Housekeeping of the tool records (N-7, N-15, F-1). (a) The tool log of a run is removed by `sutra-pro prune` with the run's other bulky evidence (finished, older than the age, default 90 days; a young, open or unlisted run's log stays). (b) The server empties the tool inputs of a step's record when its task is over, outside any transaction, for every node that ended (the host does it for a step that ends normally; this covers a step that was force-stopped, killed or crashed, and at restart the nodes that ended in the last 7 days); a file with no input left is not rewritten. (c) A tool list owed to the audit is a `tool.pending` row written in the node's transaction and closed by a `tool.logged` row, so a rollback takes it back, a restart finds it, and a repeat writes nothing twice. Package: MAN-14.
- Why: the files grow without end; R11 should hold when the host did not get to run; an in-memory debt is lost.
- Rejected: deleting the log at the node's end (the audit may not have been written yet); a database table for the debt.
- Diagram: label "wall time: union of pauses and gaps; closed app = sleep", key only.

## D349 — Housekeeping of the tool records: prune, a server sweep, a durable debt
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Housekeeping of the tool records (N-7, N-15, F-1). (a) The tool log of a run is removed by `sutra-pro prune` with the run's other bulky evidence (finished, older than the age, default 90 days; a young, open or unlisted run's log stays). (b) The server empties the tool inputs of a step's record when its task is over, outside any transaction, for every node that ended (the host does it for a step that ends normally; this covers a step that was force-stopped, killed or crashed, and at restart the nodes that ended in the last 7 days); a file with no input left is not rewritten. (c) A tool list owed to the audit is a `tool.pending` row written in the node's transaction and closed by a `tool.logged` row, so a rollback takes it back, a restart finds it, and a repeat writes nothing twice. Package: MAN-14.
- Why: the files grow without end; R11 should hold when the host did not get to run; an in-memory debt is lost.
- Rejected: deleting the log at the node's end (the audit may not have been written yet); a database table for the debt.
- Diagram: label "tool records: prune, sweep, durable debt", key only.

## D365 — The review round of DV-2 and DV-3, as built
- Sources: `docs/design/devin-parity.md`, `docs/design/devin-parity-dev-notes-dv2-dv3.md` and `docs/design/merge-plan-nodes-devin.md`; the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (take inspiration from Devin, decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The review round of DV-2 and DV-3 is built as listed in `devin-parity-dev-notes-dv2-dv3.md`: the Work list hides every attempt of a retried step and is filtered and paged by the database; the summary counts minutes from the first attempt, names steps by their title and says real retries; a tag is checked as typed and never echoed; Recipes refuse hidden text and a recipe that could never start, write a dollar as `$$`, list with one query and save as a file in any language; the page says a recipe is words, not a lock. The six static files are served with the switches off (nothing loads them).
- Why: each finding is a way the owner would meet a wrong list, a 500, or text they cannot see; each fix is the smallest that closes it.
- Rejected: hiding only a step's current task; refusing hidden text in the shared notes gate (it would change the Notes and Rules pages with the switches off).
- Diagram: label "DV-2/DV-3 review fixes as built", key only.

## D366 — Home gets a row for Work and Recipes at serve time
- Sources: `docs/design/devin-parity.md`, `docs/design/devin-parity-dev-notes-dv2-dv3.md` and `docs/design/merge-plan-nodes-devin.md`; the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (take inspiration from Devin, decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The server adds one row for each mounted page to Home's "Logs & debug" group when Home is served (`add_home_links`, the links registered by `mount_work_page` and `mount_recipes_page` in `app.state.home_links`); with both switches off Home is served exactly as the file says (a test strips the two rows and compares the bytes). The desktop window has no address bar, so a page that exists must be reachable.
- Why: a page nobody can open is not a feature; a fixed row in the file would change the flag-off page.
- Rejected: a row in `home.html` hidden by a script (the bytes change, a request fails with the switch off).
- Diagram: label "Home rows for Work/Recipes, only while mounted", key only.

## D367 — Hidden text is refused in recipes and tags only
- Sources: `docs/design/devin-parity.md`, `docs/design/devin-parity-dev-notes-dv2-dv3.md` and `docs/design/merge-plan-nodes-devin.md`; the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (take inspiration from Devin, decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: `owner_words.hidden_text_refusal` refuses any character of the Unicode categories `Cf`, `Co`, `Cs` and `Cn` (zero-width, text-direction and Tag-block characters, private use, unassigned) except the two joiners U+200C and U+200D, in every line, title, blank and imported file of Recipes and in tags. The notes and rules gate (`refusal`, `clean_text`) is unchanged.
- Why: an imported recipe's defence is that the owner reads it before saving; text they cannot see defeats that. The notes and rules pages keep their behaviour with the switches off.
- Rejected: changing the shared gate (it moves flag-off pages); allowing the joiners nowhere (it breaks some languages and emoji).
- Diagram: label "hidden text refused in recipes and tags", key only.
## D380 — A step's builder tools are narrowed to its grant
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: A step's builder tools are never wider than its grant. For a call whose tag names a node, the router (host side; the vendored engine is untouched) takes J2's tool list down to what the node's frozen grant allows, before the call is planned. `Read`, `Glob`, `Grep`: always. `Write`, `Edit`, `MultiEdit`: only inside the step's own working folder (rules `Edit(//<folder>/**)` and `Edit(./**)`, with the program's `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories` as before). `Bash`: only with a shell level on the grant (`sandboxed_builder`), which no grant holds in version 1; tier 3 is the four git reads of the owner's folder and is NOT a shell, because a builder shell is not confined to them. `WebSearch` and `WebFetch`: only with tier 4 (the approval is the GitHub one, so with it the step may also use the web tools: wider than the question said, recorded here). A tool the table does not name is never offered; a node whose rights are not known (an older task file) gets the narrowest list; a call with no node in its tag, an ordinary run, gets J2's whole list, byte for byte. The rights travel in the node block of `task.json` (`tiers`, `commands`) and `PortSpec` (`node_tiers`, `node_commands`); the `grant.issued` line says what the tools may do in words. Codex is never given a builder tools call (it cannot filter per tool: the router refuses one, as before); a Codex step with a read-only folder runs in Codex's read-only sandbox, and the Codex limit of D342 (no narrowing of a write to a path) stands, now written next to what Codex can do. The Settings and OPERATIONS words are true: "a step may look at the files it is given and write its own working files in a scratch space; it may not run commands or use the web". Package: MAN-2, MAN-8 (run 1).
- Why: run 1 found the grant line saying "tools none" while the step's real calls allowed `Bash` (7 times), `Write`, `Edit`, `WebSearch` and `WebFetch`, with the sandbox off (`srt` not found); `Bash` and the web tools are not confined to any path.
- Rejected: a task setting to turn the engine's tools off for a step (a J2 sync, and all-or-nothing); trusting the sandbox (it was off); refusing a Codex step (the owner's ruling D342 keeps Codex as it is); opening `Bash` for tier 3 (the four git reads are not what a builder shell can do).
- Diagram: label "step tools = grant: reads, own-folder writes, no shell, no web", key only.
- Corrected by: D386 (no web tool for tier 4) and D387 (no shell level; the per-call dollar clamp).

## D381 — The monthly dollar limit stops a running project
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The owner's monthly dollar limit is a project's limit too. A project's dollar limit (`projects.budget_usd`) is what is left of the month when it starts (the limit minus the month's known dollars: every call of every task, planners included); a step's own cap (`NodeCaps.usd`, which the host's meter enforces between calls) is what the project and the month have left when the step starts; the Conductor checks the month every tick and stops a running project through the stop ladder with the one sentence "Your monthly limit was reached, so the project stopped." (audit `trigger.project_month`, one note). A project that spends past what it was given at its start is stopped with the same sentence even if the owner raised the month meanwhile. A lower number the owner sets for one project keeps its own words ("the project spent more than the dollars you set"). An ordinary task keeps today's rule: new tasks wait, one that is running finishes (the Settings sentence is unchanged and true for it; it would be false to add projects to it with the flag off). The limit is read from its file at most every 2 seconds and before a transaction opens, never inside one. Package: MAN-9 (run 1).
- Why: run 1 showed a second step that would have run past $3 (the watchdog, not Sutra, stopped it); an unattended project is exactly where the owner's number must hold.
- Rejected: stopping only between steps (a step can cost most of the limit); a per-project number the owner must type; counting only the steps' own calls (the planner's calls and a failed call that reported nothing are part of the month).
- Diagram: label "month limit stops project + caps each step", key only.

## D382 — The reply to a project ends with its page address
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: A request that becomes a project gets its page address in the reply. The org chat's reply ends with "You can follow it here: /p/<id>" (shown as the "Open the project" link; only that exact shape), and the task card of each step carries `project_id` and the same link. It is one code path, `_hand_off`, so a line typed in the chat and one sent with `POST /api/projects` (which posts the same line to the org chat) are the same; run 1 found the address only in notes, which the page already linked, and not in the reply the planner's template writes. A card of an ordinary task has no `project_id` key and the flag-off reply has no address: the wire shape is what it was. Package: MAN-13 (run 1).
- Why: R6 promised the way back into the project and the first real request showed none.
- Rejected: a separate second message (it arrives after the reply and can be missed); a link on the home page only.
- Diagram: label "project reply + step cards link to /p/id", key only.

## D383 — The project page: plain words, the whole cost, a phone-sized picture
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The project page speaks plainly and costs what the ledger says. (a) A step is named by its title (its task's), "Step a1" only when it has none. (b) What the steps said is one line about what they produced and the files they made (plumbing files are not named); the engine's own summary sentence is never shown. (c) A stop is said as the owner knows it: "You stopped it", "The kill switch was used", the monthly sentence; never "Stopped: stopped on request" (the engine says that whoever asked: a step shows the reason its project was stopped for). (d) Every action the server writes has words (`node.stopped`, `kill`, `pause`, `resume`, `trigger.project_month` were missing), a test reads the code for them; a tool row says what the step did ("ran commands 7 times"), and the audit says tool rows carry the time the step ended (they are written then). (e) The project's dollars are the ledger's: the steps, the reviewer and the planner's own calls, with a call that carries no dollar figure counted apart and the page saying "about" and how many. (f) The picture is drawn twice, left to right and top to bottom, and CSS shows the one that fits (no sideways scroll on a phone). (g) The page follows the computer's light or dark setting until the owner has chosen one (other pages keep dark as their default). (h) The Home lane "Heads up" does not list a step of a stopped project with "Try again" (the owner, the kill switch or a limit stopped it; its page says why); with the flag off no node table is read. Package: MAN-13 (run 1).
- Why: each is a sentence or a number in run 1 that the owner could not use or that disagreed with the ledger ($0.97 against $2.93 with the planner and the unpriced).
- Rejected: hiding the audit rows (the owner wants the record); rewriting the engine's words at the source (the engine is vendored).
- Diagram: label "page: plain words, ledger cost, phone graph", key only.

## D384 — nodes status asks the running app; nodes resume is audited
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: `nodes status` says whose settings it shows, and `nodes resume` is in the record. The command prints the `SUTRA_NODES_*` settings "in this shell" and then asks the running app (found by the instance lock and its pid file, one local request to `/api/nodes/status`, whose answer now carries the settings the app was started with) whether projects are ON or OFF in it, so a shell with the flag unset no longer says "no agent runs" against an app that has it on. `nodes resume` writes in its marker file what it removed; the running app writes the audit row `resume` (actor owner) when it next looks (or when it starts, for a resume made while it was closed); a look and a resume that removed nothing write none. The command still needs neither the server nor a token. Package: MAN-4 (run 1).
- Why: run 1 printed "SUTRA_NODES_V1=0 ... no agent runs" against a running flag-on app, and a resume by hand left no trace.
- Rejected: the command writing the database itself (one writer; the server may be running); the server answering for a flag-off app (it has no such route and must not: flag-off is 404).
- Diagram: label "nodes status: shell vs app; resume audited", key only.

## D385 — Checklist corrections after run 1 and what it did not see
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The checklist is corrected and what run 1 did not see is written down. The token is never on disk (`SUTRA_TOKEN`); the command line of a beta is its own program; a typed request goes to `/api/chats/cs_org/messages`; the Welcome dialog is dismissed first; a request the planner reads as ONE step runs the old team path (to get a project say "two steps, one after the other"); the first P4 sample (two steps, a 6-file folder: about $0.7 and 17 calls per step; the same work as one step on the old path: 31 calls, 6 minutes, $1.83); the monthly-limit behaviour; the Mac window was not exercised (the folder was under `/private/tmp`). The two counts that look different are different things: `nodes/tools/<run id>.jsonl` has a line per tool call, `calls.jsonl` a line per model call written when it ends, so a step killed in the middle can have 18 and 0; they are not reconciled. Run 1 did not see: both steps done and the project ended, a question card, a Mac window, the Stop button, slots, a chat message during a step. Package: docs (run 1).
- Why: a checklist that is wrong costs the next run.
- Rejected: reconciling the two logs (they count different things).
- Diagram: label "checklist fixed; run 1 unseen listed", key only.

## D386 — No web tool for a step's builder call
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: No web tool for any step's builder call in version 1 (M1). `WebSearch`, `WebFetch` and the rule `WebFetch(domain:*)` are never offered to a step of a project, whatever its grant holds: `NodeRights` no longer has a web part and tier 4 opens nothing. Tier 4 is "Read GitHub (read only), through your own GitHub login"; the GitHub reads travel the separate tool-profile path of the step's agent turns, never the builder call, so the web tools gave GitHub nothing and gave a fetched page or the step itself a way to send what it had read to any address, and Decide for me answers that question by itself. Corrects D380 (which allowed the web with tier 4, recorded there as wider than the question). If a builder ever needs GitHub, only `WebFetch(domain:github.com)` and `WebFetch(domain:raw.githubusercontent.com)` could be added later, with the owner's words for it; not now. The question text is unchanged: it never said more than read-only GitHub. Package: MAN-2, MAN-8.
- Why: the review found the approval and the capability did not match, and the owner's default (Decide for me) would grant it unasked.
- Rejected: tying the web to a new level now (nothing needs it); trimming `domain:*` to GitHub (no step needs the web in v1).
- Diagram: label "no web for a step; tier 4 = GitHub reads only", key only.

## D387 — Tool-narrowing review hardening
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The review's other hardening (M2, m1, m2, m7, m8, m9). (a) One builder call is bounded by what the step has left: the router lowers the call's `--max-budget-usd` (the engine asks for $2 a call) to the meter's remaining allowance, and starts no program at zero (a plain "no dollars left" refusal). What the month or the project loses to OTHER tasks while one call runs is not seen by the step's process; the Conductor's next tick is what sees it. (b) A router made for a step's process (its task file carries the grant's tiers) narrows every builder call it gets, whatever the tag says; a test follows the rights from the port spec to the command line. (c) The confining rule is `Edit(./**)` (relative to the step's folder); the absolute rule is a second line, spelled `//c/...` on Windows and with glob and rule-syntax characters of the folder's name escaped. (d) The grant line is shorter ("own folder only; no commands or web"), so the longest case is not cut mid-word. (e) There is no shell level in the code and no unused owner words: nothing a stored grant says can open a shell. (f) A Codex call of a step with no folder is read-only whatever the policy's sandbox says. (g) Codex calls carry no dollar figure, so the monthly limit and a step's dollar cap cannot stop a Codex project; its credits (calls) and time hold (OPERATIONS says so). (h) A project step cannot run its own tests (no shell); J2's checks after the call still run. Package: MAN-2, MAN-9.
- Why: each closes a gap the review found by reading the code and by mutating it.
- Rejected: a per-call dollar figure that reads the ledger in the step's process (it has no database); keeping the absolute rule as the primary (its spelling cannot be tried without the real program on every system).
- Diagram: label "per-call dollar clamp; node router narrows all; no shell level", key only.

## D388 — The project's dollar limit, tightened
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The project's dollar limit, tightened (m3, m4, m5). The limit a project is compared with counts only what the project spent after it was made (the planner's calls came before it and are already out of the month's allowance it was given; the page still shows them). A figure taken from the month is used only while the owner still has a monthly limit and it is still the same month: a removed limit does not stop a project, a new month starts with the full allowance. When the figure runs out while the month itself has room (the owner raised the limit) the project stops with "This project used what was left of your month when it started, so it stopped." and not with "monthly limit reached". A step does not start when the project has no dollars left (it waits; the tick stops the project), instead of starting with a one-cent cap. Package: MAN-9.
- Why: the first version stopped projects early, for a limit that no longer existed, or with a sentence that was false.
- Rejected: re-reading the month on every call (the tick does).
- Diagram: label "limit counts project spend; removed/new month ignored", key only.

## D389 — The reply's project address is the app's own
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The reply's address is the app's own (m11). The org chat's reply links to the project only when the project exists, only in the org chat's page, and any look-alike `/p/prj_...` in a word a model wrote (a title) is made plain before the app's own address is added. Package: MAN-13.
- Why: the chat page turns that exact shape into a link; nothing a model writes should be able to make one.
- Rejected: marking the message on the wire (a wire shape change).
- Diagram: label "reply links only a real project, only in org chat", key only.

## D390 — No Try again for a step of an ended project
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: No step of an ended project is a "Try again" on Home. The "Heads up" lane drops a crashed or stopped step of a project that is over (stopped, could not finish, or done), and a step that finished with open items of such a project has no Try again; with the flag off no node table is read and the lane is what it was. D383 (h) covered only a stopped project; run 2 showed one for a project that ended "Could not finish", with the engine's own words ("Stopped: stopped on request"). Package: MAN-13.
- Why: the project page says how it ended; a retry of one step of an ended project does nothing the owner expects.
