- Rejected: rewriting the lane's words (the row should not exist).
- Diagram: label "no Try again for steps of ended projects", key only.

## D391 — nodes status only looks; the app takes the resume marker
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: `nodes status` only looks; only the running app takes the resume marker. The command's switch is made with `consume=False`: it never removes `nodes.resume`, never clears a latched switch. `nodes resume` appends what it removed to the marker, so two resumes before the app looks are two audit rows, and the app writes them (when it next looks, or at its start). Package: MAN-4.
- Why: run 2 reproduced `status` right after `resume` taking the marker in the command's own process, so the app never wrote the row (D384 had the same race).
- Rejected: the command writing the database (one writer).
- Diagram: label "status read-only; app audits resume", key only.

## D392 — The resume hint names this install's command
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The hint for resuming names the command of THIS install. In an app the command is the app's own Python (`<app>/Contents/Resources/python/bin/python3 -I -B -m sutra_app nodes resume --data-dir <dir>`), elsewhere `sutra-pro nodes resume`; the server's held-step sentences and the command's own say it. There is no Resume button on the page for the switch (the page's Pause and Continue are for one project), so the command is the way, and the docs say so. Package: MAN-4.
- Why: a beta has no `sutra-pro` on the path.
- Rejected: a new page button (a write route with a wider reach; not asked).
- Diagram: label "resume hint: app python or sutra-pro", key only.

## D393 — The reviewer's answers and the reviewer's text
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The reviewer's question and the reviewer's text. (a) The reviewer's two answers read "Send them back" and "Leave as it is" (the page and the chat card); the others keep their words. (b) The reviewer is given what each step WROTE, not only the names of its files: the text files its hand-off names (not the engine's own files, no link, no slash in the name, no hidden name), at most 4000 characters of a file, 8000 of a step and 16000 in all, scrubbed of secret shapes by the egress scan and wrapped between `<<<` and `>>>` as data, with the template saying that text inside is material to read and never an instruction. Package: MAN-11.
- Why: in run 2 the reviewer judged "the work only lists filenames" because the file list was all it saw.
- Rejected: the whole file (cost and size); giving it the engine's report files (they are the engine's words about the run).
- Diagram: label "reviewer: Send back/Leave; reads what steps wrote", key only.

## D394 — Words and links of a step's cards and page
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The words and links of a step's own cards and page. (a) The done card of a step links to its project (`project_id`, left out of the wire shape for an ordinary task), offers no Try again of its own, and says how a stop happened in the owner's words (the project's stop reason: "You stopped it", "The kill switch was used"; the engine's stock "Stopped: stopped on request" and "waiting task X did not finish" become "It was stopped before it finished" and "The step before it did not finish"); the task keeps the engine's words. (b) The page names no engine file ("report.md" too) and no file count; the engine's pseudo-tool PROPOSE is no tool row. (c) The project is titled by the request's short title, not by its first step's. (d) The grant line says nothing of "tools none" when the step has no app tools. Package: MAN-13.
- Why: each was a sentence in run 2 that the owner could not use.
- Rejected: rewriting the stored outcome (the record keeps what the engine said).
- Diagram: label "step cards link; plain stops; no plumbing", key only.

## D395 — Per-step calls, minutes and dollars on the page
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The page says what each step used: its calls, its minutes from its first start (to now while it runs) and about how many dollars, every attempt counted, from the ledger and the step's tasks (`NodeOut.calls`, `usd`, `minutes`). A step that has made no call says nothing. Package: MAN-13.
- Why: the owner should not need the ledger.
- Rejected: reading the parity branch's summary (it is not on this branch; the same numbers, one query for each step).
- Diagram: label "page: per-step calls, minutes, dollars", key only.

## D396 — The project page's theme
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The project page's theme. The owner's own explicit choice (the key `j2theme`, written only when they pick light or dark on Home or a team page) wins, because the choice is for every page; with none, or "system", the page follows the computer's light or dark setting and changes with it while it is open; a change of the choice in another tab is followed. Package: MAN-13.
- Why: run 2 asked that a stored light from another page not silently override. The key is only ever written by a deliberate choice, so honouring it is honouring the owner; this page has no chooser of its own (a second key would make two answers for one setting).
- Rejected: ignoring the stored choice (the owner picked it); a chooser on this page.
- Diagram: label "project theme: choice wins, else follows OS live", key only.

## D397 — A graceful close leaves the project resumable
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: A graceful close leaves the project resumable. The app's stop ladder asks every live run to stop; the engine answers with its stock "Stopped: stopped on request" before the app can write "the app was closed", so the step was read as the owner's stop: cancelled, its followers cancelled, the project "could not finish". A step whose task was marked `_stopped_by = app` (only the shutdown and the start guard mark it) AND whose outcome is only the engine's stock words is now read as a close: it fails with "the app was closed while it ran", gets a fresh attempt that spends no retry (D314, at most two such), and its followers wait. The owner's stop and the kill switch mark `owner` or `system` and still cancel. A crash or `kill -9` is found at the next start by `reconcile` and ends the same way (tests for both, with the fake engine). The closed time is left out of the wall time (D348). Package: MAN-7.
- Why: D314 said an app close gives the step a fresh attempt; it was only true after a crash.
- Rejected: ending the project on purpose at a graceful close (nothing in the design says a close should cost the owner the project).
- Diagram: label "graceful close = fresh attempt, project goes on", key only.

## D350 — Ask: the direct chat answers read only, with the limits of a function's question (DV-1, `SUTRA_ASK_V1`)
- Sources: `docs/design/devin-parity.md` DV-1 (Ask Devin: a cited answer about the code); D115 (the direct chat), D127 (the owner's access caps every call's folder), D135, D147 to D149 (`agent_ask.py`: 2 calls, $0.25, 120 s); P14 (a feature may only narrow a grant); P8 (local data).
- Decided: the owner's chat box gets a switch, Ask | Do. Do is the chat as it was. Ask is the SAME route, `POST /api/chats/{sid}/direct` (and `/direct/retry`), with `mode: "ask"` in the body: the same stores, events, live turn, Stop and Retry. An Ask turn (a) asks the router for `workspace: read`, and `Router._chat_turn` then never gives it more than the owner's folder with the four file tools that look, even when Settings, Working folder lets the team change files (Claude: only Read, Glob, Grep and LS are offered, no Edit, no Write, no command, no web tool, not even the four git reads; Codex: a read-only sandbox, on a resumed thread too and with no folder too, in which its shell may still run commands that only read, anywhere on the disk; D355 and D356 amend this); (b) carries the limits of `agent_ask.py` in the turn's own `Allowance` (`ChatTurnIn.allowance`: 2 calls and 120 s for both providers; $0.25 is a real ceiling for Claude and a check before each process for Codex, D356); (c) is NOT held back by the owner's monthly limit or plan window: a plain direct chat is not, and the read-only way must not be refused while the writing way works (D356, amends the first version of this entry); (d) is grounded with `ASK_RULES` after the fixed rules, and with the folder paragraph in its read-only form; (e) is the same chat session: no new table, no new route, the owner's line is stored as written. With no folder set the turn still runs and every source then says "no working folder is set". A turn that is stopped or fails stores no card. The mode is stored with the owner's line and a Retry takes it from there: an Ask is retried as an Ask whatever the body says, and a body may only narrow (D354, amends the first version of this entry, which left it to the page).
- Why: the owner wants answers about a folder that can be trusted and cannot change anything; the grant already says what a chat may do, so Ask only narrows it (P14). One route means every fix to the direct chat reaches Ask.
- Rejected: a new route `POST /api/ask` (a second copy of the turn, the stop token, the checkpoint and the retry); a mode that is only a prompt ("please do not edit": a prompt is not a grant); reading the mode from the model; a separate chat session per mode (the owner would lose the thread); a cost estimate on the card (the usage ledger already books the call).
- Diagram: label "Ask: read-only cited answer (DV-1)", pill on Connector router.

## D351 — The sources of an answer are checked by code, and "checked" says only what code looked at
- Sources: `orchestrator/citations.py`; the owner rule that a model never approves a model (labels meaning true or allowed come from code or the owner); D127 and `connectors/workdir.py` (`check_folder`, `_inside`).
- Decided: an answer names its sources with marks, `[path:12-18]`, `[path:12]` or `[path:12-18 "exact words"]` (a mark in a fenced code block is an example, not a source; at most 30 marks are checked, the rest are counted). `citations.check_answer` looks at each mark and gives it one of two labels, `checked` or `could not find`, and a reason in plain words for the second. `checked` means: the path is a plain path inside the owner's folder (no `..`, no `~`, no drive letter, colon or backslash, no part that Windows reads differently, no device name; an absolute path only when it starts with the folder as text and the file system keeps it inside; D357 amends this: the spelling rules run before any file-system call), after `realpath` it is still inside (a link out is outside), it is a regular file of at most 4 MB that is text, the lines exist, the range is at most 400 lines and, when words were quoted (at least 4 characters, at most 300), the words are on those lines (white space squeezed, case kept). It never means the lines support the claim: the card says so. Nothing a model writes can change a label: the module reads a path, two numbers and a quote from the answer and nothing else, and the card is built by the server. No file outside the folder is ever opened, and nothing is ever written.
- Why: a model that labels its own citations is a model approving a model. A path check that is only text is not a check (`..`, a link, `C:x`, `file:stream`, `CON`).
- Rejected: a model or a heuristic judging whether the lines support the claim (not code, and wrong in both directions); one label per answer ("verified"); accepting Windows spellings by converting them; showing the file's lines on the card (a file's words would go into the chat store); failing the answer when a source is wrong (the owner reads the answer and the card).
- Diagram: label "sources checked by code, never by the model (citations.py)", pill on Chat sessions.

## D352 — With `SUTRA_ASK_V1` off nothing of Ask exists: the word `mode` is refused as before, no answer has a new key, no switch is drawn
- Sources: the DV rules (flag off is byte-identical: no new field in any existing API shape, no prompt or golden change); D81 (the token first); D235 (the closed request models).
- Decided: the request models `DirectIn` and `RetryIn` are closed and unchanged, so today a body that names `mode` is a 422 `extra_forbidden` like any unknown field. `orchestrator/ask_mode.AskRoute`, the route class of the two routes, looks at the flag first and does nothing when it is off: the refusal is the same bytes as for any other unknown field and nothing is stored or started. With the flag on it takes `mode` out of the body before the closed model sees it and keeps it for the route, which checks the value after the token (`ask` or `do`, else 422 "mode is ask or do"); a body without `mode` is untouched and `do` is the same as none. The page learns that Ask exists from the pill it already reads (`GET /api/chats/{sid}/providers` gets `ask: true`, only when the flag is on); `/api/config` lists `ask_v1` only when it is on; `sutra-pro config show` follows `/api/config`. No script is added to a page, so the page is the same document; the switch is drawn only when the pill says `ask`, and `ChatComposer.bar` without `{ask: true}` is the bar it was. The default prompts, the grounding text of a chat without a mode (byte for byte), the schemas and the goldens are unchanged; the sources card is a new member of the `ChatCard` union that no flag-off turn can store. `config init` lists the new variable as one more commented line (a reference, not a setting).
- Why: a client that asks for a read-only answer must never be given an ordinary one silently (an ignored `mode` would do that); the closed model gives that refusal for free and keeps today's bytes.
- Rejected: a new optional field `mode` on `DirectIn` (a new field of an existing shape, and a hand-made refusal that could differ from the old one); a query parameter (an unknown query parameter is ignored today, so a flag-off server would answer an Ask as an ordinary chat); a new nav entry or page; asking `/api/config` for the flag (one more request on every page load).
- Diagram: label "Ask off: mode refused, no new field", pill on Chat sessions.

## D353 — The sources are one card after the answer; "Turn this into work" drafts a request and never sends it
- Sources: the DV-1 spec ("an answer card (additive ChatCard kind)", "a button that DRAFTS a request"); D135 (the deliverable card is a message of kind card); D216 (a try's refs).
- Decided: after the answer of a finished Ask turn the server stores ONE message of kind `card` in the same transaction, `who` sutra, no text, with the new card `answer_sources` (`AnswerCard`: the owner's question, the sources with label, how and reason, the counts, and `more`). Its `src_ref` is `direct-sources:<owner line>[.N]`, so the page reads which line and which try it belongs to, a retry supersedes it with its reply, and a live page holds it back with the reply. It is stored also when the answer named nothing: the card then says "No sources given. Nothing in this answer points to a file, so none of it was checked." When every source is wrong it says none could be found and not to rely on the answer. Strings on the card (a path, a quote) go through the secret scrub. The card has a button, "Turn this into work", whose `data-draft` holds the start of a request ("Based on my question … and what you found in a.py (lines 3 to 4), please "): the page puts it in the box, switches Send to the team (Plan with Sutra, Tell the team), puts the caret at the end and focuses the box; it posts nothing, and the owner edits it and presses Send. The draft names only sources that were checked.
- Why: a card of its own keeps the answer a plain message every older reader still understands, and lets the labels be code's. A draft the owner must finish is the only way "turn into work" cannot start work by itself (D296: silence is a refusal).
- Rejected: labels inside the answer text (a model's words next to a code's); a second message kind; sending the draft; filling in the whole request from the answer (the owner's words decide what is done); a table of citations (the card is the record).
- Diagram: label "sources card + draft-only Turn this into work", pill on Chat sessions.

## D354 — The mode is stored with the owner's line, and a retry can only narrow it (amends D350; DV-1 review M2)
- Sources: the DV-1 review (a Retry that named no mode ran a failed Ask as a Do, with the owner's write access); the rule that every grant can only narrow (P14, DV rules).
- Decided: the owner's line of an Ask turn carries `mode: "ask"` in its own meta (a sibling key of `meta_json`, like `via` and `ordered`; `ChatMessage.mode`; no new table, no new column) and `ChatMessageOut.mode` shows it only for such a line (every other message has no `mode` key). `POST /direct/retry` reads it: a line that was an Ask is retried as an Ask, with no body mode or with `ask`; a body that names `do` for it is refused (422, "That question was asked read only (Ask), so it can only be asked again that way."); a line that was not an Ask is retried as it was and an Ask only when the body says `ask`. The page passes the line's own `mode` to Retry (it no longer relies on what it remembers).
- Why: the read-only guarantee must not depend on a page remembering, in memory, fifty lines.
- Rejected: asking the page every time (a reload forgets); a retry that follows the switch (it can widen); ignoring a body `do` silently (the owner would think it was heard).
- Diagram: label "Ask mode stored with the line; a retry only narrows", pill on Chat sessions.

## D355 — A Codex thread is one mode; an Ask with no folder is read only too (amends D350; DV-1 review M3, m3, m4)
- Sources: the DV-1 review; `connectors/codex.py` (the system text of a thread is read on its first turn only).
- Decided: Codex keeps the system text of a thread's first turn, so the Ask words (and the read-only folder paragraph) would be stale for a later Do turn, and absent for a later Ask. A turn whose mode differs from the thread's starts a new Codex thread with the carry-over (the way a changed folder does; the mode of a thread is kept in `session.json` as `thread_mode`, only for a thread an Ask started). Claude is given the text on every turn and keeps its thread. An Ask turn with no working folder passes `read_only` to the connector, so Codex runs in its read-only sandbox whatever the policy's sandbox says. What is and is not offered is said truthfully: Claude is given four read tools and no command or web tool; Codex has a read-only shell (no write, no network) that can still read anywhere on the disk, and Claude's `Read` is not confined to the folder either (not changed here). An Ask turn that made an `edit` or `web` tool call anyway (counted from the turn's own blocks, `tool_kinds`) says so on its card (`AnswerCard.tried`); `run` is not counted, because Codex reads files through its shell.
- Why: "the chat as it was" must hold for Do after an Ask on Codex; and "read only" must hold without a folder.
- Rejected: the Ask words in the user's line (the stored line would differ from the sent one and the question would be carried over twice); a new thread on every mode change for Claude (it needs none).
- Diagram: label "Codex: one thread per mode; Ask read-only even with no folder", pill on Connector router.

## D356 — The dollars of an Ask are a ceiling for Claude only; Ask follows Do on the monthly limit (amends D350; DV-1 review M4, m9)
- Sources: the DV-1 review; `connectors/claude.py` (`--max-budget-usd`); D127; the spec's "limits as in agent_ask".
- Decided: the router passes what the allowance still lets the next process spend (the limit less what earlier processes of the turn cost) to the connector (`stream(max_usd=)`, nothing when there is no dollar limit); Claude ends the turn at that cap (`envelope_exceeded`, which an Ask shows as "Sutra stopped this answer: it reached the limit for one question (2 tries, about $0.25 or 2 minutes)"); Codex has no flag that caps a process, so for it $0.25 is a check before each process, said so in the code and the owner's note. Calls (2) and the deadline hold for both. The second round after a lost provider session goes through the same gate (proved by a test: two processes under a two-call limit, one under a one-call limit). The owner's monthly limit and plan window no longer hold an Ask back: they do not hold a plain direct chat back, and the read-only way must not be refused while the writing way works.
- Why: cost was the reason for the ceiling; and refusing the safer mode first is backwards.
- Rejected: gating the plain chat too (a change to the chat as it was); a cost estimate on the card.
- Diagram: label "Ask $ cap: Claude yes, Codex a check; limit as for Do", pill on Connector router.

## D357 — A path is refused by its spelling before any file-system call; only path-shaped text is a citation (amends D351; DV-1 review M1, m6, m7)
- Sources: the DV-1 review (on Windows `realpath` of `//host/share` or `\\.\pipe\x` connects to the host); D351.
- Decided: `citations.spelling_problem` refuses a backslash, a colon, a leading `//`, `~`, a control character or a character a path never needs from the text alone, and `check_mark` calls it before anything else: no `realpath`, `stat` or `open` is made for such a path, and `os.path.isabs` is no longer asked. An absolute path (one leading `/`) is compared with the folder as text first (`absolute_parts`); on Windows only relative marks are accepted. Tests trap every file-system entry for Windows spellings and simulate `ntpath`. Only text with the shape of a citation is one: a `/` in the path or an extension that starts with a letter; a bare name (`Makefile:3`) only when that file is in the folder, so `x[0:5]`, a log line `[12:30]` or a version `[1.2:3]` is not a source that "could not be found". The reasons on the card are one short plain sentence each.
- Why: "no file outside the folder is opened, no network" has to hold on Windows too.
- Rejected: converting Windows spellings; dropping absolute paths (the model sometimes copies one from a tool result).
- Diagram: label "path refused by spelling before any file call", pill on Chat sessions.

## D358 — Flag off holds in two more places: a body nobody can encode, and the order of the OpenAPI paths (amends D352; DV-1 review m1, m2)
- Sources: the DV-1 review; D352.
- Decided: the route class re-encodes the body it rewrites with ASCII escapes, so a lone surrogate (`\ud800`) and a body nested past the parser are a clean refusal (422, the same as with the flag off) and the token is still asked for first (it was a 500 before the token). `/direct` and `/direct/retry` are registered where their decorators were, so the OpenAPI document of the chat routes is byte for byte the one published before DV-1 (`tests/fixtures/openapi/chat_routes_before_ask.json`, written from commit 22bbbf0a, compared in `test_api_ask.py`).
- Why: "flag off is byte-identical" includes the published document and the error for a bad body.
- Rejected: accepting a different path order as "the same data".
- Diagram: label "flag off: OpenAPI bytes and bad bodies unchanged", pill on Chat sessions.

## D398 — A step is never tried again by hand
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: A step is never tried again by hand (M-A). "Try again" is not offered for any task that is or ever was a node's (the node's current task or an earlier attempt, found through its `parent_task_id` chain), whatever state the project is in; and `TaskService.retry` refuses such a task with a plain 409 ("A step of a project is tried again by its project, not by hand"). The only retries of a step are the Conductor's one retry and the fresh attempt after a close (D397). With the flag off no node table is read. Package: MAN-5, MAN-13.
- Why: a retry by hand made an ordinary task with no node: no grant, no tool narrowing, no scope, no dollar limit. Reproduced in the rig.
- Rejected: hiding the button only (the route stays open).
- Diagram: label "no retry by hand of a step; API 409", key only.

## D399 — One list of the engine's own files
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: One list of the engine's own files (M-B). `handoff.ENGINE_FILES` is the one list (`report.md` and `summary.txt` included); the reviewer reads none of them as what a step wrote, the page names none of them, and a test keeps `project_view.js` `PLUMBING` equal to it. They are skipped before the reviewer's character budget is counted. Package: MAN-11, MAN-13.
- Why: the reviewer had been given the engine's own report as the step's work. Package: MAN-11, MAN-13.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "one engine-files list: page, hand-off, reviewer", key only.

## D400 — The card of an attempt the project replaced
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The card of an attempt the project replaced (M-C). The done card finds its project through the task's own chain, not through the node's current task (which `node_sync` has already moved to the fresh attempt). For an attempt that was replaced while the project goes on (a close of the app, a retry, a send-back) no "stopped" card is posted: one plain line says "The app was closed, so the step ... starts again" (or "This try ended, so ...") with the project's link; the fresh attempt's card links. Package: MAN-13.
- Why: every graceful quit posted "Your Web team stopped: Stopped: stopped on request" with no link while the project carried on (seen in run 3). Package: MAN-13.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "replaced attempt: plain line + link, no stopped card", key only.

## D409 — Steps that start together share the dollar remainder
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: Steps that start together share the dollar remainder: a step's cap is what the project and the month have left divided by the steps of the project that run or are ready (the first, alone, gets all of it; two ready, half each), so together they cannot pass the limit by steps times the remainder before the tick sees it (m-13). Rejected: holding the extra step (it would idle for money it may not spend). Package: MAN-9.
- Why: it answers a finding of the review of D386 to D397 or of real-Claude run 3 (read in dev-notes N82 to N86). Package: MAN-9.

## D415 — Host-parsed buttons and a refused stale answer
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The buttons of a step's own question are the host's, and a stale answer is refused (r-2, r-11). Only the closing run of numbered lines (`Option 1:`, `Option 2:` ... to the end of the text, counting up from 1) is buttons; an `Option N:` line anywhere else is just text, and the page always shows the whole real question. The page answers through `POST /api/projects/{id}/questions/{message id}/answer` with the number of the button; the route takes the answer only while that question is still one of the project's open step questions and the option is one of its buttons, otherwise a plain 409 ("That question is not open any more"), and it delivers as an ANSWER only (`TaskService.answer_waiting`), never as a follow-up: a stale card, a second tab or a double click cannot become a new ordinary task with no grant. The page reads the project again after a refusal. Package: MAN-13.
- Why: a step's own text could forge the buttons, and a bare "Option N" sent to the team chat made a new ordinary task when the step had ended (the M-A class).
- Rejected: sending the text to the team chat route (it follows up).
- Diagram: label "step-question buttons host-parsed; stale answer 409", key only.

Only the page rewords a read of a repository's own files ("looked at the folder's history") for the owner. ## D418 — The per-call dollar cap is read on every call
- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: What one builder call may spend is read on EVERY call, never frozen at the step's start (R-2, reworks D409). Before each builder call a step's own process reads, read-only, the database and the owner's limits file and takes `min(the step's own remainder, what is left of the month, what is left of the project's own dollars divided by the steps of the project that RUN at that moment, this one included)`; a step held for an answer takes no share, a share is given back when a sibling ends, and a step that began alone is lowered when a sibling starts. The same arithmetic (`orchestrator/livecap.py`) is the server's. It is a bound on one call, not a promise: steps that run at the same time can pass the limit by up to one call's worth each before the Conductor's tick sees the ledger; steps of different projects each divide only their own project's dollars and share the month's remainder; Codex calls carry no dollar figure, so nothing here can stop a project that runs on Codex. OPERATIONS says so. Package: MAN-9.
- Why: the first version froze a share at the start (staggered starts passed the limit, a step could be cut below what it needed) and no test failed without it. Package: MAN-9.
- Rejected: no alternative beyond the whole-design options in `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes/architecture.md` section 12 was weighed for this one.
- Diagram: label "per-call cap read live, shared by running steps", key only.

- Sources: `docs/design/multi-agent-nodes` (decisions.md, architecture.md, guardrails-and-permissions.md); the owner's direction of 2026-11-20 (enhance, do not replace; decide autonomously, document every decision).
- Decided: The rest of the review of D398 to D409. (a) D402 completed: the engine's real `workable()` shapes (`done-when item N (F-N VERDICT)`, `design rubric D-2`, `document rubric`, `rubrics PJ-4, PJ-5`, `necessary condition NC-1 not met: ... (evidence: ...)`, the render check, "finished as it is") lose their ids and code words, one open item at a time, and "the Requester's decision" is "your decision" (r-1). (b) Decide for me is shown never to answer a step's engine question by a test through the real path (a real open question, the owner's switch, the rule table's own pass), and a second test fails if the rule table ever names a kind such a question could have (r-4). (c) A step that finished with open items in a project that still runs has no Try again, tested (r-5). (d) A file called `constructor` or `__proto__` is shown by its name; the tool rows say "a project file" for a name that may be the owner's own, and "its report" is said only in the hand-off line (r-6). (e) A project title is never empty, never ends on a run of connectors, is not split at "e.g.", is not cut inside a glyph, and the runner's use of it is pinned (r-7). Package: MAN-13.

## D420 — Stop on the team page and the home box: the existing stop ladder, after a confirm
- Sources: Rubak's feedback (no way to stop a run); the dollar-budget spec, step 1; the stop ladder `POST /api/tasks/{id}/stop` (`orchestrator/api/tasks.py:105`, `TaskService.stop`); the owner's answer 1 (Stop only while working; Send/Stop toggle while paused).
- Decided: the team page's studio send button (port edit E26a to E26c) is Stop, with a filled square, while the team's run is live and not a saved run: always while it works (the box stays disabled, Stop stays pressable), and while it waits for an answer only until the owner types (then Send again). The home box's Go (`FrontDoor.stopOf`, `home.js`) is Stop while the box is empty, the card under it shows the work it just started and that team has a `live_task_id`. Stop asks "Stop this run? What's built so far is kept." and on yes reads the team's `live_task_id` (team page: `GET /api/departments/{id}`; it must match `tsk_` and 12 hex digits) and calls the ladder once with `{}` (no force). A 409 (already ended) only refreshes; any other error says "Could not stop the run. Try again." (team page) or shows the error banner (home). No server change. Home's Stop is brief by design: it shows only while the "Your project is set up" card is on screen (about 1.6 s, `LAND_MS`) and once `live_task_id` has been fetched; the team page, where Home takes the owner, has the real Stop. The landing is not lengthened for it (review R1-9, 2026-11-20).
- Why: the ladder existed (stop line, up to 10 s for J2 to save, then SIGTERM) but no button called it, so an owner could not stop a run that went the wrong way.
- Rejected: a department-scoped stop route (duplicates the ladder); a force stop from the page (the ladder escalates by itself); taking messages while the team works (the server answers 409 `busy`; R-1 keeps the box disabled).
- Diagram: label "Stop button on team page + home box (stop ladder)", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D421 — A run's cost is the API price each AI call reports; a call without one (Codex today) is estimated at the run's own rate, else $3.18 per million tokens; $0.0187 per credit; default $20
- Sources: `scripts/usd_basis.py --data ~/.sutra-pro-beta` (2026-11-20, beta data only, read-only): 12 runs, 10 with credits spent, 400 task calls (397 priced, all Claude `reported`; 3 Claude calls with basis `none`; no Codex call); the cost per call as recorded: Claude reports it (`connectors/claude.py:401-413`, basis `reported`), Codex does not (`connectors/codex.py:361`, shipped rates null in `connectors/policy.default.json`), the router stores the sum of a request's attempts (`connectors/usage.py` `build_usage_row`), the engine clears `llm.LAST_META` per call (`engine/llm.py:317`), keeps `usd` when known (`:334-337`) and writes it to `calls.jsonl` (`engine/j2.py:4505`).
- Decided: a run's cost is the sum of the usd each AI call reports (API price; runs use the Claude subscription, nobody is billed it). A call with no usd is estimated: its tokens (input + output, or characters / 4 when the call has no token count, as in mock mode) times this run's own dollars per token from its priced calls, or, before any priced call, $3.18 per million tokens (pooled: all the dollars of the 397 priced calls over all their tokens; the median per call, $3.73, was not taken). The question and the cards say how many calls were estimated. Credits stay internal at a fixed $0.0187 per credit (the median over 10 runs; p25 $0.0142, p75 $0.0454, min $0.0090, max $0.3494). The default budget is 1000 credits at that rate ($18.72), rounded to $20, with presets $10 / $20 / $40. The owner confirmed on 2026-11-20: the $20 default and presets (OQ-7), estimating unpriced calls at the run's own rate (OQ-8), and the pooled fallback rate.
- Why: one number the owner understands, already reported per call; an unpriced call must not run free, or a Codex fallback would have no limit.
- Rejected: counting tokens (revision 1 of the plan); pricing unpriced calls at $0; refusing or stopping a run whose connector reports no price (Codex is the fallback that keeps runs going); requiring Codex rates in Settings (the shipped rates are null and the owner would have to look up OpenAI prices).
- Calibration: `USD_PER_CREDIT` ($0.0187), `USD_PER_MTOK_FALLBACK` ($3.18) and `USD_RAISE_SHARE` (50%) are calibration values measured on 2026-11-20 from beta runs; update them in a release when model prices change.
- Diagram: label "Run cost = reported API price; unpriced calls estimated; $/credit measured", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D422 — Patch P26, dollar mode (J2_USD_BUDGET): credits follow the dollars left; only the dollar limit asks, and only the Requester answers
- Sources: the dollar-budget spec (FR-8 to FR-13, FR-20, NFR-2, NFR-3, NFR-6) and the owner's option A (credits stay internal for Priority, sized from the limit at a fixed rate, topped up by code); D421 (the rate and the estimate rule); D249 (a recorded patch may change prompts); D251 (every raise is the Requester's own).
- Decided: patch P26, on only when `J2_USD_BUDGET` is set (Sutra sets it from `EngineSettings.usd_budget`, default $20; `J2_USD_CAP` from `usd_cap`, unset means no cap). Each logged AI call adds its API price, or the D421 estimate, to the run's cost (part 1, T3). The starting credits are the limit at $0.0187 per credit (`J2_BUDGET` is ignored), and wherever J2 asked the Requester for credits (the cycle start, an approval over an envelope, a ruling over budget, a change that costs more than is left) code first tops the credits up from the dollars left, with a system line and no question. Between turns, a run at or over its dollar limit hands Identity the dollar question, written by code: "This run has used $X of $Y (API price). Add $Z? So far: $Y approved." (plus "N calls estimated: <provider> reports no price." when some were estimated); a limit whose work the dollars left cannot fund asks the same question. The suggested raise is the credits needed at the run's own dollars per credit, else half the limit (to the cent, to $1 above $10, at least $1, at most the cap's room); the option tail is "[adds $Z]". Code reads the answer's amount (`parse_usd`), never the model's credits; a typed amount over 5 times the limit is asked back once with that amount as the option, and a click on it or the same amount sent again (the card's button sends "Add $N and keep going") confirms it; "Add $0" is no raise and is asked again. Where the dollars left cannot fund an over-envelope approval, the dollar question is asked (and the envelope check runs again after a raise); a ruling over budget keeps its own two-option question, in dollars, whose first option raises the dollar limit; a document chain's shortfall (`_stage_gate`) tops up first, then asks in dollars. A stated need (`budget_needed`) that the dollars left cover is topped up and not asked for. Identity's limit reason is in dollars, never credits. Decide for me never answers the dollar question.
- Amended (review round 2, R2-1): one rate converts dollars and credits in both directions. `_usd_rate` is the run's measured dollars per credit (dollars used / credits spent) once it has spent credits and made `USD_RATE_MIN_CALLS` = 8 calls, else $0.0187; `_usd_credits` turns dollars into credits at it. The code top-up, the price of a raise (`_usd_raise`) and an approved raise (`_usd_add`) all use it. Before this, the raise was priced at the measured rate and funded at $0.0187, so in a run cheaper than $0.0187 per credit an approved raise bought too few credits (probe: +$1,018 funded 54,445 of 100,000; the workflow was held unfunded and the run ended "Done"), and in a dearer run it bought more than it was priced for. Each dollar question carries the rate it was priced at in its handoff (`usd_rate`, kept on a re-ask and on the over-5x confirm), and the approval adds exactly the credits the added dollars buy at that rate (dollars already spent past the limit buy none), so a call made between the question and the answer cannot move it. Why 8 calls: the opening (Identity's charter, Adaptation's plan, Priority's prices; 7 calls in the mock Brew Lab run) costs dollars before any credit is spent, so the ratio means nothing until the work's own calls start. Known limit: a workflow is charged in full when it is registered, before its calls run, so the measured rate drops right after a large registration and rises as the work runs; the dollar limit, checked between turns, stays the guard.
- Amended (review round 2, R2-1, the probe's second half): the envelope check tops credits up from the dollars left before Priority can hold an uncharged workflow. In the probe the raise was approved, code topped up (41 -> 54,477 credits, all the dollars bought at $0.0187), the envelope check ran again with approval already asked, and Priority held W-1 unfunded with no question, so the run ended "Done"; the top-up used to run only on the approval path, never at the check itself.
- Amended (review round 2, R2-2): the ruling's dollar question takes a typed amount like the limit and stage questions. It keeps its `budget` (option 1 ends "[adds $Z]", so Home's card reads the amount), the team page's approval card shows the same raise input above Approve / Not now (port edit E27e), and a typed amount is the raise only when the Requester chose to add (the model reads choice 1, or the answer is the card's own "Add $N and keep going"), so "No, don't add $3" raises nothing. "Add $0" and an amount that funds less than the ruling are asked again with the amount offered (never registered "in full" and then dropped by the validator), an amount over 5 times the limit is asked back once with it as option 1, and Approve, or the same amount sent again, confirms it; the stored question stays the first one.
- Why: one honest number (dollars) that the Requester sets and raises; credits keep Priority's pricing working without asking anybody about them.
- Rejected: a check inside each model call (a run cannot pause mid-turn; between turns is the existing pause point, so a turn in progress may pass the limit by its own cost, NFR-3); counting on the server from the usage ledger (two processes and a race); removing credits from the engine (Priority prices in them); letting Decide for me answer (D251); pricing a raise at the higher of the measured rate and $0.0187 (R2-1's first suggestion: the two directions would still use different rates, and a cheap run would be asked for more dollars than its work costs).
- Diagram: label "P26 dollar mode: credits follow dollars, only the dollar limit asks", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D423 — The Budget chip, Settings in dollars and the raise input on both cards: amounts are "API price" (the pill says it in its popover); no credits on the Requester's surfaces (amends D251, D252)
- Sources: Rubak's feedback (credits nobody understands, an unclear Budget field, a fixed +30 raise); the dollar-budget spec (FR-14 to FR-19); D421 and D422.
- Decided: one shared `budget_chip.js` (`BudgetChip`). The home box and a team page's start form show a pill beside Go, "$20 budget ▾", drawn once `GET /api/engine/settings` answers (`effective.usd_budget`), never with a placeholder. The pill shows only the amount, "$20 budget ▾": "API price" and "(team default)" are in its `aria-label`, and the popover's line "estimated at API prices, not billed" carries the API-price message (the owner kept this pill design over FR-14's visible "API price" and "(team default) · change", 2026-11-20, commit 57bf791e). It opens a popover below it (above, it covered the text box; review R1-8) that floats over the page (the row under it never moves) and stays inside the screen at phone width: "Run budget", one row of pills (half, the default with a "default" tag, double, Custom; picked, the Custom pill itself becomes a "$" box with the placeholder "amount", in its place in the row with the same height and radius, that applies as you type, $1 to $10,000, two decimals; its focus is the pill's border in the accent plus a ring that follows the radius, no rectangular outline, and a bad amount turns the border `--s-bad` and shows "Dollars from $1 to $10,000" under the row, tied to the box by `aria-describedby`; amended 2026-11-23) and "Asks before going over · estimated at API prices, not billed." A click outside or Esc closes it; focus goes in on open and back to the pill on close. A choice goes with the next request only as `usd_budget` (the home box's cs_org message, the team page's `POST /api/say`), which becomes the task setting `J2_USD_BUDGET`; nothing is sent for the default. Settings shows "Budget (API price, $)" and "Budget cap (API price, $)" (its hint says a cap below the budget leaves no room to raise it; a cap below a run's budget stays valid, as a request's own chip may pass the team cap) and hides `budget`, `budget_cap` and `limit_add` (still valid when stored). The welcome says "inside a budget of $20 (API price) for each run". The `budget` field of D251 and D252 is gone from both pages; the routes still accept it. Both raise cards (the team page's question card, port edit E27, and Home's Needs you card) show, for a dollar question whose continue option adds dollars, an input with the suggested amount filled in, "Add and keep going" and "API price, estimated"; they send "Add $N and keep going", which the engine reads (D422), and a bad amount marks the box and sends nothing. Home's Needs you card is not repainted while its raise box has the focus and an amount other than the suggested one (like a half-typed answer). The option tail "[adds $Z]" is stripped like the credits one.
- Why: one number the owner understands, set where the request starts and raised by any amount, not a fixed +30; the credits stay the engine's own bookkeeping.
- Rejected: a placeholder value before the settings answer (it would show a figure that may not be the team's); a separate chip per page (one module, the same words everywhere); removing the credit settings (old engine.json files and queued tasks must still validate, NFR-5).
- Diagram: label "Budget chip in dollars (API price); Settings in dollars; raise input on both cards", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D424 — The dollar budget and Stop on a project (amends D420, D422, D423 for SUTRA_NODES_V1)
- Sources: the merge of `origin/main` (0.7.0, multi-agent projects) into the dollar-budget branch; D320 (a step's slice is final, no raise), D381 and D388 (a project's dollar limit), D409 and D418 (the per-call dollar cap), D401 (a step's engine question on the project page, answered by option number), D402 (the open-items sentence), D403 (a stopped project's steps); `orchestrator/tasks.py` (`_stop`, `stop_node`), `engine/host/loop.py`.
- Decided: (a) A project's step runs without P26: the host drops `J2_USD_BUDGET` for a run with a node block, so its dollars are the project's (D381, D418, enforced by the meter between calls) and its credits its slice (D320). P26 would otherwise top credits up past `J2_BUDGET_CAP` and ask a dollar raise the project page cannot take as a typed amount. (b) The dollars the owner picked on Home's Budget chip for a request that becomes a project are the project's own `budget_usd` (no `usd_from_month`), so it stops with "the project spent more than the dollars you set"; the month still stops it on every tick (D381). With no pick, the project gets the month's remainder as before. (c) Home's Stop on the work it just started stops the whole project (`POST /api/projects/{id}/stop`) when the card is a step of one, asking "Stop this project? What's built so far is kept."; the team page's Stop stops only that team's step, through the same `_stop` path main's `stop_node` uses ("stopped by you", by the owner), so the failure policy then applies. The project page keeps its own Stop. The project page's spend line names the project's own dollars: "about $0.86 of $2.00". (d) D401 shows a step's engine question with its options as buttons; without P26 on a step the question is P25's credits question within the slice, so the dollar amount box is not on the project page. (e) D402 is unchanged: no dollar words reach a step's done sentence.
- Why: two budgets on one step (P26's dollars and the project's) disagreed, and a home Stop that stopped only the first step of a project left the rest running.
- Rejected: P26 on project steps with the cap respected (two dollar meters on one step, and a raise question D320 rules out); an amount box on the project page (D320: a step's budget is final); mapping the team default ($20) to a project's dollars (it would stop projects that run today on the month's remainder).
- Diagram: label "projects: P26 off per step; chip $ = project's own; Home Stop stops the project", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D425 — A working folder says where a team works; it no longer selects project work (amends D129, D165, D253)
- Sources: the Named Projects PRD (23 Nov 2026, Q7, R1.7); the 19 Nov run where a folder made a whole request one-team project work with no charters; `engine/capabilities/select.py` (rule 4 `folder`), `connectors/workdir.py`, `connectors/router.py`, `engine/host/loop.py`.
- Decided: (a) The output rule `folder` is gone: with the owner's choice on `auto`, a brief the project words and a follow-up do not claim goes to J2's planner (`None`, rule `planner`) whether a folder is set or not, so a team with a folder still gets stages and charters. The owner's explicit "Project work" pick still selects it. (b) A team has a working folder of its own, `departments/<id>/workdir.json` (the body and file mode of the app-wide `connectors/workdir.json`); its chats and runs use it, and a team without a usable one falls back to the app-wide folder (`WorkFolder.team_grant`). A team's direct chat tags its turns with the team id so the router picks its folder.
- Why: one rule turned every run of a team with a folder into single-team project work, which lost the planner's stages and charters (the 19 Nov run); and one app-wide folder cannot hold several products at once.
- Rejected: keeping the rule and adding a per-team folder (Friday's result again, for every project); a folder per run (the owner wants one project that grows).
- Diagram: label "folder = where a team works, not project work; one folder per team", pill on Department records.

## D426 — The owner makes, renames and deletes projects without a model call (amends DL5)
- Sources: the Named Projects PRD (Q1, Q2, Q8, Q11, R1.1 to R1.5, R1.13); `orchestrator/team_admin.py`, `orchestrator/api/teams.py`, `orchestrator/schema.py` (v9), `orchestrator/departments.py`, `ui/boot.py`, `oslayer.documents_dir`.
- Decided: (a) A project is a team: `POST /api/teams` `{name, folder?, access?}` makes it through `ChartStore.apply` as the owner (`actor="user"`, no model call, no credits): its five function agents, its home folder and page, and its project folder: the chosen one (checked like the app-wide folder) or `Documents/Sutra Pro/<name>` (the system's Documents folder, `SUTRA_DOCUMENTS_DIR` overrides it), with a name every system accepts and never one that already holds files ("Pulse 2"). A name is 1 to 80 characters after whitespace is collapsed; one a visible project has (case and spaces ignored) is the 409 `name_taken` "A project called Pulse already exists". Owner-typed names skip the similar-name `reuse_candidate` rule (it judges the chat agent); the exact sibling clash still holds. (b) `PATCH /api/teams/{id}` renames, the id kept. (c) `DELETE /api/teams/{id}` hides the team: refused while it has an unfinished task ("Pulse is working. Stop it first."), else renamed "Pulse (deleted 23 Nov)" (then " 2" on a clash) and `departments.hidden_at` set. A hidden team leaves the sidebar, `listing()`, Agent Org and the org chat (its digest, candidates, reuse and assignments: "That project was deleted"); its row, id, runs and files stay, its page opens read-only with "This project was deleted. Its runs and files stay on disk.", and the name is free again. (d) `GET`/`PUT /api/teams/last` keeps the project last picked on Attention on the server (`ui/last_project.json`): a browser's storage belongs to one port.
- Why: the owner could not start, name or remove a project; every prompt went to Product Engineering and its work sat in Sutra's own data.
- Rejected: a new layer that groups runs across teams (teams already have agents, runs and a page; the unfinished multi-agent projects show the cost); deleting files or rows on delete (cannot be undone; DL5 relies on ids never being reused); naming a project from the prompt's first words.
- Rollback: the schema goes to v9 (`departments.hidden_at`), and an older app refuses a newer database (`db.migrate`). A rollback is therefore a revert that keeps `DDL_V9_HIDDEN_TEAMS` (the column is harmless to code that does not read it), never a plain revert of the merge. Check `schema.MIGRATIONS` on `origin/main` right before landing: another v9 there means renumbering this one.
- Diagram: label "projects = teams: make, rename, hide by code; folder per project", pill on One global org chart.

## D427 — The org chat asks the owner to name a project before it adds one (amends D68)
- Sources: the Named Projects PRD (Q13, R1.11); `orchestrator/chart_rules.py` (`classify`), `orchestrator/chart.py` (`decide(names=)`), `orchestrator/agent/runner.py`, `orchestrator/api/agent.py`, `ui/static/js/front_door.js`, `chat_view.js`, `home.js`, `create_project.js`.
- Decided: (a) A chart change of the chat agent that adds a department waits for the owner (`classify` is `approve`), like a merge or a split; a reuse, a division, a move, a rename and every change the owner made by hand stay automatic. (b) Its approval card says "I'd start a new project for this: <name>. Name it to create it, or say no." and carries `adds` (`{ref, name}`, also on the needs-you row with its `change_id`). The card's "Name it…" opens the Create project box with the suggested name; after a "Let Sutra choose" send on Attention the box opens by itself, once per change. (c) The box's yes is `POST /api/chart/changes/{id}/decision` `{approve: true, names: {ref: name}, folder?, access?}`: the names and folder are checked first (422, 409 `name_taken`, the change stays pending), the stored change is applied under the owner's names, and the new project gets its folder. A plain "yes" in the chat keeps the suggested name; "no" leaves the chart as it was. Stage C (assignments) runs after the yes.
- Why: no project may exist without a name the owner confirmed.
- Rejected: the org chat naming projects from the prompt's first words; asking only on Attention (every add of the chat agent is a project).
- Diagram: label "a team the org chat would add waits for the owner's name", pill on Chat agent (orchestrator).

## D428 — A finished run saves its results into its project's folder, guarded by a manifest
- Sources: the Named Projects PRD (Q9, R1.12, R1.14); `engine/host/export.py`, `engine/host/finish.py` (`STEPS`).
- Decided: (a) A run that ended `done` or `done_with_open_items` copies its deliverables (the deliverable rows, every file of a folder output, the run's own result such as `project.md`, the `.docx` beside a document) into its team's OWN folder, never the app-wide fallback, under the same relative paths; Sutra's records stay in the run (`*.json`, `*.jsonl`, `site/`, `out/`, dotfiles). A Read only folder gets nothing; a stopped or crashed run writes nothing. (b) `<folder>/.sutra/manifest.json` keeps the sha256 of what Sutra last wrote. A target Sutra never wrote, or one changed since, is not touched and the chat names it ("Not updated: prd.md was changed outside Sutra"); a file that cannot be written is named and the rest still go; a folder that is gone is reported and not made again. Each file goes through a temp file and an atomic replace. (c) The chat says "Saved to <folder>: 12 files (3 new, 9 updated)."; `<run>/export.json` records what happened.
- Why: the work sat in `.sutra-pro/departments/.../runs/<run>/` next to about 40 record files, where the owner does not look.
- Rejected: building inside the owner's folder while the run works (a failed run would leave it half-done); a dated subfolder per run (the owner wants one project that grows; history stays in Sutra).
- Diagram: label "a run's results go to its project folder; manifest guards the owner's files", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D429 — A follow-up continues any finished run and edits the project folder in place
- Sources: the Named Projects PRD (Phase 2, R2.1 to R2.7, CUJs 6 and 7); `engine/rehydrate.py` (`check_source`, `_continuable`, `_output_files`), `engine/host/loop.py` (`_seed`, `_start_from_folder`), `engine/host/export.py` (`import_from_folder`, `lines`).
- Decided: (a) A follow-up continues the team's earlier run when it finished with a charter and workflows, whatever its output: a page or a document chain as before, and now a single-file capability output (project work) or a folder output (an app, its whole folder at any depth). A plain document run that lost its chain, or a run with no type, is still refused and starts fresh with the old files as reference, saying why (R2.7). (b) Before the follow-up works, the files the project folder's manifest lists (only those: R2.4) are copied from the folder into the new run, so a file the owner edited by hand is what the team starts from; the chat says "Starting from your edits in <folder>: <files>". With edit access the manifest then takes those bytes as Sutra's, so the follow-up's change is written back over the edit it started from. (c) At the end only files whose bytes changed are written back, new files are added, nothing is deleted from the folder, and the chat says "Changed in <project>: <files>" (R2.6). Every earlier version stays in Sutra's run history: an earlier run's folder is never written again (R2.5).
- Why: "change the digest time to 08:30" must give the same app with one file changed, not a new build.
- Rejected: building inside the owner's folder while the run works (the Phase 1 decision, D428); reading every file of the folder (files Sutra never wrote are never read as its work).
- Diagram: label "follow-up continues any finished run; starts from the folder, writes back changes", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D430 — The record models take what J2 really writes (SUT-11, records part)
- Sources: every `record_rejected` line in `run.stderr.log` of the beta runs (19 of 21 runs), and the same run files checked again in full with `check_record` (the log names three violations per file and stops a workflows.json at its first bad row); J2 `j2.py` (`_usd_metrics`, the raise records, the audit entry, the real run receipt, the superseded row, `mock_form_folder_charter`), `j2_realization.py` (the second look near the gate, `deferred` as `{item: int, until}`), `artifacts/types.json` (each form's `check_types`).
- Decided: the library accepts each shape J2 wrote. realization: a score's `second_look` (`[first, second]`), `plan.deferred.item` is a done-when item number. workflows: status `superseded` and `superseded_by`; dependency names up to 200 characters (55 seen); `completion_check` up to 4,000 (2,076 seen); a check takes up to 40 targets (13 seen) and an atom up to 40 checks (15 seen); the agent form's `test-passed` and `inside-folder` are check names (28 in all; `charter-files-present` and `charter-fields-present` are condition checks, and `ConditionResult.check` is already free text). audits: `version` 0 (a folder output has no site), `after_real_run`, and on an entry that has `after_real_run` a false `all_pass` with every row passing (the real run's receipt turns rows to pass and J2 leaves `all_pass` as it was). goal: a stored charter may have no done-when item when the Requester dropped them (every one of 9 in the run seen); a document charter's `files`, `test`, `may_touch`, `never_do` (the folder form). summary.json: a raise's `code`, `budget.usd` (P26), `output_type` null. atoms.json: `how.date_control`, `how.date_case`. Fixtures: the refused files themselves in `tests/library/fixtures/records/`, home paths written as `~`, a person's name and the street addresses replaced by placeholders. Where the values come from: the library still imports nothing from the engine. J2's check names have one definition there (`artifacts/types.json`, each form's `check_types`, plus `lld-slice` and `deed-send`, which code writes and `j2.py` names only in its branches), and `test_lib_engine_parity.py` fails when the library's list drifts from it. The workflow statuses (literals across `j2.py`) and the atom `how` keys (only in the `ATOM_FMT` prompt) have no single definition in the engine, so the library lists them itself.
- Why: the check exists to catch drift between J2 and the library; a rejection of what J2 writes on purpose is noise that hides the real drift.
- Rejected: dropping the bounds (they still catch a runaway reply); accepting an empty charter always (the rule still holds when nothing was dropped); an exception for the brief factor's quote rule (a brief factor with no quote, in a plan round J2 sent back, stays rejected: Adaptation's reply check needs the rule and J2's own `plan_faults` names it); the library reading `types.json` through `j2_types` (it would import the engine and read a file; the parity test binds the two instead).
- Follow-ups: the engine: after a real run, `j2.py` turns result rows to pass and leaves `all_pass` as it was; recompute it there, then drop the exception in `ResultEvaluation`. The records: `calls.jsonl` `usd_counted`, `receipts.jsonl` `target` null and `map.json` duplicate node ids differ from the models but are never gated (J2 writes them outside `save()`); model them when they are.
- Diagram: label "records: models take what J2 writes (second look, superseded, agent checks, usd)", pill on Department records.

## D431 — The same failure twice in a row, across workflows, stops a run; nothing built is never shipped or done (patches P28, P29; amends P22)
- Sources: beta runs j2-20261123-103238-3429 (Pulse; its folder was not on the developer's Mac, so its replay is built from the owner's description) and j2-20261118-071410-17e1 (Bean There): six builds (W-1, W-6, W-7, W-11, W-12, W-13) failed with the same outside write ("changed outside its folder: agent_live.json, events.jsonl, snapshot.json") and spent 100 of 100 credits; then, with nothing built, the second limit question offered "Leave out" of all four done-when items, it was picked, and Identity tried to declare done. The run's own reviewer: "That would have cost about 12 credits instead of 100." Spec SUT-10. Code at 06ea534c: "same failure twice" compared audits only (`_identity_audit`), never two builds; P22's repeat keyed on the workflow's name; the "Leave out" option and `workable()` had no "nothing built" rule; Decide for me answered a second limit question with its last option (`_auto_choice`).
- Decided: (a) P28: every build failure records a code-made `failure` on its workflow (`outside_write` with every outside file, now kept on `BuilderToolFault.files`; `real_path`; `unusable_reply`; `no_artifact`; `no_build`), and `build_failure(w)` gives a finished build's signature, or `checks` with the sorted (type, evidence) of its failed receipts; never a workflow's name or a check's words. `_turn_workflow_agent` wraps the build (`_build_workflow`) and `_repeat_build_stop` compares each finished build with the previous one: the same signature twice in a row, from two workflows, hands Identity a limit question ("The same failure came back twice in a row: W-1 and W-6 both failed (...)"), resuming Adaptation's atom_failed when the build had one, else Coordination's queue when work waits (review R1-5), as P22's repeat does. A passing build or a new stage clears the memory; the Requester's answer does not (OQ-1), so after "keep going" the next identical failure asks again. While the two workflows are a repeated step, a proposal that redoes them (names one in revises or replaces, or covers one of their done-when items; a shared output file such as `index.html`, which nearly every page workflow writes, does not count) must carry a non-empty `cause_change` or the validator rejects it; Adaptation's and Priority's prompts gain a line only then, and whether the cause is new is Priority's REFUSE, never code's (OQ-3). (b) P22 amended: every rejection carries a key set by code where it is made (`_reject(name, reason, key)`: the rule that fired and code-made ids only, such as the done-when items of a real-run clash or the required fields; never a workflow's name, a section, dependency or part an agent wrote, or the judge's words), and the repeat compares keys, whatever any workflow is called (OQ-2, review R1-2). In the validator's rule chain the key comes from `key_rules`: one entry per rule test of the reason chain, the same test in the same order, each with its own rule name; a reason no entry names gets its own `("unkeyed",)` bucket, never another rule's. A test reads j2.py's code (every `_reject` call passes a key; the key list follows the reason chain test by test; the rule names are distinct), so a rule added without a key fails it. The chain itself was not restructured into (reason, key) pairs: that would split P22's and P24's patch text. The worded reason stays for display; `summary.json`'s `validator_rejections` rows gain `key`. (c) P29: `workable()` says "nothing has been built yet" for an audit at version 0, so Identity's done goes back to Adaptation; with nothing built the limit question offers no "Leave out" that leaves only the gate item to build (a partial one stays, OQ-4; "Stop here" fills in), and a typed answer that leaves everything out is applied as a stop ("Stopped on the Requester's decision (...): nothing was built."). With nothing built, a lone "Stop here" (when "keep going" can't be offered again) is asked, never applied by code (review R1-3); with a site, a lone "Finish as it is" is still applied as before. (d) P29: Decide for me never answers a limit question, the same-failure one among them: `AUTO_ASK` holds `limit`, so it waits and `auto_waits` says so, as for P26's dollar question. Its suggestion on the Simple view is unchanged (SUT-15). (e) P29, follow-up from beta run j2-20261123-103238-3429 (Pulse, 23 Nov): a necessary condition the charter decides, unmet for the same code-made reason (its id and evidence, never its wording) in two audits in a row, stops the cycle with the limit question, whatever else moved between the audits. Its reason reads exactly: "This condition comes from the charter, and building can't change it. The charter needs fixing." A condition is the charter's only when code marks it so where it checks it (`from_charter`, set by `_folder_condition` when the charter lists no files or no test); every other condition, which a build can meet (a website's in-page links, N4), goes back to Adaptation as before (review R1-1). In that run the charter listed no files and no test, so C1 ("the charter lists no files") and C2 ("the test '' is not a file in the folder") failed in every audit while the done-when results moved, so the whole-audit check (`failure_signature`) never matched. The guard sits in `_identity_audit`'s existing same-failure check; the Requester's answer resets it as it does for audits (`fail_floor`).
- Why: 100 credits went on one environment fault that two builds had already shown; with the guard the replay stops after W-6 (12 credits). A run that built nothing must end "Stopped", never "Done", and the Requester, not Decide for me, decides when a run is stuck.
- Rejected: a guard at each of the six build exits (six copies of one rule); a guard at the dispatcher (inside P8's text) or in Coordination's turn (misses atom-failed builds, which go to Adaptation); a signature read from chat or event text (it is cut at five files and carries numbers); folding builds into the audit check (an audit comes after the money is spent); dropping "Leave out" altogether with nothing built (loses a real scope cut); rewriting a "Done" outcome in the summary (hides the bug); clearing the build-failure memory on "keep going" (it changes nothing about the cause); a per-caller Decide-for-me exception (one guard in `AUTO_ASK` covers every limit question, the turn and cycle caps and project steps included: the owner's choice, review R1-7); a repeat on any condition that repeats (it told the Requester a buildable condition could not be built, R1-1); comparing worded rejection reasons (other workflows' names entered through them, R1-2). Accepted as designed: some signature details carry non-code text (a section name in a parse error, a CLI error, a check's target), which only makes failures look different (R1-4); a fresh proposal that names neither failed workflow nor covers their items needs no `cause_change` (R1-6). Prompt goldens did not change: the new prompt lines appear only after a repeat. The legacy flow's page-run case now expects "Stop here" with nothing built, and Decide for me's second-limit case expects a wait (its suggestion is still asserted); EXPECTED.json records the new hash.
- Diagram: label "same build failure twice stops (P28); no ship/done with nothing built (P29)", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.

## D432 — Sutra Pro Beta on Windows: the same packaged beta, built from a checkout that follows main (amends D242)
- Sources: owner, 2026-11-24: "create a sutra beta app for me on Windows where I can keep taking the pull from the main so I need not wait for the releases";
  of two ways (the packaged app, a few minutes per update, or the server run from the checkout, seconds per update) the owner chose the packaged app. D242
  (the beta channel), D243 (the Windows icon), D120 (the packaged-app proof), `docs/design/windows-beta/design.md`.
- Decided: the beta channel builds for `win-x64` (the builder, the packager and the proof allow it; the proof starts the exe that `build-info.json` names). On
  Windows `install_app.py beta` installs the folder `Sutra Pro Beta` (default `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs`, or `--install-dir`), unsigned, with a Start-menu entry
  and its own icon (the BETA band over the Windows picture); it reads processes from PowerShell, closes a running beta only when it is idle and only through its
  window (`taskkill /PID`, no `/F`), copies the folder in whole, starts its exe and waits for `/api/health`. `--pull` fetches and fast-forwards the checkout to
  its upstream first and refuses a tree with changes. The owner keeps one checkout that only follows `origin/main` for it. `release` stays a Mac command.
- Why: the owner works on Windows and wants what is on `main` without a release; the packaged app is what ships, so a fault of the packaging (as the one that
  stopped 0.8.0's desktop builds) shows in the beta first. The same identity as the Mac beta keeps the stable app, its folder and `~/.sutra-pro` untouched.
- Rejected: running the server from the checkout in a plain window (fast, but packaging faults go unseen); an installer or signing for the beta (nothing to
  distribute: it is built where it runs); a beta that updates itself from `main` (a broken `main` would land without the owner asking); `taskkill /F` to replace a
  running beta (it would end a run; the owner closes it or waits).
- Diagram: label "beta on Windows too, built from a checkout of main", pill on Desktop app · Electron window.

## D433 — A real run is offered only after a dry run that exits 0, its approval is never lost, and a built script's packages get their own environment (patch P30)
- Sources: owner, 2026-11-24, on the Pulse runs that never read mail: Google access was fine (the same key read 5 of 6 mailboxes when the built code was run
  by hand); run j2-20261124-062837-01a2, `receipts.jsonl` lines 90 and 91: `approved: false, ran: false, exit: null` (the folder was not on the developer's Mac,
  so the tests are built from the owner's description). Code at 541e572c: `_ask_real_run` posted the approval card whatever the dry run's exit code;
  `_identity_real_run` took any reply but the exact `RUN <code>` as a refusal, wrote that receipt and never asked again; `_goal_done` then posted "ready"; a
  receipt kept 1,500 mixed characters of output; the test rerun, the dry run and the real run used the system `python3`, where a built script's packages are
  not installed. J2's branch `fix-real-run-approval` (the same fix in `prototypes/j2`, with `j2_runenv.py` and `real_run_fix_test.py`).
- Decided: P30, five rules. (1) Only a dry run that exits 0 is offered. Any other writes a "not offered" receipt and goes back to the builder with the stderr on
  the workflow's remaining attempts (`MAX_CHECK_ATTEMPTS`); with none left the real run is closed as not run. (2) A reply is `RUN <code>` (any case, spaces
  trimmed, never without the code), a decline, or neither; neither keeps the question open with "Not run. Reply exactly RUN <code>, or decline." (3) A run
  never ends "Done:" or ready while a planned real run is pending, unapproved, declined or failed: the outcome is "Done with open items: The live step is not
  done. ..." with why, per workflow. (4) Every real-run receipt, and the dry run inside it, keeps the exit code and the last 2,000 characters of stdout and of
  stderr. (5) A build with a `requirements.txt` gets a virtual environment, made by code (`engine/runenv.py`, Sutra's copy of J2's module): the interpreter is
  `J2_RUN_PYTHON`, else the first python3.13 to 3.10 on the PATH, else the Python the engine runs on (in the desktop app, the one inside the app); none means
  the real run is not offered, with the reason. Environments are kept by the hash of `requirements.txt` in `<data folder>/envs` (`J2_DATA_DIR`, set by
  `DataPaths.j2_env`). Only pinned PyPI packages (`name==version`) are installed, with `pip install --isolated --index-url https://pypi.org/simple`; with the
  sandbox on, the network is open to pypi.org and files.pythonhosted.org for the install step alone. Nothing is installed without the Requester's `RUN <code>`:
  the run pauses before the build's checks with the packages and versions listed, and the real-run card lists them again. The test rerun, the dry run and the
  real run use that environment's python and are not run without it; before the offer code checks that the script's top-level imports resolve there, and a
  missing module goes back to the builder. Receipts (`real_run`, and the new `env_install`, open JSON in the records catalogue) record the interpreter's path
  and version, the requirements hash and the installed versions. (6, added the same day on the owner's word: with the sandbox on, the dry run and the real
  run had no network, so a real run that must call a service, Gmail for one, could never pass its dry run.) A real-run plan names the `hosts` its command
  must reach, as data: plain host names only, checked by `real_run_problems`. With the sandbox on they are asked for before the dry run (`RUN <code>`), since
  nothing is let through without approval; then they are the only hosts that dry run and real run can reach, and a plan that names none has no network. The
  approval card lists them with or without a sandbox, and every real-run receipt and its dry run record `network: {hosts, enforced}`. The planning rules of a
  form that can plan a real run gain one paragraph about the field (`REAL_RUN_HOSTS_RULE`): P30's one prompt change (D249).
- Why: the run that prompted this offered a live step that had already failed on copies, lost the owner's approval to a reply that was not letter-exact, and
  ended ready with no mail read. Each rule closes one of those steps, and the receipts now say what happened without a rerun.
- Rejected: one approval for the packages and the live run together (the packages must be installed before the test rerun and the dry run can run, and only a
  dry run that exits 0 may be offered, so a new environment is asked for first, once per `requirements.txt`; a cached one needs no question); installing
  before any approval (nothing is installed without it); taking "yes" or "run" without the code as approval (a guess at consent to change the owner's files);
  ending the run when a reply is not exact (how the approval was lost); the system `python3` with `pip install --user` (it changes the owner's own Python);
  unpinned packages (the card could not say what would be installed); an environment per run (minutes of install each run, and Pulse runs daily); a new pause
  kind for the install question (the run-message schema and the page know `real_run`; the handoff says `env_install`); vendoring `j2_runenv.py` (J2's main is
  ahead of the pinned commit, so the file is not in the extraction: it is Sutra's own module until a sync moves the pin); opening the whole network to an
  approved real run (the approval would cover hosts nobody saw); a dry run without network followed by a real run with it (the dry run of a script that
  reads mail would always fail, and never be offered); allowing the hosts for the dry run before any approval (nothing is allowed without it); wildcards or
  addresses as hosts (the card could not say what is reached); editing the vendored `real_run.md` (the artifacts tree is a byte copy: the paragraph is in
  `j2.py`).
- Diagram: label "real run: only after a dry run that exits 0; approval never lost; packages in their own environment (P30)", pill on band:DEPARTMENT RUNTIME.
